

===== PREFACE =====
This update to the County Code of Utah County, UT began in-­house in 2020 with the assistance of Municipal Code Corporation. This County Code shall be cited as the Utah County Code or “UCC” as an acronym.

UCC references found within the code maintain a structure by subject matter using a decimal numbering system which identifies the chapter and section ( for example: 1.01.010 ). This complete set of numbers is designed to aid in searching and/or referencing the Municipal Code, and to assist in subsequent codification as new ordinances are added to the Municipal Code. The first number in the sequence ( 1 .01.010) designates the TITLE level
 The second series of numbers (1. 01 .010) designates the Chapter level
 The last series (1.01. 010 ) designates the Section level
 If a fourth series exists comprising letters or numbers beyond the section level, it designates a Subsection level. 
 The legislation herein may be arranged as an alphanumeric ordered list A, 1, a, (1), (A), (a) to show hierarchal relationships. The legislation may also be arranged as an unordered list (using bullets, discs, squares, etc.) to show items without hierarchal relationships. These list structures assist in subsequent codification as new legislation is added. To forego the naming of each list item and to more granularly reference legislation that employs alphanumeric characters, use “Paragraph” followed by the desired alphanumeric reference(s), comma separated. For example, “Paragraph B,7,d”, specifically references item “d”, of item “7”, of item “B”—whereas “Paragraph B” refers more generally to any or all of list item B’s descendants.

References herein to “Utah Code” are references to the " Utah State Legislature " and to Utah Code 1953, as amended. Vacant titles, chapters, or sections may be designed for future use and may be marked “Reserved” to ease internal expansion. The legislative history identifies the specific legal sources of a section as may be provided in footnotes.

This County Code is supplemented from time­ to ­time with amendments and additions made by Utah County, Utah. The specific legal sources that comprise this Municipal Code have been adopted during the codification process from the original formatting of the official hard copy. In the event of discrepancies between the online Municipal Code and the official hard copy, the official hard copy governs. Municipal Code Corporation, provides a searchable database of the Municipal Code for easy reference and convenience.

NOTICE: THE COUNTY CODE AVAILABLE ONLINE MAY NOT REFLECT ALL OR THE MOST CURRENT VERSION OF LEGISLATION ADOPTED BY THE COUNTY BOARD THAT HAS YET TO BE UPDATED ONLINE. IN THE EVENT OF CONFLICT BETWEEN THE COUNTY CODE AND A WRITTEN ORDINANCE, THE ORDINANCE TYPICALLY GOVERNS. ALSO, THE COUNTY CODE MAY NOT REFLECT RULES OR OTHER REGULATIONS PROMULGATED UNDER THE AUTHORITY OF THE COUNTY CODE, INCLUDING TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS. FOR MORE INFORMATION CONTACT THE COUNTY CLERK. 1 GENERAL PROVISIONS 1.01 Code Adoption 1.02 Code Construction And General Provisions 1.03 Official And Corporate Provisions 1.04 General Penalties 1.05 Adoption Of State Codes


===== 1.01 Code Adoption =====
1.01.010 Adoption, Designation, And Citation 1.01.020 Effect Of Repealing Existing General Ordinances


===== 1.01.010 Adoption, Designation, And Citation =====
The title, chapters, sections, and subsections embraced in this Code of Ordinances shall constitute and may be cited as the "Utah County Code." State law reference - Adoption of general revision of county ordinances, Utah Code § 17-64-502.


===== 1.01.020 Effect Of Repealing Existing General Ordinances =====
The repeal of the general ordinances as provided in Utah County Code, 2022 shall not affect: any right which accrued; any duty imposed; any fee or penalty due, incurred, or unpaid prior to the effective date of the of the Utah County Code, 2022; any prosecution for ordinance violations committed prior to the effective date of this ordinance and the Utah County Code; or any action or proceeding commenced administratively or judicially under or by virtue of any of the general ordinances so repealed. The repeal of any general ordinance or provision thereof by this ordinance shall not repeal the repealing clause of any such general ordinance nor revive any general ordinance or provision thereof which had been repealed or superseded by such general ordinance.


===== 1.02 Code Construction And General Provisions =====
1.02.010 Definitions 1.02.020 Rules Of Construction 1.02.030 Interpretation Of Language 1.02.040 Parenthetical And Reference Matter 1.02.050 Catchlines Of Section 1.02.060 Correction Of Technical Errors 1.02.070 Altering Code


===== 1.02.010 Definitions =====
In this Code and of all ordinances of this County, the following definitions and rules of construction shall be observed, unless such construction or definition would be inconsistent with the manifest intent of the Commission, would be repugnant to the context of the provisions, the context clearly requires otherwise, or the term is defined otherwise for the purpose of a specific chapter. Any word or phrase not specifically defined in this subsection shall have the definition set out in state law, in the common law, or according to ordinary English usage. "Board of health" means Board of Health of the County Health Department. "Business" means any activity, operation, enterprise or calling referred to in this Code for which a license is required. "Code" means this Code of Ordinances as designated and cited in UCC 1.01.010. "County" means the political subdivision of the State of Utah known as Utah County and may also be construed to mean the portions of Utah County outside the limits of the incorporated cities or towns therein. "County Commission" means the Board of County Commissioners, being the governing body of the County, unless defined otherwise for the purposes of a specific chapter. "County officers": the terms "County Attorney," "County Auditor," "County Sheriff," "Sheriff," "County Surveyor," "County Treasurer," etc., shall mean and refer to those respective officers of the County, as well as their duly authorized deputies and/or assistants. "Easement" means a right, liberty, privilege or advantage without profit which the owner of one parcel of land may have in the lands of another. A right in the owner of one parcel of land, by reason of such ownership, to use the land of another for a special purpose not inconsistent with a general property right in the owner. "Highway or road" includes the words and phrases "public bridges," "county ways," "county roads," "common roads," "state roads," as well as sidewalks or crosswalks. "In the county" or "in this county" means and includes any territory within the boundaries of this County and any other territory over which regulatory power has been conferred on the county by general or special act, except as otherwise specified. "Joint authority" means words purporting to give authority to three (3) or more officers or other persons shall be construed as giving such authority to a majority of such officers or other persons, unless it is otherwise specifically declared. "Knowingly" imports only a knowledge that the facts exist which bring the act or omission within the provisions of this Code or ordinances of the County. It does not require any knowledge of the unlawfulness of such act or omission. "Law" means any statute, ordinance or regulation promulgated by the United States, the State, the County or any agency thereof, as well as the rules and regulations of other bodies politic that may be appropriate. "Maliciously" or "malice" means a wish to vex, annoy or injure another person, or an intent to do a wrongful act, established either by proof or by presumption of law. "Month" means a calendar month. "Oath" includes an affirmation in all cases in which, by law, an affirmation may be substituted for an oath, and in such cases the words "swear" and "sworn" shall be equivalent to the words "affirm" and "affirmed." "Owner" includes any part owner, joint owner, tenant in common, tenant in partnership, joint tenant, tenant or lessee by the entirety of the whole or of any part of a building or land. "Peace officer" means a sheriff of a county, a county deputy sheriff, or a constable. "Person" includes and applies to a firm, partnership, association, corporation, organization, club, society, group acting as a unit, or body politic and corporate, as well as to an individual. "Personal property" means both tangible and intangible property including, but not limited to, money, goods, and evidences of debt but does not include ownership in real property. "Preceding" and "following" mean the next before and the next after, respectively. "Property" means and includes real, personal, and mixed property. "Public place" includes any public way, park, cemetery, school yard or open space adjacent thereto; any public lake or stream; and any place of business open to the use of the public in general, open to public view or to which the public has access. "Public way" means any street, alley, boulevard, parkway, highway, sidewalk or other public thoroughfare. "Real property" shall include lands, tenements, and hereditaments. "Reasonable time." In all cases where any section of this Code or County ordinance shall require any act to be done in a reasonable time or reasonable notice to be given, such reasonable time or notice shall be deemed to mean such time only as may be necessary for the prompt performance of such duty, or compliance with such notice. "Residence" means the place adopted by a person as his or her place of habitation, and to which, whenever he or she is absent, he or she has the intention of returning. When a person eats at one place and sleeps at another, the place where such person sleeps shall be deemed to be his or her residence. "Roadway" means that portion of a street improved, designed or ordinarily used for vehicular traffic. "Sidewalk" means any portion of the street between the curb, or the lateral line of the roadway, and the adjacent property line intended for the use of pedestrians. "Signature" or "subscription" includes a mark when the person cannot write, when the person's name is written near such mark and is witnessed by another person who writes his or her own name as witness. "State" or "this state" means the State of Utah. "Street" means and includes public streets, avenues, boulevards, highways, roads, alleys, lanes, viaducts, bridges, public ways and approaches thereto and other public thoroughfares in the city devoted to public use. "Tenant" or "occupant," applied to a building or land, mean and include any person who occupies the whole or any part of such building or land, either alone or with others. "Willfully," when applied to the intent with which an act is done or omitted, implies simply a purpose or willingness to commit the act or make the omission referred to. It does not require any intent to violate law, or to injure another, or to acquire any advantage. "Written" or "writing" mean or include typewritten, printed on paper, expressed electronically, and any other mode of representing words and letters. "Year" means a calendar year.


===== 1.02.020 Rules Of Construction =====
Whenever a provision appears in this Code requiring an officer of the County to do some act or make certain inspections, it is to be construed to authorize such officer to designate, delegate, and authorize subordinates to perform the required act or make the required inspection unless the terms of the provision or section expressly designates otherwise. Any word importing the singular number shall include the plural, and any word importing the plural number shall include the singular. Words used in the present tense include the past and future tenses and vice versa, unless manifestly inapplicable. Words importing single gender shall extend and apply to all genders and to firms, partnerships, associations, and corporations. The term "and" may be read "or" and the term "or" may be read "and" if the context requires it. The term "may" is permissive, the terms "must" and "shall" are each mandatory. Whenever certain hours are named in this Code, they shall mean standard time or daylight-saving time as may be in current use in the County.


===== 1.02.030 Interpretation Of Language =====
All words and phrases shall be construed and understood according to the common and approved usage of language; but technical words and phrases and such others as may have acquired a peculiar and appropriate meaning in the law shall be construed and understood according to such peculiar and appropriate meaning.


===== 1.02.040 Parenthetical And Reference Matter =====
The reference and version tracking material in this Code is for information only and is not a part of this Code, unless otherwise indicated. Citations to later ordinances indicate only the source of such section and the text may or may not be changed by this Code.


===== 1.02.050 Catchlines Of Section =====
The catchlines of sections of this Code printed in boldface type are intended as mere catchwords to indicate the contents of the sections and shall not be deemed or taken to be titles of such sections, nor as any part of any section, nor, unless expressly so provided, shall they be so deemed when any section, including its catchline, is amended or reenacted.


===== 1.02.060 Correction Of Technical Errors =====
The County Attorney’s Office has the discretion to review, examine, and correct any technical and stylistic errors in this Code. Technical errors include: adopting a uniform system of punctuation, capitalization, numbering, and wording; eliminating duplication and renumbering when necessary; correcting defective or inconsistent section and paragraph structure in the arrangement of the subject matter of existing statutes; eliminating all obsolete and redundant words; correcting obvious errors and inconsistencies including those involving punctuation, capitalization, cross references, numbering, and wording; changing the boldface to more accurately reflect the substance of each subsection, section, chapter, or title; and merging or determining priority of any amendments, enactments, or repealers to the same code provisions that are passed by the County Commission. This section in no way grants the County Attorney’s Office the discretion to modify the substance of an ordinance. Any necessary substantive changes to this Code may be recommended to the County Commission for consideration in a follow-up ordinance.


===== 1.02.070 Altering Code =====
It shall be unlawful, and an infraction, for any person to: change or amend by addition or deletion any part or portion of this Code; insert or delete pages, or portions thereof; or alter or tamper with such Code in any manner whatsoever which will cause the law of the County to be misrepresented thereby. Supplementation of this Code by authorized persons shall be permitted.


===== 1.03 Official And Corporate Provisions =====
1.03.010 Reference To Code, Conflicts 1.03.020 Effect Of Repeals 1.03.030 Severability Of Parts Of Code


===== 1.03.010 Reference To Code, Conflicts =====
In addition to the rules of construction and definitions specified in this title, the following rules shall be observed in the construction of this Code:
 All references to titles, chapters, or sections are to the titles, chapters, and sections of this Code unless otherwise specified. If the provisions of different titles of this Code conflict with or contravene each other, the provisions of each title shall prevail as to all matters and questions growing out of the subject matter of such title. If conflicting provisions be found in different sections of the same title, the provisions of the section which is last enacted shall prevail unless such construction would be inconsistent with the meaning of such title.


===== 1.03.020 Effect Of Repeals =====
The repeal of an ordinance shall not revive any ordinance in force before or at the time the ordinance repealed took effect. The repeal of an ordinance shall not affect any punishment or penalty incurred before the repeal took effect, nor any suit, prosecution or proceeding pending at the time of such repeal, for an offense committed or cause of action arising under the ordinance repealed.


===== 1.03.030 Severability Of Parts Of Code =====
The sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this Code are severable; and if any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, or section of this Code shall be declared invalid, unenforceable, or unconstitutional by the valid judgment of decree of a court of competent jurisdiction, such invalidity, unenforceability, or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs or sections of this Code.


===== 1.04 General Penalties =====
1.04.010 General Penalties; Continuing Violations 1.04.020 Penalty Not Exclusive 1.04.030 Liability Of Employers And Agents


===== 1.04.010 General Penalties; Continuing Violations =====
Except as provided hereafter, if the performance of an act is declared in a section of this Code, including technical codes adopted by reference and state statutes adopted as part of this Code, to be unlawful, or is described as a misdemeanor or a class B misdemeanor, or is otherwise prohibited, the act shall be punishable by a fine in an amount not exceeding one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) or by imprisonment in the County jail for a period of time not exceeding six (6) months, or by any combination of said fine and imprisonment. If the performance of an act is declared in a section of this Code, including technical codes adopted by reference and state statutes adopted as part of this Code, to be a class C misdemeanor the act shall be punishable by a fine in an amount not exceeding seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00) or by imprisonment in the County jail for a period of time not exceeding ninety (90) days, or by any combination of said fine and imprisonment. If the performance of any act is declared in a section of this Code, including technical codes adopted by reference and state statutes adopted as part of this Code, to be an infraction, the act shall be punishable by a fine in an amount not exceeding seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00). No imprisonment shall be imposed for an infraction.
 Each day that any such violation or failure to perform such act shall continue, constitutes a separate offense, unless otherwise specifically provided. In compliance with the provisions of Utah Code § 17-64-501, whenever in this Code or in any ordinance of the County a penalty for a violation of this Code or of any ordinance of the County includes any possibility of imprisonment, the County is required, under Utah Code § 78B-22-301, to provide for indigent legal defense, as those terms are defined in Utah Code § 78B-22-201.


===== 1.04.020 Penalty Not Exclusive =====
The imposition of a penalty under the provisions of this Code shall not prevent the revocation or suspension of any license, franchise, or permit issued or granted under the provisions of this Code. In the event any violation of this Code is designated as a nuisance under the provisions of this Code, such nuisance may be summarily abated by the County in addition to the imposition of a fine or imprisonment.


===== 1.04.030 Liability Of Employers And Agents =====
When the provisions of a section of this Code or an ordinance prohibit the commission or omission of an act, not only the person doing the prohibited thing, or omitting the directed act, but also the employer, if the act or omission is done within the course and scope of employment, and all other persons aiding or abetting therein, shall be guilty of the offense described and to the penalty prescribed for the offence.


===== 1.05 Adoption Of State Codes =====
1.05.010 Adoption Of Utah Criminal Code 1.05.020 Adoption Of Utah Code Of Criminal Procedure 1.05.030 Adoption Of Utah Controlled Substances Act 1.05.040 Adoption Of Imitation Controlled Substances Act


===== 1.05.010 Adoption Of Utah Criminal Code =====
The Utah Criminal Code, Utah Code § 76-1-101 et seq., is hereby adopted as a Utah County Ordinance. Provided, however, that any provision of the foregoing having a penalty which cannot be imposed for violation of a County ordinance is not adopted.


===== 1.05.020 Adoption Of Utah Code Of Criminal Procedure =====
The Utah Code of Criminal Procedure, Utah Code § 77-1-1 et seq., is hereby adopted as a Utah County Ordinance.


===== 1.05.030 Adoption Of Utah Controlled Substances Act =====
The Utah Controlled Substances Act, Utah Code § 58-37-1 et seq., is hereby adopted as a Utah County Ordinance. Provided, however, that any provision of the foregoing having a penalty which cannot be imposed for violation of a County ordinance is not adopted.


===== 1.05.040 Adoption Of Imitation Controlled Substances Act =====
The Imitation Controlled Substances Act, Utah Code § 58-37b-1 et seq., is hereby adopted as a Utah County Ordinance. Provided, however, that any provision of the foregoing having a penalty which cannot be imposed for violation of a County ordinance is not adopted. 2 ADMINISTRATION / PERSONNEL 2.01 In General 2.02 Theft Or Crime Insurance 2.03 Government Records Access And Management 2.04 Accident Review Board 2.05 County Constables 2.06 Historic Preservation Commission 2.07 Campaign Financial Disclosure 2.08 The Indigent Dead 2.09 Contact By And With Individual County Commissioners Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings 2.10 Rules Of Procedure For The Operation Of The County Commission And Other County Offices And Departments 2.11 Consolidated Of County Offices 2.12 Disaster Response And Management 2.13 Emergency Interim Succession 2.14 Professional Ethics And Conflict Of Interest 2.15 Setting Of Salaries Of Elected Officials 2.16 County Legislative Body's Consent To The Appointment Of Deputies And Employees 2.17 Appeal Of Certain County Recorder Rule Implementations


===== 2.01 In General =====
2.01.010 Improvements Reserve Fund 2.01.020 Annexation 2.01.030 Authority To Acquire, Hold, Manage And Dispose Of Real And Personal Property For Benefit Of County 2.01.040 Financial Administration 2.01.050 Adoption Of Procurement Regulations 2.01.060 Fees Of County Officers; Refund Of Overpayments 2.01.070 Fee Waiver Requests 2.01.080 The County Logo And Slogan


===== 2.01.010 Improvements Reserve Fund =====
There is hereby established a reserve fund for this County for the specific purposes of accumulating funds pursuant to Utah Code §§ 17-63-803, 17-63-804, and any other applicable state code, for financing the purchase of real property, costs of planning, constructing, or designing any of the following facilities in and for this County: jail building; court building; animal control shelter; youth home; or additions to any of the foregoing. Each structures in paragraph A or improvements thereto is hereby defined and determined to be a capital improvement. The reserve fund established by this section shall be funded in the tax year 1976 by a 1.41 mill levy and in subsequent tax years by a mill levy as established by the County Commission on a year-by-year basis.
 State law reference - Reserve fund for major capital improvements, Utah Code § 17-63-803 et seq.


===== 2.01.020 Annexation =====
Any annexation of territory in the County by a municipality which has part or all of its incorporated limits lying outside of the County, shall occur only upon compliance with Utah Code § 10-2-401 et seq. or its successor statute.


===== 2.01.030 Authority To Acquire, Hold, Manage And Dispose Of Real And Personal Property For Benefit Of County =====
The County Commission may purchase, receive, hold, sell, lease, convey or otherwise acquire and dispose of any real or personal property or any interest is such property that it determines to be in the public interest, subject to the following provisions:
 Means of disposal. Subject to Subsection C and D, the County Commission may dispose of, or control the disposition of, any County property, real or personal, and including lost or abandoned property, or any legal or equitable interest in property, the disposition of which is determined to be in the public interest and in accordance with good property management. The disposition of property, or any interest therein, may be by: public or private sale; exchange; exchange and sale; option to purchase; lease; lease with an option to purchase; rental; trade-in; public auction; public advertisement for sealed bids; or any other lawful manner or means. Disposition of property shall not be for less than a full and adequate consideration except for: property which is determined to lie within the boundaries of a street or highway; properties which are conveyed or traded to a municipality or the State of Utah; or for small parcels of property unusable by the County. Consideration for property may be other than monetary. The County Commission may set minimum bid prices on property, real or personal, based upon professional appraisals or by recommendations by the department or departments of the County involved in the sale. Disposal of personal property. The power to declare and dispose of surplus, obsolete, or unusable personal property held by the County is vested in the County Purchasing Agent and such disposition may be made in any manner authorized in Subsection A. The Purchasing Agent shall have discretion, subject to the best interests of County government and of the people of the County and in accordance with good property management techniques, over the disposition and manner of disposition of surplus, obsolete, or unusable personal property with a value of five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) or less, provided, however, that such discretion extends only to the initiation and negotiation of the sale or other disposition. For property with a value in excess of five thousand dollars ($5,000.00), the disposition and manner of disposition of surplus property must be approved prior to the commencement of negotiations by the Purchasing Agent and County Administrator. No disposition involving amounts in excess of five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) shall be split into parts by any concerned party so as to produce amounts of five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) or less, for the purpose of avoiding the provisions of this chapter. Disposal of real property in public use or of a significant parcel of real property. The power to dispose of real property held by the County is vested in the County Commission and such disposition may be made in any manner authorized in Subsection A. The disposition of real property which is in public use, regardless of the value thereof, or of a significant parcel of real property, as defined herein, shall be made only with the approval of the County Commission and after a public hearing and reasonable notice thereof, as defined herein. Real property in public use is defined as realty, and improvements thereon, in actual current use by the County, including but not limited to: governmental offices or other public buildings; courthouses; jails and police stations; fire stations; developed parks or other recreational or entertainment facilities; libraries; schools or other educational facilities; utilities; cemeteries; animal control facilities; hospitals or other health facilities; facilities for the welfare of indigents; sanitary landfills; or any other realty or improvement thereon held for the benefit or advantage of the general public and not confined to use by privileged or particular individuals, without regard to whether that use may be classified as governmental or proprietary. A significant parcel of real property is defined as any parcel larger than ten (10) acres, or having an estimated value, as determined by the County Commission, of more than five hundred thousand dollars ($500,000.00). The County Commission shall hold a public hearing concerning dispositions of realty under this subsection, which hearing may be at any special or regularly scheduled meeting of the County Commission, at which hearing all interested persons may appear and be heard. Public notice shall be given of said hearing which shall contain the date, time, and place thereof, a statement of the purpose of the hearing, and a general description of the property, or the interest therein, to be disposed. Such notice shall be published at least once, fourteen (14) or more days prior to the date of the hearing, in a newspaper of general circulation in the County. The notice required herein is defined as reasonable notice. Application to County agencies. The provisions of this title shall be applicable to all boards, commissions, authorities, committees, councils, or other bodies within the jurisdiction of the County Commission and owning or holding real or personal property. When real or personal property is disposed of under the provisions of this title and such property was purchased by a County agency or other body which has an independent revenue base or which has an account separate from the general fund, that agency's account shall be credited with the amount realized from the disposition. If the County receives something other than monetary consideration for the disposition, distribution of that consideration shall be in accordance with the direction of the County Commission.
 HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-163 Updated Authority To Acquire, Hold, Manage, And Dispose Of Real And Personal Property For Benefit Of The County on 2/12/2025


===== 2.01.040 Financial Administration =====
"Benefits" are payments made to employees for uniform allowances, insurance waivers, and bonuses; or to other entities on behalf of employees, which are in addition to the employee's salaries/wages, including insurance premiums, retirement contributions, unemployment contributions, and worker's compensation. employees for uniform allowances, insurance waivers, and bonuses;
or other entities on behalf of employees, which are in addition to the
employee's salaries and wages, including insurance premiums,
retirement contributions, unemployment contributions, and worker's
compensation. "Capital assets" are land, buildings, building improvements, vehicles, machinery, equipment, works of art, historical treasures, infrastructure, easements, software, and water rights that: are used in operations; have an initial useful life of two years or more; and have a per-unit cost of five thousand ($5,000) or higher. "FGP" means the Foster Grandparents Program "GASB" means the Governmental Accounting Standards Board. "Internal service charges " means appropriations for: motor pool charges; building rents; telephone system charges; radio charges; computer support charges; GIS/programming charges; Administrative service charges; and equipment replacement charges. "Salaries" and "wages" includes all compensation paid to employees but does not include compensation classified as overtime by the Fair Labor Standards Act, as amended. "SCP" means the Senior Companion Program. In enacting this section, it is the purpose and intent of the County Commission to comply with Utah Code § 17-63-601 by adopting a financial administration ordinance. County warrants. The County Commission shall approve the issuance of all County warrants, except as provided in Subsection C,2. payroll checks and payroll-related expenses that are prepared in accordance with the salary schedule established by the County in its personnel resolution; stipend checks for the FGP and SCP volunteers; bail commissioner checks; Justice Court surcharge payments to the state; Sheriff extradition travel advance checks; stipend checks for inmates who work in the jail; vehicle purchases with payment terms less than thirty (30) days; and regularly scheduled utility payments. All warrants authorized for issuance by the County Auditor must be ratified thereafter by the County Commission to have effect. County purchase orders approvals. Purchase orders shall require approvals from the following entities: for purchases up to ten thousand dollars ($10,000), Division Manager; for purchases greater than ten thousand dollars ($10,000), and up to fifty thousand dollars ($50,000), Department Head; for purchases greater than fifty thousand dollars ($50,000), and up to one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000), the County Administrator; and for purchases greater than one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000), the County Commission. The County Purchasing Agent or designee is authorized to approve purchase orders that do not require the approval of the County Commission as provided for in Subsection D,1. Notwithstanding the requirements of Subsection D,1, the County Purchasing Agent may approve purchase orders for the following purchases: postage costs paid to the United States Postal Service and State of Utah Mail Services; debt service payments related to County bonds; FGP & SCP volunteer stipends; pass-through payments for intergovernmental purposes, such as the State portion of marriage license fees or vital records fees; payments to the health insurance provider for the County; refunds; survivor benefits in accordance with the rules and regulations governing human resources; vaccinations and medications obtained by the Health Department; utility payments including those for electricity, natural gas, water, and telecommunication services; or bulk fuel purchases made at facilities owned by the County and fuel purchases made pursuant to requisitions to the State of Utah gas card program or similar program. The County Treasurer shall authorize the disbursement of all County warrants issued by the County Commission and the County Auditor in such a manner as to allow the County to maximize earnings on its investments. The County Auditor, acting as the financial officer under the provisions of this chapter, shall be bonded in the amount of ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00). Budget Transfers. Budget transfers from either the following accounts, or budget appropriations for the following purposes, require the prior approval and authorization of the County Commission: salaries/wages for employees eligible for County paid health insurance premiums/waivers; benefits; internal service leases; capital assets; and other budget appropriations deemed restricted by the County Commission through amendment of this ordinance or temporarily by resolution except as authorized in Subsection G,2. The County Administrator may approve a budget transfer from salaries or wages accounts to overtime and time-limited accounts: when budgeted positions have been vacant during the current budget year; and after ensuring that unused salaries/wages from vacant positions are greater than the amount of the proposed transfer. Write-off of Uncollectible Accounts Receivable. This section applies only to receivables that are processed through the County Auditor's Office. Receivables include amounts billed by the County for services rendered to another individual, governmental agency, or entity and any claim for funds, monies, or other property which would normally be required by GASB to be recorded as a receivable in the official records of a governmental unit. A write-off of uncollectible accounts receivable from the County's accounting records does not constitute forgiveness of the debt or a gift of public funds. Only uncollectible accounts receivable that are ninety (90) days past due or older may be written off under this section. For receivables that meet the requirements of sections H,1 through H,4, above, the County Commission authorizes the write-off of uncollectible accounts receivable as follows: for receivable balances equal to or less than one thousand dollars ($1,000), the County Auditor may write off the receivable upon review by the County Attorney's Office that all reasonable efforts ·have been taken to collect the receivable; for receivable balances greater than one thousand dollars ($1,000) and equal to or less than ten thousand dollars ($10,000), the receivable may be written off with the approval of both the County Auditor and the County Administrator after review by the County Attorney's Office that all reasonable efforts have been taken to collect the receivable; and for receivable balances over ten thousand dollars ($10,000), the County Commission must approve the write-off. Nothing in this section prevents the County Auditor or County Administrator from submitting any uncollectible accounts receivable less than or equal to ten thousand dollars ($10,000) to the County Commission for write-off approval. Capital asset donations. A capital asset donated to the County may only be accepted by resolution of the County Commission. Any department that wishes to accept a donated capital asset must complete a capital asset donation form created and maintained by the County Auditor's Office. The County Auditor's Office shall provide a written analysis and recommendation to the County Commission regarding the financial impacts of accepting the capital asset.
