===== 2.01.050 Adoption Of Procurement Regulations =====
Pursuant to the Utah Procurement Code and the Utah State Procurement Policy Board, the Utah County Procurement Regulations are hereby adopted in book form and by this reference made a part of this chapter to the same extent and effect as though said Regulations were copied herein in full.


===== 2.01.060 Fees Of County Officers; Refund Of Overpayments =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 17-66-303, a Utah County Government Fee Schedule is hereby adopted in book form and by this reference made a part of the County Code to the same extent and effect as though said Fee Schedule was copied herein in full. In the event a county fee is paid in advance and the amount paid exceeds the actual fee by ten dollars ($10) or less, the amount so overpaid shall be forfeited to the general fund of the County. In the event a county fee is paid in advance and the amount paid exceeds the actual fee by more than ten dollars ($10), the county office which received the overpayment shall transmit to the payor a form by which the payor may request a refund of the overpayment. If the County Auditor receives a completed request for refund within ninety (90) days of the date the form is transmitted to the payor, the County Auditor shall prepare and send a check in the amount of the overpayment to the payor. If a completed request for refund is not received by the County Auditor within ninety (90) days of the date the form is transmitted to the payor, the amount overpaid shall be forfeited to the general fund of the County.


===== 2.01.070 Fee Waiver Requests =====
Requests for waiver of fees shall be by written application, shall set forth the specific basis for the request, and shall include all evidence supporting the request. A decision shall be made on each request based upon the submitted materials unless the County Commission desires further input from the applicant and takes action in a public meeting to place the matter on the commission agenda for further review. Except as specifically otherwise authorized by the County Commission, each request for waiver of fees shall be referred to the Civil Division of the County Attorney’s Office for review and recommendation. The attorney assigned to review the fee waiver request may request recommendations from involved county offices, additional information from other governmental agencies and private organizations, additional information from the applicant, and that the applicant appear to address issues raised by the application. The attorney assigned shall review all of the submitted materials and information and shall then provide a recommendation on the application to the County Commission. The County Commission shall review the submitted materials and the recommendation of the assigned attorney and shall then take action on the request at a County Commission meeting based upon the submitted materials.


===== 2.01.080 The County Logo And Slogan =====
The County logo is described as follows: A sketch of Mount Timpanogos and the Wasatch Range, with the words “Utah County, Heart of Utah” below. The County Commission is authorized to develop and approve other County logos and any logo so approved by the County Commission shall be retained on file with the County Commission. Use of logo and slogan. The County logo and slogan shall only be used for the official business of the County, its County Commission, boards and commissions, officers or departments, except upon approval of the County Commission by ordinance or resolution. Except as provided for in this section, no individual or entity, other than the County and its authorized agents, shall reproduce, use, give away, sell, or distribute any logo or slogan, or facsimile thereof, purporting to be or represented to be the logo or slogan of the County. Additional County logos and slogans. The County Commission retains the right to create variations of the County logo and slogan and to adopt and establish other official logos and slogans. Logo or slogan variations may include, but are not limited to, centennial seals, or other seals which mark anniversaries, events, or any other County occasion the County wishes to commemorate. The County logo and slogan may be altered pursuant to resolution of the County Commission. The County Clerk shall be the official custodian of the County logo and slogan, and any other County logo and slogan adopted by the County Commission. A recognized employee organization may use the County seal or logo and slogan, or facsimile thereof, on stationery and printed materials used in the scope of its representation of County employees and on articles of clothing intended for use by County employees. The County Commission is empowered to authorize the use of the County logo and slogan on items that are offered for sale by the County for the purpose of promoting the County.
 Penalty. Any person violating this section shall be guilty of an infraction. A violation of this section may be prosecuted by the County Attorney’s Office in the name of the people of the State of Utah or redressed by civil action. Every violation of this section is punishable by: a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100) for a first violation; a fine not exceeding two hundred dollars ($200) for a second violation of the same section within one year; or a fine not exceeding five hundred dollars ($500) for each additional violation of the same section within one year. The County reserves the right to take any other legal action it finds appropriate to enforce this section.


===== 2.02 Theft Or Crime Insurance =====
2.02.010 Amount Required For County Officials 2.02.020 Amount Required For County Treasurer 2.02.030 Amount Required For Deputies Or Assistants 2.02.040 Blanket Policy 2.02.050 Payment Of Premium


===== 2.02.010 Amount Required For County Officials =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 17-66-105, the County Commission has prescribed ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00) as the amount of theft or crime insurance to be acquired for county officials, as therein defined in Utah Code § 17-66-105(1)(a) through (d), before those county officials may discharge the duties of their respective offices.


===== 2.02.020 Amount Required For County Treasurer =====
The State Money Management Council created in Utah Code § 51-7-16 shall prescribe the amount of theft or crime insurance to be acquired for the County Treasurer before the County Treasurer may discharge the duties of that office.


===== 2.02.030 Amount Required For Deputies Or Assistants =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 17-66-105, the County Commission has prescribed five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) as the amount of theft or crime insurance to be acquired for each deputy or assistant of those listed in Utah Code § 17-66-105(a) through (d) before those county officials may discharge the duties of their respective offices.


===== 2.02.040 Blanket Policy =====
The County Commission may acquire theft or crime insurance on all county officials as a group rather than individually.


===== 2.02.050 Payment Of Premium =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 17-66-105, the cost of theft or crime insurance policies shall be paid from county funds.


===== 2.03 Government Records Access And Management =====
2.03.010 In General 2.03.020 Purpose Of Chapter 2.03.030 Definitions 2.03.040 Right Of Privacy 2.03.050 Classification And Retention Of Records 2.03.060 Request For Records 2.03.070 Establishment Of Fees 2.03.080 Appeals 2.03.090 Amendment Of Records 2.03.100 Records Officers 2.03.110 Management Of Records 2.03.120 Justice Court Records 2.03.130 Penalty


===== 2.03.010 In General =====
The County Commission finds the following:
 It is in the best interests of the County and the citizens thereof, and essential for the administration of County government, to maintain and preserve accurate governmental records; to provide ready access to records which are defined by law as open to the public; to maintain the security of records which are defined by law as non-public; and to ensure the preservation of vital and historically valuable records. As the records of the County government agencies are a resource containing information which (1) allows government programs to function; (2) provides officials with a basis for making decisions and ensuring continuity with past operations; and (3) permits citizens to research and document matters of personal and community importance; this resource must be systematically and efficiently managed. It is the policy of the County that all governmental records, which are defined by applicable Utah statutory and case law as public records, shall be made available to citizens as set forth in this chapter. The County recognizes a public policy interest in allowing the government to restrict access to certain records, as specified in the Government Records Access and Management Act and this chapter, for the public good.


===== 2.03.020 Purpose Of Chapter =====
In enacting this chapter, it is the purpose and intent of the County Commission to provide, in accordance with the Government Records Access and Management Act, Utah Code § 63G-2, (hereinafter referred to as “the Act”) an ordinance acknowledging and complying with the Act and providing for its application in the County. County agencies shall comply with the provisions of the Act except for the section of this chapter that are contrary to or in addition to the Act, as allowed by the Act, and shall also comply with other federal and state statutory and regulatory record-keeping requirements.


===== 2.03.030 Definitions =====
Unless otherwise defined herein, the defined terms in the Act shall have the same meaning in this chapter as they have in the Act. As used in this chapter, the following definitions shall be applicable: "Act" shall refer to the Government Records Access and Management Act, Utah Code § 63G-2-101, et seq. "Agency" shall refer to any office, department, division, section, staff office, board, committee, or other division of the County government. "Chief Administrative Officer" shall refer to the County Commission. "Data" shall refer to individual entries (for example, birth date, address, etc.) in records. "Dispose” means to destroy, or render irretrievable or illegible, a record or the information contained in it by any physical, electronic, or other means, including unauthorized deletion or erasure of electronically recorded audio, visual, non- written formats, data processing, or other records. "Non-public" records shall refer to those records defined as private, controlled, or protected under the provisions of this chapter and of the Act.


===== 2.03.040 Right Of Privacy =====
The County recognizes and upholds the personal right of privacy retained by persons who may be the subject of governmental records. The County also recognizes that the Act and Utah case law establish a presumption that governmental records will generally be considered open and public, with certain specific exceptions. In circumstances where a record’s public or non-public status is not specifically established by the Act or another statute, by this chapter, or by policies established or designations made under this chapter, the public’s right to access and the record subject’s right of privacy must be compared. The County shall not release any records when to do so would constitute a clearly unwarranted invasion of personal privacy. The County may, as determined appropriate by the Agency director of the Agency responding to a request for records, notify the subject of a record that a request for access to the subject’s record has been made.


===== 2.03.050 Classification And Retention Of Records =====
All County records and records series, of any format, shall be evaluated, designated, and classified under the supervision of the County Records Officer according to the provisions of this chapter and Part 3 of the Act, as amended. Upon the receipt of a records request under the Act, the County Attorney shall make the final determination as to the classification or reclassification of the requested records. All County records and records series, of any format, shall be scheduled for retention under the supervision of the County Records Officer and according to the provisions of the Act and this chapter. The County shall retain its records in compliance with the County General Retention Schedule created and maintained by the Utah Division of Archives and Records Service unless and until the County adopts a records retention schedule. The County may designate or redesignate or classify or reclassify records or data at any time and is not required to classify a particular record or item of data until access thereto is requested. Any records or record series generated in the future shall also be so designated, classified and scheduled for retention. Designation, classification and retention scheduling forms and guidelines may be prepared and promulgated by the Records Officer. Each Agency shall receive, store, and preserve County records and provide reasonable access thereto as may be calculated to accurately and safely maintain County records during the retention period in compliance with this chapter and the Act. The County retains and reserves to itself the right to use any type of format for the storage, retention and retrieval of government records, including but not limited to, imaging or electronic information storage or processing equipment or systems. The methods of access to records in non-written formats or data processing systems shall be as determined appropriate by the Agency director of the Agency maintaining the records, considering all circumstances.


===== 2.03.060 Request For Records =====
Under circumstances in which an Agency is not able to immediately respond to a records request, the requester shall submit a records request through the State of Utah web-based Open Records Portal or fill out and present to the Agency a written request in compliance with the Act. For written requests, the date and time of the request shall be noted on the written request form and all deadlines provided under this chapter and the Act shall commence from that time and date. For requests submitted through the Records Portal, the deadlines provided under this chapter and the Act shall be calculated within the Records Portal. Requesters of non-public information shall adequately identify themselves and, if applicable, their status when requesting access to non-public records. Any County record which is subject to litigation, criminal investigation or audit or has been requested in accordance with this chapter and the Act, that is disposable by approved retention schedule, may not be disposed of until the litigation or audit has been resolved or the request is granted and fulfilled, or sixty (60) days after the request is denied if no appeals are filed, or sixty (60) days after all appeals are completed.


===== 2.03.070 Establishment Of Fees =====
The County or any of its Agencies may charge a reasonable fee to cover the actual cost of duplicating a record or compiling a record in a form other than that maintained by the Agency. All fees charged hereunder shall be consistent with the Act and the adopted Utah County Fee Schedule.


===== 2.03.080 Appeals =====
The County adopts the Appeal procedure from Part 4 of the Act, as amended, with the following amendments or additions to provide for the specific appeal procedure in the County and recognizing that in some respects the County has limited resources that necessitate the time frames provided for herein: A requester or interested party may appeal an access denial or County claim of extraordinary circumstances, including the date for compliance, to the County’s chief administrative officer, by filing a written notice of appeal, satisfying the requirements of a notice of appeal in the Act, with the County Administrator within thirty (30) calendar days of the denial. The chief administrative officer shall enter its decision on the appeal in accordance with Utah Code Part 63G-2-4 or schedule the appeal for an administrative hearing to be held within fifteen (15) business days of receipt of the notice of appeal. If the appeal involves a record that is the subject of a business confidentiality claim under the Act, an administrative hearing shall be scheduled. If an administrative hearing is held, the chief administrative officer shall provide adequate notice to the requester, interested party or business confidentiality claimant with the date, time, and location of the hearing where they will have an opportunity to be heard concerning the appeal and enter its decision on the appeal within five (5) business days of holding the hearing. Failure of the chief administrative officer to issue a decision or schedule an administrative hearing in accordance with Part 4 of the Act or failure to issue a decision within five (5) business days of the administrative hearing, is the equivalent of affirming the access denial. The chief administrative officer shall send written notice of its decision to all participants. If the chief administrative officer’s decision is to affirm the access denial in whole or in part, the notice required herein shall satisfy the requirements of the Act.
 If the decision of the chief administrative officer is to affirm the access denial in whole or in part, the requester or interested party may appeal the decision to the Utah State Records Committee or petition for judicial review of the decision in state district court in accordance with the Act.


===== 2.03.090 Amendment Of Records =====
Records held by the County may be amended or corrected as needed, in accordance with the Act. Requests for amendments, corrections, or other changes shall be made in writing to the Agency having custody of the records and setting forth, with specificity, the amendment or correction requested and the reason for the change. When necessary, the County shall comply with the Open and Public Meetings Act before amending a record. When an amendment or correction of a government record is made, generally both the original record and the amended or corrected record shall be retained, unless the nature of the record indicates otherwise or as may be provided by policies and procedures adopted under the provisions of this chapter. This section does not apply to records relating to title to real or personal property, medical records, judicial case files, or any other records that the County Commission determines must be maintained in their original form to protect the public interest and to preserve the integrity of the record system. Denials of requests to amend records may be appealed following the appeal procedure provided by this chapter.


===== 2.03.100 Records Officers =====
There shall be appointed a County Records Officer to oversee and coordinate records access and management and County archives activities. The Records Officer shall make annual reports of records services activities to the County Commission. The County Commission may hereafter create a Government Records Access and Management Policy Administration (“Records Policy Administration”), to be chaired by the County Records Officer. Members of the Records Policy Administration, if created, would include representatives from the Commission staff, the County departments, and the elected offices. The Records Policy Administration, if created, would establish meeting times and policies as needed, and as determined by the County Records Officer. Each Agency of County government shall appoint a Records Representative to assist with and be directly responsible for the implementation of this chapter. Regular training shall be provided under the direction of the Records Officer to Agency Records Representatives. The Records Officer shall develop and provide records management, maintenance and access standards, policies and procedures, as approved by the County Commission, to govern and implement the provisions of the Act and this chapter. Copies of any rule or policy promulgated under this chapter shall be forwarded by the County Records Officer to the Utah State Division of Archives within thirty (30) days after its effective date. Any Agency’s internal policies regarding records management and access shall be consistent with this chapter and state law.


===== 2.03.110 Management Of Records =====
Records management procedures shall be developed to ensure that due care is taken to maintain and preserve County records safely and accurately during the retention period for each classification of record. The Records Officer shall work with County departments to ensure the care, maintenance, scheduling, disposal, classification, designation, access, and preservation of records. The Records Officer shall be responsible for monitoring the application and use of technical processes in the creation, duplication, and disposal of County records and shall monitor compliance with required standards of quality, permanence, and admissibility pertaining to the creation, use, and maintenance of records. Policies and regulations regarding types and formats of papers, inks, electronic media, and other records and information storage media, materials, equipment, procedures, and techniques shall be developed and promulgated, subject to the approval of the County Commission. Custodians of any County records shall, at the expiration of their terms of office, appointment or employment, deliver custody and control of all records kept or received by them to their successors, supervisors, or to the County Records Officer.


===== 2.03.120 Justice Court Records =====
Records activities of the County Justice Court system shall comply with and be governed by § 63G-2-702 of the Act.


===== 2.03.130 Penalty =====
A public employee or other person who has lawful access to any private, controlled, or protected record under this chapter, and who intentionally discloses, provides a copy of, or improperly uses a private controlled, or protected record knowing that the disclosure or use is prohibited under this chapter, is, except as provided in Utah Code § 53-5-708(1)(c), guilty of a class B misdemeanor. It is a defense to prosecution under Subsection A1 that the actor used or released private, controlled, or protected information in the reasonable belief that the use or disclosure of the information was necessary to expose a violation of law involving government corruption, abuse of office, or misappropriation of public funds or property. It is a defense to prosecution under Subsection A1 that the record could have lawfully been released to the recipient if it had been properly classified. It is a defense to prosecution under Subsection A1 that the public employee or other person disclosed, provided, or used the record based on a good faith belief that the disclosure, provision, or use was in accordance with the law.
 A person who by false pretenses, bribery, or theft, gain access to or obtain a copy of any private, controlled, or protected record to which the person is not legally entitled is guilty of a class B misdemeanor. No person shall be guilty under Subsection B1 who receives the record, information, or copy after the fact and without prior knowledge of or participation in the false pretenses, bribery, or theft.
 A public employee who intentionally refuses to release a record, the disclosure of which the employee knows is required by law, is guilty of a class B misdemeanor. It is a defense to prosecution under Subsection C1 that the public employee’s failure to release the record was based on a good faith belief that the public employee was acting in accordance with the requirements of law. A public employee who intentionally refuses to release a record, the disclosure of which the employee knows is required by a final unappealed order from a government entity, the records committee, or a court is guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 2.04 Accident Review Board =====
2.04.010 Establishment Of County Risk Management Committee And Discretion To Form Department Safety Subcommittees 2.04.020 Department Head Responsibility HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-931 Updated Accident Review Board on 10/29/2025


===== 2.04.010 Establishment Of County Risk Management Committee And Discretion To Form Department Safety Subcommittees =====
There is hereby established a County Risk Management Committee for the County. The County Risk Management Committee shall be charged with advising the County Commission concerning County-wide safety, risk, and loss prevention issues, including: risk financing and insurance; incidents and claims; litigation; risk and safety prevention; mitigation; training; and response programs The County Risk Management Committee shall consist of: the County Safety and Risk Manager; the County Sheriff; the County Public Works Director; the County Health Department Director; the County Attorney; the County Director of Information Technology Services; the County Auditor; the County Emergency Manager; and the County Administrator. There shall be a secretary selected for the Committee by the Safety and Risk Manager. The individuals listed in Subsection C may select a designee to participate in the County Risk Management Committee in that individual's place. The individuals listed in Subsection C may authorize subject matter experts to attend on a case-by-case basis depending on the topics being discussed. Department Safety Subcommittees. The County Risk Management Committee may establish and oversee additional Department Safety Subcommittees to serve the: County Sheriff's Office; County Public Works Department; County Health Department; and all other County Departments. These Safety subcommittees, if created, may be tasked with the following responsibilities: investigate incidents and accidents, determine root causes, and recommend corrective actions to management for the following: vehicular incidents and accidents that involve a County vehicle; vehicular incidents and accidents that involve a non-County vehicle where an employee is driving or riding as a passenger during the scope of employment; incidents and accidents resulting in potential or actual damage to County property; incidents and accidents resulting in potential or actual injury to a County employee; incidents and accidents resulting in potential or actual injury to a community member as a result of county operations: incidents and accidents resulting in potential or actual damage to a community member's property as a result of County operations; inform the Risk Management Committee of significant incidents and claims that may have inter-departmental impact; conduct inspections, identify hazards, and recommend changes; recommend and implement safety and risk programs and ensure compliance with safety standards; and provide training and education to employees, supervisors, and management. Department Safety Subcommittees will be directed by the respective department head, except in the case of the Subcommittee described in Subsection G,1,d, which will be directed by a designee of the County Risk Management Committee, in coordination with the Risk Management Committee. For example, duties may be distributed among existing departmental committees rather than forming one central committee. Members of the Department Safety Subcommittees should be selected from employees who can represent the business functions, speak to the safety issues, and make informed recommendations on potential solutions to safety and risk issues. Each Department Safety Subcommittee will include a member of the County Safety and risk staff and a Deputy County Attorney from the County Attorney's Office, Civil Division. The Department Safety Subcommittees do not have disciplinary authority.


===== 2.04.020 Department Head Responsibility =====
If a Department Safety Subcommittee's findings and recommendations include corrective actions, policy changes, or specialized training, the Department Head shall consider the recommendations and implement those which the Department Head deems appropriate within their departmental budgetary and operational constraints. Recommended corrective actions may include: facility modifications; equipment modifications; modifications to operating procedures; program modifications or changes; required safety training; or any other recommended corrective actions deemed appropriate based on individual circumstances.


===== 2.05 County Constables =====
2.05.010 Appointment Of Constables 2.05.020 Liability Of Constables 2.05.030 Constable Nominating Commission 2.05.040 Term Of Office 2.05.050 Certification 2.05.060 User Agreements 2.05.070 Removal Of Constables 2.05.080 Approval Of Deputy Constables 2.05.090 Procedure For Oversight Of County Constables And Deputy Constables By County HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-52 Updated County Constables on 1/8/2025


===== 2.05.010 Appointment Of Constables =====
The County Commission may appoint as many qualified persons to act as County constables as convenience requires, subject to the provisions of Utah Code § 17-78-601. Each County constable shall be appointed on a County-wide basis and may serve civil and criminal process pursuant to state law. To qualify for appointment as a County constable, a person shall be certified as a special function peace officer in the State of Utah. The County Commission shall conduct full background checks on persons seeking appointment as County constables before considering such appointment.


===== 2.05.020 Liability Of Constables =====
County constables shall be responsible for supervising their own deputies, office personnel and administrative matters, and for insuring compliance with all applicable laws and ordinances. County constables are independent contractors and are responsible for their own actions and for the actions of their deputies, agents and employees, and any liability resulting therefrom. When executing civil process, constables are acting agents of the litigant or litigant's attorney who employs their services. Constables, as officers of the court, are also acting in their official capacity as special function peace officers empowered by law to enforce the lawful orders of any court of competent jurisdiction.


===== 2.05.030 Constable Nominating Commission =====
When the County Commission determines that one or more constables should be appointed, the County Commission shall establish a Constable Nominating Commission. The Nominating Commission shall consist of the following individuals or their designees: a County Commissioner; the County Sheriff; a judge of the justice or district court; the County Attorney; and an impartial resident appointed by the County Commission Upon being appointed, designated, or informed of the request of the County Commission that the Nominating Commission is to be convened, the members so designated will meet within ten (10) days' time and develop the criteria for advertising the opening of the constable position and the selection and ranking of applicants for the position. Applicants who meet the eligibility criteria established by Utah Code § 17-78-601 for the position of County constable shall be interviewed by the Nominating Commission. After interviewing the applicants and evaluating their qualifications, the Nominating Commission shall rank the applicants in order of preference and submit up to three names for each vacancy together with other recommendations to the County Commission. The County Commission, upon receipt of the Nominating Commission's list of successful applicants ranked in order of preference, or stating that all applicants are equally qualified, together with its recommendations and within fifteen (15) calendar days thereafter, shall: approve the Nominating Commission's recommendations and appoint the recommended applicant(s); reject the recommended applicant(s) and select one or more of the remaining applicants on the list; or reject all the named applicants and refer the matter back to the Nominating Commission for a new list and recommendation.


===== 2.05.040 Term Of Office =====
A constable's term of office is six (6) years. A constable may serve more than one term if reappointed by the County Commission.


===== 2.05.050 Certification =====
Deputy constables must be certified by the Division of Peace Officer Standards and Training as qualified to undertake the duties of a special function peace officer and shall be approved by the Nominating Commission. After a newly appointed County constable has been appointed by the County Commission, the constable's name shall be reported to the Division of Peace Officer Standards and Training by the County Commission Clerk.


===== 2.05.060 User Agreements =====
Constables may enter into user agreements with the County Sheriff for access to the County Sheriff's Department criminal history information the County Sheriff deems appropriate. Upon entering into a user agreement, the constable shall be responsible for ensuring the confidentiality requirements of the agreement are complied with. Constables may enter into user agreements with agencies of the State of Utah for access to State and national criminal history information systems.


===== 2.05.070 Removal Of Constables =====
County constables shall be subject to removal for cause after a determination for removal has been made by an administrative tribunal convened by the County Commission to adjudicate the matter. Removal proceedings may be initiated by the County Attorney or the County Commission, and shall be structured as to afford the constable due process. Detailed procedures for initiating and conducting removal proceedings shall be as set forth in the Utah County Human Resources Rules and Regulations manual. The following shall constitute an automatic disqualification and removal from office: loss of certification by the Division of Peace Officer Standards and Training; the conviction of any felony, or conviction of an offense involving dishonesty, unlawful sexual conduct, physical violence, or the unlawful use, sale or possession for sale of a controlled substance, as defined by the Division of Peace Officer Standards and Training administrative rule; failure to comply with all requirements set forth in applicable statutes and this ordinance; and a judgement against the constable in any civil case resulting from or related to the constable's discharge of official duties as a constable. A County constable may be removed from office if it is established that the constable has willfully failed to comply with the written administrative policies and procedures approved by the County Commission or the County Officers and Employees Disclosures Act, Utah Code § 17-16a-1, et seq.


===== 2.05.080 Approval Of Deputy Constables =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 17-25a-3(3), an appointed County constable may, upon approval of the County Commission, employ and deputize persons who are certified as special function peace officers to function as deputy constables. The County Commission shall consider the approval of deputy constable candidates after the submission of the following to the County Commission by the appointed County constable: a copy of the deputy constable candidate’s employment application; Peace Officer Standards and Training (POST) certification for the deputy constable candidate;
 a letter from the appointed County constable declaring that the deputy constable candidate is a person of good moral character; letters of reference from two registered voters in the County, declaring that the deputy constable candidate is a person of good moral character; and a certification by the appointed County constable that the deputy constable candidate has had no criminal convictions during the previous seven (7) years. The County Sheriff shall, within ten (10) days of the submission of the documents enumerated in Subsection B, complete the following: verify that the deputy constable candidate is POST certified and is in good standing with POST; perform a credit check on the deputy constable candidate; perform a drug screen on the deputy constable candidate; and file a written report of the County Sheriff’s findings with the County Commission.
 The appointed County constable shall certify annually in writing to the County Attorney’s office that each deputy constable has completed a minimum of forty (40) hours of in-service training, including a minimum of eight (8) hours of firearm training with a POST certified firearms instructor. Within one (1) year after appointment as a deputy constable, each deputy constable shall undergo psychological testing and evaluation to determine the deputy constable’s fitness to carry a firearm and to serve as a deputy constable. A deputy constable shall be required to undergo psychological testing and evaluation at any time upon the written request of the County Attorney’s Office or the County Commission. All costs of training, testing, and evaluation of deputy constable candidates and deputy constables shall be borne by the appointed County constable employing said deputy constables. The County Commission may revoke its approval of a deputy constable, at any time, with or without cause. The term of a deputy constable's approval shall expire with the term of the supervising constable.


===== 2.05.090 Procedure For Oversight Of County Constables And Deputy Constables By County =====
If an individual has a complaint against an appointed County constable or deputy constable concerning a constable’s performance, the complainant may file a written statement concerning the complaint with the Civil Division Chief of the County Attorney’s Office. Upon receipt of such a complaint regarding a constable the Civil Division Chief shall review the complaint and may thereafter schedule a meeting with the affected constable, the County Sheriff or designee, and, if deemed appropriate, the complainant, to discuss the complaint and to formulate the manner in which to resolve the complaint. An appointed County constable, or an approved deputy constable, shall appear before the County Commission at any time upon request of the County Commission to respond to questions or concerns regarding the conduct or performance of an appointed County constable or an approved deputy constable. An appointed County constable shall provide a quarterly report to the County Commission by the end of March, June, September, and December of each year containing the following: Total number of papers served year-to-date, broken down by type or category, including total number of papers served outside of Utah County; List of all deputy constables working for constable; List of names of all business entities registered under or affiliated with constable that provide constable related services; Number and description of past and current claims, complaints, or lawsuits against the constable or the constable's deputies; and The constable's current fee schedule.


===== 2.06 Historic Preservation Commission =====
2.06.010 Purpose 2.06.020 Historic Preservation Commission 2.06.030 Historic Preservation Commission Duties


===== 2.06.010 Purpose =====
The County recognizes that the historical heritage of the County is among its most valued and important assets. It is therefore the intent of the County to identify, preserve, protect and enhance historic and prehistoric areas and sites lying within the County. The County asserts its authority under Utah Code § 17-78-202 to: expend public funds to preserve, protect, or enhance an historical site or area; acquire an historical area or site by direct purchase, contract, lease, trade, or gift; obtain an easement or right-of-way across public or private property to ensure access or proper development of an historical area or site; protect an historical area or site; ensure proper development and utilization of land or an area adjacent to an historical area or site; and enter into an agreement with a private individual for the right to purchase an historical area or site if and when the private individual elects to sell or dispose of the owner's property.


===== 2.06.020 Historic Preservation Commission =====
An Historic Preservation Commission is hereby established with the following provisions:
 The Commission shall consist of seven (7) members with a demonstrated interest, competence, or knowledge in historic preservation. Each Commission member shall be appointed for a term of four (4) years by the County Commission. Two of the Commission members shall be professionals, as defined by National Park Service regulations, from the disciplines of history, architecture, or architectural history. The Commission will meet at least two (2) times each year and shall conduct business in accordance with the Open Public Meeting laws of the State of Utah, including public notification of the meeting place, time, and agenda items. The Commission shall be authorized to apply for matching grants for local historic preservation projects after consultation with and approval by the County Commission. The Commission shall not be authorized to expend or encumber any County funds without prior and specific approval by the County Commission. Written minutes of each Commission meeting will be prepared and made available for public inspection.


===== 2.06.030 Historic Preservation Commission Duties =====
The Historic Preservation Commission shall conduct or cause to be conducted a survey of the historic, architectural, and archeological resources within the County. The survey shall be compatible with the Utah Inventory of Historic and Archeological Sites. Survey and inventory documents shall be maintained and open to the public. The survey will be updated at least every ten (10) years. The Historic Preservation Commission shall review and comment to the State Historic Preservation Officer on all proposed National Register nominations for properties within the County. When the Historic Preservation Commission considers a National Register nomination which is normally evaluated by professionals in a specific discipline, and that discipline is not represented on the Commission, the Commission will seek expertise in this area before rendering its decision. The Historic Preservation Commission shall act in an advisory role to other officials and departments of government regarding the identification and protection of local historic and archeological resources. The Historic Preservation Commission shall also work toward the continuing education of citizens regarding historic preservation and the County's history. The Historic Preservation Commission shall support the enforcement of all State laws relating to historic preservation. These include, but are not limited to: Utah Code §§ 9-8-305, 9-8-307, and 9-8-308 regarding the protection of Utah antiquities; and Utah Code § 9-8-404 regarding notification of the State Historic Preservation Office of any known proposed action which will destroy or affect a site, building, or object owned by the State of Utah and included on or eligible for the State or National Registers.


===== 2.07 Campaign Financial Disclosure =====
2.07.010 Definitions 2.07.020 Campaign Financial Disclosure 2.07.030 Penalties


===== 2.07.010 Definitions =====
As used in this chapter:
 "Contribution" "Contribution" means any of the following when done for political purposes: a gift, subscription, donation, loan, advance, or deposit of money or anything of value given to the filing entity; an express, legally enforceable contract, promise, or agreement to make a gift, subscription, donation, unpaid or partially unpaid loan, advance, or deposit of money or anything of value to the filing entity; any transfer of funds from another reporting entity, candidate, or a corporation to the filing entity; compensation paid by any person or reporting entity other than the filing entity for personal services provided without charge to the filing entity; and goods or services provided to or for the benefit of the filing entity at less than fair market value. "Contribution" does not include: services provided without compensation by individuals volunteering a portion or all of their time on behalf of the filing entity; or money lent to the filing entity by a financial institution in the ordinary course of business. "Expenditure" means: any disbursement from contributions, receipts, or from a separate bank account required by State law; a purchase, payment, donation, distribution, loan, advance, deposit, gift of money, or anything of value made for political purposes; an express, legally enforceable contract, promise, or agreement to make any purchase, payment, donation, distribution, loan, advance, deposit, gift of money, or anything of value for political purposes; compensation paid by a corporation or filing entity for personal services rendered by a person without charge to a reporting entity; a transfer of funds between the filing entity and a candidate's personal campaign committee; or goods or services provided by the filing entity to or for the benefit of another reporting entity for political purposes at less than fair market value.
 "Expenditure" does not include: services provided without compensation by individuals volunteering a portion or all of their time on behalf of a reporting entity; or money lent to a reporting entity by a financial institution in the ordinary course of business.
 "Filing entity" means the reporting entity that is filing a report required by this chapter.


===== 2.07.020 Campaign Financial Disclosure =====
Each candidate for County office shall file signed campaign financial statements with the County Clerk reporting his or her itemized and total campaign contributions and expenditures at the following times: candidates who withdraw or who are eliminated at any time prior to the date of the primary election shall file a signed campaign financial statement containing the information required by this section no later than thirty (30) days after withdrawing or being eliminated; candidates for elective office in which a primary election is held shall file a signed campaign financial statement containing the information required by this section seven (7) days before the date of the primary election, reporting contributions and expenditures as of ten (10) days before the date of the primary election; candidates for elective office who are eliminated at a primary election shall file a signed campaign financial statement containing the information required by this section not later than thirty (30) days after the primary election; candidates for elective office at the general election shall file a signed campaign financial statement containing the information required by this section seven (7) days before the date of the general election, reporting contributions and expenditures as of ten (10) days before the date of the general election; and candidates for elective office at the general election shall file a signed campaign financial statement containing the information required by this section not later than thirty (30) days after the date of the general election. The statement filed seven (7) days before the primary election and the statement filed seven (7) days before the general election shall include: a list of each contribution of more than fifty dollars ($50) received by the candidate as of ten (10) days before the election, and the name of the donor; an aggregate total of all contributions of fifty dollars ($50) or less received by the candidate as of ten (10) days before the election; and a list of each expenditure for political purposes made during the reporting period, and the recipient of each expenditure.
 The statement filed thirty (30) days after the primary election and the statement filed thirty (30) days after the general election shall include: a list of each contribution of more than fifty dollars ($50) received after the cutoff date for the prior statement, and the name of the donor; an aggregate total of all contributions of fifty dollars ($50) or less received by the candidate after the cutoff date for the prior statement; and a list of each expenditure for political purposes made by the candidate after the cutoff date for the prior statement, and the recipient of each expenditure.
 HISTORY Amended by Ord. Default on 10/12/2022


===== 2.07.030 Penalties =====
Any person who fails to comply with the requirements of this chapter shall be guilty of an infraction.


===== 2.08 The Indigent Dead =====
2.08.010 Purpose Of The Provisions 2.08.020 Cremation Services - Designated 2.08.030 Attendance Of Services And Disposal Of Remains 2.08.040 Objections To Cremation


===== 2.08.010 Purpose Of The Provisions =====
The state has recognized that the proper disposition of human remains is a proper county function as set forth in Utah Code § 17-63-701. This chapter recognizes that the prompt and proper disposition of human remains implicates issues of public health, the investigation and prosecution of homicides, the decreasing availability of burial plots and society’s general acceptance of cremation as a proper and dignified disposition of human remains.


===== 2.08.020 Cremation Services - Designated =====
The remains of indigent persons who die within the county shall be cremated; provided, however, that the remains of indigent homicide victims may, at the County’s discretion, be buried in a plot designated by the County. The County’s obligation for the cremation of any deceased indigent person’s remains shall not include the costs associated with any service of memorial.


===== 2.08.030 Attendance Of Services And Disposal Of Remains =====
The friends and family of a deceased indigent person shall have the right to attend and witness cremation services held under this chapter. If any surviving family member of a deceased indigent person so desires, cremated remains shall be deposited in an urn or other suitable container and custody thereof given to that family member. If a deceased indigent person has no known surviving family, or if family members decline custody of cremated remains, such remains shall be disposed of at the discretion of the mortuary responsible for cremation and such disposal shall be performed with all due respect for the dead.


===== 2.08.040 Objections To Cremation =====
If a deceased indigent person’s surviving spouse, sibling, parent or direct descendant objects to disposition by cremation before the deceased indigent person is cremated, the County may provide a payment equal to the cost of cremation toward the burial or other disposition of that deceased indigent person. If no objection is made pursuant to this section or if a deceased indigent person has no known surviving family, said deceased indigent person shall be disposed of by cremation. This will satisfy in full the statutory requirements of Utah Code § 17-63-701.


===== 2.09 Contact By And With Individual County Commissioners Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings =====
2.09.010 Purpose 2.09.020 Definitions 2.09.030 Goals 2.09.040 Permitted Contact Between Individual Commissioners And Constituents Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings 2.09.050 Prohibited Contact Between Individual Commissioners And Others Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings 2.09.060 Penalty For Violation


===== 2.09.010 Purpose =====
The governmental entity of Utah County can only act upon a majority vote of the County Commission. A commissioner has no authority to individually represent the position of the County, or to bind the County, without the prior authority of the County Commission. Communications between individual commissioners and parties to litigation affecting the County could cause detrimental results to the County in the litigation. In matters in which the County Commission sits as a quasi-judicial body, it is imperative that the County Commission remain impartial and neutral to both sides of an issue.


===== 2.09.020 Definitions =====
As used in this chapter:
 “Ex parte contact” means contact by one side only of a matter before the County Commission, when the County Commission is acting in its quasi-judicial capacity.
 “Legislative capacity” means that role of the County Commission involved with the: framing and enactment of laws for the County, including the adoption of or amendments to this Code; adoption of or amendments to the Utah County General Plan; adoption of or amendments to the Utah County Land Use Ordinance ; and rezoning of properties. “Litigant” means one engaged in litigation. “Litigation” means a judicial controversy or dispute, either civil or criminal, involving the County as a party thereto, including: disputes where the County or County employees have been named as defendants in a civil suit; disputes where a claimant has served a notice of claim on the County; disputes where individuals or entities have filed an application before a County Appeal Authority, or are appealing a decision of a County Appeal Authority; or matters where the County has filed a civil complaint or criminal action against an individual or entity for violation of provisions of this Code, the Utah County Land Use Ordinance , or the Utah County Health Code. “Quasi-Judicial capacity” means that role of the County Commission where it acts to investigate facts, and draw conclusions therefrom, as a basis for its official actions, and to exercise discretion of a judicial nature, including matters in which the County Commission: acts in its capacity as the County Board of Equalization; acts as an appellate body; considers a request for approval of a large scale development, as defined by the Utah County Land Use Ordinance ; considers a conditional use permit request; or considers an exception to the Utah County Land Use Ordinance requiring its approval.


===== 2.09.030 Goals =====
To permit contact between individual commissioners and constituents in matters in which the County Commission is acting in its legislative capacity. To prohibit contact between individual commissioners and litigants in litigation by or against the County. To prohibit ex parte contact between individual commissioners and proponents or opponents in matters in which the County Commission is acting in its quasi-judicial capacity.


===== 2.09.040 Permitted Contact Between Individual Commissioners And Constituents Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings =====
Legislative capacity matters : Contact between individual commissioners and constituents is permitted when the contact regards matters involving the legislative capacity of the County Commission.


===== 2.09.050 Prohibited Contact Between Individual Commissioners And Others Outside Regularly Scheduled County Commission Meetings =====
Litigation matters : Contact between individual commissioners and litigants, counsel for litigants, any representative of litigants, or other implied representative of litigants involved in litigation with the County shall be prohibited outside regularly scheduled meetings of the County Commission. Individual commissioners are prohibited from participating in site or office visits, written communication, electronic communication, or verbal conversation either face-to-face or over the telephone, with any litigant, counsel for a litigant, any representative of a litigant, or other implied representative of a litigant involved in litigation with the County. In actions filed by the County, such as violation of this Code, the Utah County Land Use Ordinance , or the Utah County Health Code, this prohibition shall begin when the matter is referred to the County Attorney’s Office. In actions against the County, this prohibition shall begin when it becomes apparent that litigation is imminent against the County, when a notice of claim is served, or when a complaint is filed, whichever occurs first. In matters before a County Appeal Authority, this prohibition shall begin when an application is filed with the Appeal Authority. If a litigant, counsel for a litigant, representative of a litigant, or other implied representative of a litigant initiates contact with an individual commissioner, the commissioner shall immediately inform the initiator of the contact of the prohibition of such contact and shall immediately terminate the contact. The commissioner shall inform the County Commission of the attempted contact at the next meeting of the County Commission or shall document such contact in a written memorandum to the other commissioners and the County Attorney’s Office in a timely fashion. Quasi-Judicial capacity matters : Contact between individual commissioners and others outside a regularly scheduled County Commission meeting regarding matters involving the quasi-judicial capacity of the County Commission shall be considered ex parte contact and shall be prohibited. Individual commissioners are prohibited from participating in site or office visits, written communication, electronic communication, or verbal conversation either face-to-face or over the telephone, with any individual or representative of a company or entity when such contact involves the quasi- judicial capacity of the County Commission. In matters involving the County Board of Equalization, this prohibition shall begin when an application or appeal is filed with the County Board of Equalization. In matters involving a large-scale development approval request, conditional use permit request or an exception to the Utah County Land Use Ordinance requiring County Commission approval, this prohibition shall begin when an application is filed with the County Community Development Department. This prohibition shall apply to both proponents and opponents of a matter involving the quasi-judicial capacity of the County Commission. If an individual involved in a quasi-judicial capacity matter initiates contact with an individual commissioner, the commissioner shall immediately inform the initiator of the contact of the prohibition of such contact and shall terminate the contact. The commissioner shall inform the County Attorney’s Office of such contact by memorandum in a timely fashion. Written material provided by one side in a quasi-judicial capacity matter should be made a part of the record of the County Commission.


===== 2.09.060 Penalty For Violation =====
A commissioner in violation of this chapter or any part thereof shall be guilty of an infraction and upon conviction thereof may be sentenced to pay a fine not to exceed two hundred and fifty dollars ($250.00).


===== 2.10 Rules Of Procedure For The Operation Of The County Commission And Other County Offices And Departments =====
2.10.010 Purpose 2.10.020 Commission Meeting Agendas 2.10.030 Regular Meetings Of The County Commission To Be Held At County Seat 2.10.030 General Rules Of Procedure 2.10.040 Authority Of Commission Chair 2.10.050 Authority Of Commissioners 2.10.060 Commission Decorum 2.10.070 Commission Administration 2.10.080 Procedures Of Other County Offices And Departments


===== 2.10.010 Purpose =====
The County Commission has the responsibility of managing the governmental and business affairs of the County and does so through various capacities of the County Commission, including its legislative capacity, its executive or administrative capacity, and its quasi-judicial capacity. Procedural rules for the operation of the County Commission are necessary to allow for the proper and professional management of the governmental business affairs of the County.


===== 2.10.020 Commission Meeting Agendas =====
To the extent practicable, all action or information items desired to be included on the agenda of a County Commission meeting are to be filed on the County agenda system by 11:59 p.m. on the Wednesday before the County Commission meeting. The County Commission Chair is responsible for organizing the agenda. Any County Commissioner may add an item to the agenda. At the County Commission meeting, any County Commissioner may request that an agenda item be added to or removed from the consent agenda portion of the meeting agenda. The items moved to the consent agenda shall be approved by consent if no County Commissioner in attendance at the meeting objects to the action. No separate vote is required. If any County Commissioner objects to the approval by consent of any agenda item, such agenda item shall be removed from the consent agenda and remain on the regular agenda. Approval of the consent agenda constitutes approval of each of the items on the consent agenda. The agenda shall be available to the public. Backup materials shall, as a general policy, be made available to the public, but only to the extent practicable and taking into consideration the Government Records Access Management Act, privacy laws, personal privacy interests, the best interests of the County government, and other considerations. The backup materials made available to the public shall contain a clear indication that the County Commission has not yet approved the agenda item.
 HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-120 Updated Commission Meeting Agendas on 1/29/2024 Amended by Ord. 2024-800 Updated Commission Meeting Agendas on 9/18/2024


===== 2.10.030 Regular Meetings Of The County Commission To Be Held At County Seat =====
In accordance with Utah Code §§ 17-64-301 and 52-4-202, notice is hereby given that the County Commission will hold its regular meetings at the county seat of Utah County on those dates and times designated in the annual meeting schedule, unless otherwise changed by action of a quorum of the County Commission. All regular meetings of the County Commission will be held in Room 1400 of the County Administration Building, 100 East Center Street, Provo, Utah, unless otherwise changed by action of a quorum of the County Commission.


===== 2.10.030 General Rules Of Procedure =====
Parliamentary rules for the County Commission are to be governed by Robert’s Rules of Order recognizing the basic tenets and principles of parliamentary procedure: courtesy to all, justice to all, one item at a time, the rule of the majority, the rights of the minority, and partiality to none. A County Commission meeting is adjourned only after being declared adjourned by the Chair. Public comment shall generally be limited to three minutes per individual and should not be redundant. The three-minute limit may be modified or waived by the Chair or a majority of the County Commission.


===== 2.10.040 Authority Of Commission Chair =====
The Chair of the County Commission may call another commissioner to order if the other commissioner’s remarks are not relevant to the subject under discussion or for lack of decorum by the other commissioner. The Chair is obligated to vote or abstain with explanation on each motion, except as otherwise provided for by state law. The Chair may discuss a motion just as any other commissioner without relinquishing the position of Chair. In the event of a tie vote of the County Commission, the Chair shall rule that the motion is lost. The Chair may call a recess for a definite period if no objection is raised. If an objection is raised, the Chair is obligated to call for a motion.


===== 2.10.050 Authority Of Commissioners =====
Commissioners must vote or abstain with explanation on every motion, unless otherwise provided by state law. Each commissioner may be limited to a specific period of time by the Chair for discussion of each subject. This time limitation may be automatically extended if there is no objection thereto. If an objection is made, a motion to extend time must be made, seconded, and approved by a two-thirds vote of the County Commission. Commissioners may appeal from a decision of the Chair without a second and said appeal shall be decided by a majority vote of the County Commission.


===== 2.10.060 Commission Decorum =====
Any commissioner who receives a request for a dignitary function on behalf of the County shall forward the request to the other commissioners within a reasonable time, and in a reasonable manner. The Chair is generally the appropriate representative for dignitary functions. Any commissioner who receives a press inquiry involving County business shall notify the other commissioners of the nature of the request and of the general substance of the response to the inquiry, within a reasonable time, and in a reasonable manner. If the County Commission has not officially adopted a position regarding an issue, an individual commissioner is prohibited from representing or implying what the position of the County is regarding any such issue. An individual commissioner cannot bind the County to any position, obligation, or liability, without the prior express authorization of the County Commission. Press releases generated by one or more commissioners stating or implying a position of the County regarding any issue must be approved by official action of the County Commission prior to release. A commissioner may not use County employees or County resources to further the individual commissioner's proposals involving County business unless authorized by a majority of the County Commission. However, requests for existing departmental information, use of County Commission staff, and use of the County Attorney's Office to assist in drafting proposals and legislation are permissible. An individual commissioner is prohibited from waiving or modifying any policy, procedure, or fee established by the County Commission. Any such waiver or modification must be approved by official action of the County Commission.


===== 2.10.070 Commission Administration =====
The County Commission shall annually assign administrative responsibility for the various County departments to each of the individual commissioners in the form of “portfolio” management assignments. The responsibility of a portfolio assignment for a commissioner for a given department includes: periodic attendance of the commissioner at portfolio department staff meetings; review by the commissioner of agenda requests by a portfolio department for completeness and for recommendation of session placement on the County Commission agenda. All complete agenda requests which require action by the County Commission must be placed on the County Commission agenda; become familiar with portfolio departmental staffing requirements; review and approval by the commissioner of purchase orders and expenditures for each portfolio department; review and assistance by the commissioner in annual budget preparation and adjustments for each portfolio department; and review and assistance by the commissioner in resolution of citizen inquiries and complaints for each portfolio department. All citizen inquiries and complaints shall be forwarded to the commissioner who has the department involved with the citizen inquiry or complaint in the commissioner’s portfolio.


===== 2.10.080 Procedures Of Other County Offices And Departments =====
Other County offices and departments may not issue press releases, stating or implying the official position of the County Commission, without prior County Commission approval. Department policies and procedures manuals may be adopted to address internal department operations. For departments supervised by an appointed or merit employee department head, departmental policies and procedures and subsequent modifications will have effect after approval by the Director of Human Resources and the County Commission after legal review by the County Attorney. For departments supervised by an elected department head, departmental policies and procedures and subsequent modifications must be approved as follows, after legal review by the County Attorney: policies and procedures concerning human resource matters will have effect after approval by the Director of Human Resource and the County Commission; policies and procedures concerning matters of general county administrative ordinances, rules or policies or the legislative powers, duties or functions of the County will have effect after approval by the County Commission; and policies which pertain to the performance of the elected officer’s functions, duties, and responsibilities specifically provided for by law may be established by the elected department head consistent with existing law. Any department policies and procedures which conflict with the Utah County Office of Human Resource Rules and Regulations, which concern human resource matters addressed in the Utah County Office of Human Resource Rules and Regulations, or which are assigned to the Director of Human Resources or the Office of Human Resources by the County Personnel Management Act, Utah Code § 17-75-4 et seq., are hereby rescinded. The determination as to whether the department policy conflicts with Human Resource Rules and Regulations shall be made by the Director.


===== 2.11 Consolidated Of County Offices =====
2.11.010 Consolidation Of County Offices Of County Clerk And County Auditor 2.11.020 Separation Of Previously Consolidated County Offices Of County Recorder And County Surveyor
