===== 2.11.020 Separation Of Previously Consolidated County Offices Of County Recorder And County Surveyor =====
The duties, functions, and responsibilities of the previously consolidated offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor are hereby separated, with the separation to take effect at noon on January 3, 2011. The separation of the previously consolidated offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor shall create separate county offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor effective at noon on January 3, 2011, pursuant to the provisions of Utah Code § 17-66-104(3)(b). The separated office of the County Recorder shall perform the statutory duties of the County Recorder as enumerated in Utah Code Chapter 17- 71. The separated office of the County Surveyor shall perform the statutory duties of the County Surveyor as enumerated in Utah Code Chapter 17- 73. Pursuant to the provisions of Utah Code § 17-66-104, the separation of the previously consolidated county offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor shall take effect at noon on January 3, 2011, and one person shall be elected to the separated office of County Recorder and another person shall be elected to the separated office of County Surveyor, at the general election held on November 2, 2010. The elected County Recorder and the elected County Surveyor shall each be entitled to a separate annual salary as fixed by the County Commission. The term of office for the separated county offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor shall each be for a term of four
(4) years commencing at noon on January 3, 2011, with each succeeding term to be four (4) years thereafter. The separation of the previously consolidated county offices of the County Recorder and the County Surveyor shall not, in any way, affect the term of office of the currently elected County Recorder/Surveyor, which term shall expire at noon on January 3, 2011.


===== 2.12 Disaster Response And Management =====
2.12.010 Legislative Findings - Purpose 2.12.020 Definitions 2.12.030 Emergency Disaster Council Created 2.12.040 Local Emergency Declaration 2.12.050 Political Subdivisions Of The County - Declaration Of A "Local Emergency Declaration" 2.12.060 Authority Of The County Commission 2.12.070 Orders, Rules, And Regulations Having The Force Of Law 2.12.080 Acquisition Of Property For Public Use 2.12.090 Mutual Aid Agreements


===== 2.12.010 Legislative Findings - Purpose =====
The County Commission finds that existing and increasing threats of the occurrence of destructive disasters resulting from attack, internal disturbance, natural phenomena, or technological hazard could greatly affect the health, safety, and welfare of the people of this county, and it is therefore necessary to grant to the County Commission special emergency disaster authority. Pursuant to Utah Code §§ 53-2a-203 and 53-2a-213, the County Commission is authorized to carry out the measures as may be ordered by the governor under Utah Code § 53-2a. It is the purpose of this ordinance to assist the County Commission to effectively provide emergency disaster response and recovery assistance to protect the lives and property of the people of this county.


===== 2.12.020 Definitions =====
"Attack" means a nuclear, conventional, biological, act of terrorism, or chemical warfare action against the United States of America, this state, or this County.

“Chair” means the Chair of the County Commission, or, if the Chair is unavailable or otherwise incapacitated, then the Vice Chair. 

"Disaster" an event that: causes, or threatens to cause, loss of life, human suffering, public or private property damage, or economic or social disruption resulting from attack, internal disturbance, natural phenomena, or technological hazard; and requires resources that are beyond the scope of local agencies in routine responses to emergencies and accidents and may be of a magnitude or involve unusual circumstances that require response by government, not-for-profit, or private entities. "Emergency Operations Plan" means the formal plan compiled by the County Sheriff providing emergency operations procedures to be used during a disaster.

"Exigent circumstances" means a significant change in circumstances following the expiration of a state of emergency declared in accordance with this chapter that: substantially increases the threat to public safety or health relative to the circumstances in existence when the state of emergency expired; poses an imminent threat to public safety or health; and was not known or foreseen and could not have been known or foreseen at the time the state of emergency expired.
 "Internal disturbance" means riot, prison break, disruptive terrorism, or strike.

"Local emergency" means a condition in any part of the County that requires County government emergency assistance to save lives and to protect property, public health, welfare, or safety in the event of a disaster, or to avoid or reduce the threat of a disaster. 

"Long-term state of emergency" means a state of emergency: that lasts longer than thirty (30) days; or declared to respond to exigent circumstances
 "Natural phenomenon" means any earthquake, tornado, storm, flood, landslide, avalanche, forest or range fire, drought, or epidemic.

"Technological hazard" means any hazardous materials accident, mine accident, train derailment, air crash, radiation incident, pollution, chemical weapons incident, structural fire, or explosion.

"Terrorism" means activities or the threat of activities that: involve acts dangerous to human life; are a violation of the criminal laws of the United States, the state of Utah, or this County; and to a reasonable person, would appear to be intended to: intimidate or coerce a civilian population; influence the policy of a government by intimidation or coercion; or affect the conduct of a government by mass destruction, assassination, or kidnapping.
 "Political subdivision" means a municipality, special service district, or local district.


===== 2.12.030 Emergency Disaster Council Created =====
An Emergency Disaster Council is created to provide advice to the County Commission on matters relating to local government emergency disaster response and recovery actions. The Emergency Disaster Council shall meet at the direction of the County Commission. The County Commission shall appoint a Director of Emergency Management who shall serve on the Emergency Disaster Council as chair of that council. The Emergency Disaster Council is comprised of the following: County Commissioners; County Sheriff; County Public Works Director; Local Health Officer; County Assessor; County Recorder; County Treasurer; County Attorney; County Clerk; County Auditor; and Others as appointed by the County Commission.


===== 2.12.040 Local Emergency Declaration =====
A local emergency may be declared by the Chair of the County Commission if the Chair finds a disaster has occurred or the occurrence or threat of a disaster is imminent in any area of the County in which County government response or recovery is required or assistance is required to supplement the response and recovery of any political subdivision of the County. The local emergency declaration shall not be continued or renewed for a period more than thirty (30) days except by or with the consent of the County Commission. All declarations or orders issued under this chapter shall include: the nature of the local emergency; the area or areas that are affected or threatened; the conditions creating such an emergency or those conditions allowing termination of the local emergency; the number or quantity of resources or assistance needed.
 Any order or proclamation declaring, continuing, or terminating a local emergency shall be filed promptly with the office of the County Clerk. When the Chair of the County Commission or the County Commission declares a disaster or emergency, the County's emergency response will operate as outlined in the County Emergency Operations Plan. Except as provided in Subsection (E), a state of emergency expires the earlier of: the day on which the Chair finds that: the threat or danger has passed; the disaster reduced to the extent that emergency conditions no longer exist; or the County no longer requires state government assistance to supplement the response and recovery efforts of the municipality or county; thirty (30) days after the day on which the Chair declares the state of emergency; or the day on which the County Commission terminates the state of emergency by majority vote. The County Commission may at any time terminate by majority vote a state of emergency declared by the Chair. The County Commission may by majority vote extend a state of emergency for a time period stated in the motion. If the County Commission extends a state of emergency in accordance with this subsection, the state of emergency expires on the date designated by the County Commission in the motion. Except as provided in Subsections (G), (H), and (I) after a state of emergency expires in accordance with this Subsection (D) and (E), the Chair may not declare a new state of emergency in response to the same disaster or occurrence as the expired state of emergency. After a state of emergency expires, the Chair may declare a new state of emergency in response to the same disaster or occurrence as the expired state of emergency, if the Chair finds that exigent circumstances exist. A state of emergency declared in accordance with Subsection (G) expires in accordance with Subsections (D) and (E). After a state of emergency declared in accordance with Subsection (G) expires, the Chair may not declare a new state of emergency in response to the same disaster or occurrence as the expired state of emergency, regardless of whether exigent circumstances exist.


===== 2.12.050 Political Subdivisions Of The County - Declaration Of A "Local Emergency Declaration" =====
A declaration of local emergency by any political subdivision of the County is official recognition that a disaster situation exists within the affected political subdivision and provides a legal basis for requesting and obtaining state or federal government disaster assistance. Notice of any local emergency declared by any political subdivision shall be promptly delivered to the County Sheriff, Emergency Services Division Commander, or any member of the County Commission. The County Commission may provide the requested assistance and resources based on an evaluation of the resources utilized by the County political subdivision, the sufficiency of the political subdivision’s emergency response plan and reserves, and the County’s available resources. If the County cannot provide such requested assistance, the County may declare a local emergency. Following a County local emergency declaration, the County Sheriff shall direct the request for assistance to the state and coordinate other local agency assistance, considering proximity to the emergency and available resources. During any disaster where local, state, or federal assistance is required, the County Sheriff shall direct and coordinate such assistance. All requests for outside assistance and resources shall be coordinated through the County Sheriff. If more than one municipality and/or the County declares a local emergency during the same period of time, a Unified Command Committee shall be formed. The Unified Command Committee shall include a representative from each of the affected jurisdictions. The representatives shall have the authority to make decisions for the jurisdiction they represent.


===== 2.12.060 Authority Of The County Commission =====
The County Commission, during a declared local emergency, is authorized and empowered to: enact laws, rules, and regulations or suspend any law, rule, or regulation when necessary to save human life and protect property; utilize all available resources as reasonably necessary to cope with the emergency; suspend the County rules and regulations regarding overtime restrictions and personnel use, allowing department heads to utilize personnel as necessary to answer the challenges of the emergency presented to each department;
 employ measures and give direction to County and local officials and agencies which are reasonable and necessary for the purpose of securing compliance with the provisions of this chapter, and with orders, rules, and regulations made pursuant to this chapter; in connection with evacuation suspend or limit the sale, dispensing, or transportation of alcoholic beverages, explosives, and combustibles or other hazardous materials; establish curfews for the protection of life and property in the affected areas; establish price freezes to prohibit the practice of price gouging for items in short supply or high demand in the affected area; establish the ration of critical supplies necessary to sustain life; The Chair of the County Commission, during a declared local emergency, is authorized and empowered to: approve and advise the evacuation of all or part of the population from a stricken or threatened area within the County, if necessary for the preservation of life; approve routes, modes of transportation, and destination in connection with an evacuation; control ingress and egress to and from a disaster area, the movement of persons within the area, and approve the occupancy or evacuation of premises in a disaster area; clear or remove debris or wreckage from publicly or privately owned land or water which may threaten public health or public safety; establish temporary shelters for the housing of evacuated persons; authorize the on-scene commander to approve expenditures of thirty thousand dollars ($30,000) or less. Nothing in this section shall be construed to be contrary to or limit the authority of the County as set forth in Utah Disaster Response and Recovery Act, Utah Code § 53-2a-2.


===== 2.12.070 Orders, Rules, And Regulations Having The Force Of Law =====
All orders, rules, and regulations promulgated by the County Commission shall have the full force and effect of law during the local emergency, provided a copy thereof is filed with the County Clerk if immediately practicable.


===== 2.12.080 Acquisition Of Property For Public Use =====
Upon proclamation of the "local emergency" the Board of County Commissioners may purchase or lease public or private property for public use including: food; medical supplies; clothing; shelter; means of transportation; fuels and oils; or buildings or land.
 Such purchases under authority of this section may be used for any purpose to meet the needs of an emergency, including its use to relieve want, distress, and disease.


===== 2.12.090 Mutual Aid Agreements =====
The County Commission is authorized to enter into mutual-aid compacts with other political subdivisions within the state of Utah concerning matters involving cooperative disaster response and recovery assistance support, consistent with Utah Disaster Response and Recovery Act, Utah Code § 53-2a-2.


===== 2.13 Emergency Interim Succession =====
2.13.010 Title 2.13.020 Definitions 2.13.030 Emergency Interim Succession Procedure


===== 2.13.010 Title =====
This chapter shall be known as the Emergency Interim Succession Act.


===== 2.13.020 Definitions =====
As used in this chapter, the following definitions shall be applicable:

"Absent" means physically not present or not able to be communicated with; does not include a person who can be reached by telephone, radio, or other communications methods.

"Emergency interim successor" means a person designated by the officer to exercise the powers and discharge the duties of the officer in the officer’s absence.


===== 2.13.030 Emergency Interim Succession Procedure =====
On January 1 of each year, each department head, with the exception of the County Commissioners, shall: designate three (3) qualified emergency interim successors from within the department who meet the constitutional qualifications for the office, if any; specify the order of their succession; provide a list of those designated successors to the County Sheriff; and have an emergency alert plan in place and provide a copy of the plan to the County Sheriff. If any department head is unavailable following a disaster or local emergency, a designated emergency interim successor shall exercise the powers and duties of the office according to the order of succession specified by the officer until: the County Commission appoints a successor to fill the position; a permanent successor is appointed or elected by law; the officer, or an emergency interim successor earlier in the order of succession becomes available to exercise or resume the powers of the office; Emergency interim successors shall exercise the powers of the office which they succeeded only when a disaster has occurred. Successors may only serve for thirty (30) days after the date of the local emergency declaration unless approved by the County Commission. Extension of emergency powers shall be by the authority of the County Commission and only during the time of the disaster and recovery operations. The Chair of the County Commission, or successor as set forth in Subsection D, shall settle all disputes concerning emergency interim successors. Decisions of the Chair or successor are final. Emergency succession of the Chair of the County Commission shall proceed as follows when disaster response is occurring or imminent: The Chair shall serve, if available. If the Chair is unavailable, the Vice-Chair shall serve as Chair. If neither the Chair nor the Vice-Chair is available, the remaining commissioner shall serve as Chair. If no commissioner is available, the County Sheriff shall serve as Chair of the County Commission.


===== 2.14 Professional Ethics And Conflict Of Interest =====
2.14.010 Purpose 2.14.020 Definitions 2.14.030 Prohibited Conflicts Of Interest 2.14.040 Exceptions To The Gifts And Honoraria Prohibitions 2.14.050 Public Contract And The Procurement Process 2.14.060 Representation Regarding Ethical Standards Clause In Contracts 2.14.070 Restricted And Non-Restricted Conflicts Of Interest - Disclosure Required 2.14.080 Disclosure Statement 2.14.090 Professional Code Of Ethics 2.14.100 Loss Of County Property Or Funds 2.14.110 Violations 2.14.120 Prohibition Of Filing A Complaint Immediately Before An Election


===== 2.14.010 Purpose =====
It is the intent of the County to require honest and ethical conduct by its officers, employees and volunteers. The effective operation of County government requires officers, employees and volunteers to be independent, impartial, and responsible to County government and its citizens. This Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, in conjunction with other County policies and State laws, will assist County officers, employees and volunteers in understanding their ethical obligations. The purpose of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance is to establish standards of conduct for County officers, employees, and volunteers where there are actual or potential conflicts of interest between their public duties and their private interests, and to promote honest and ethical conduct.


===== 2.14.020 Definitions =====
In addition to the definitions in Utah Code § 17-70-501 which are incorporated herein, the following terms are hereby defined for purposes of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance. “Gifts” means anything of value including a loan at a rate that is substantially less than a prevalent commercial rate, compensation for goods or services exceeding fair market value, goods or services provided for less than fair market value, gratuity, entertainment, hospitality or forbearance, unless consideration of equal or greater value is received. “Honoraria” means the offering or acceptance of perquisite, gift or anything of value for speaking, writing or participating in a meeting, convention, social event, meal or like gathering. “Household” means all persons who occupy a residence or dwelling with the officer, employee or volunteer for an entire year including family members (spouse, child, ward, parents, siblings, mother-in-law, father-in-law, grandparent, legal guardian, grandchild, adult designee, dependent child of an adult designee and step-relatives of the same order), relatives (uncle, aunt, nephew, niece, first cousin, brother-in-law, sister-in-law, son-in-law, daughter-in-law) and any unrelated persons. “Non-Restricted Conflict or Non-Restricted Conflict of Interest” means any and all other interests not considered a Restricted Conflict of Interest, as defined in this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, including political, family, fraternal, social, other interests or associations which may create the appearance or the actuality of a conflict of interest between an officer, employee, or volunteer’s outside interest and his or her County responsibilities, other conflicts as defined by state statute and any campaign contribution made to the officer, employee, or volunteer or to any member of his or her household, of more than two thousand dollars ($2,000.00) during the prior calendar year. “Purchasing Official” means the County Purchasing Manager and any subordinate County Purchasing Agents. “Restricted Conflict of Interest” means any direct outside, substantial interest held by the officer, employee, or volunteer, or by any member of his or her household. Outside public sector employment does not constitute a Restricted Conflict of Interest and is addressed in the Utah County Human Resource Rules and Regulations. “Substantial interest” means the ownership, either legally or equitably, by an individual, the individual’s spouse, and the individual’s minor children, of at least ten percent (10%) of the outstanding shares of a corporation or ten percent (10%) interest in any other business entity.


===== 2.14.030 Prohibited Conflicts Of Interest =====
Unless excepted under UCC 2.14.040, County officers, employees, and volunteers shall not: Accept employment or engage in any business or professional activity which may be reasonably expected to require or induce improper disclosure of controlled, private, or protected information gained through affiliation with the County. Disclose or improperly use controlled, private, or protected information acquired through affiliation with the County for the private gain or benefit of self or others. Use or attempt to use their official position to secure special privileges or exemptions for self or others. Engage in any outside activity, employment, or financial investment which constitutes a Restricted Conflict of Interest. Knowingly receive, accept, take, seek or solicit, directly or indirectly, any gift or economic benefit tantamount to a gift such as a loan at an interest rate that is substantially lower than the commercial rate then currently prevalent for similar loans; and compensation received for private services rendered at a pay rate substantially exceeding the fair market value of the services, if:
1. It may be reasonably expected to improperly influence an officer, employee, or volunteer to depart from the faithful and impartial discharge of their County duties;
2. The officer, employee, or volunteer knows, or a reasonable person in that position should know under the circumstances, it is primarily for the purpose of rewarding the officer, employee, or volunteer for an official action already taken; or
3. The officer, employee, or volunteer is now or in the near future may be involved in any governmental action directly affecting the donor or lender unless disclosures have been made as required under Section 2.14.080. Participate in vendor / customer incentive programs which place the officer, employee, or volunteer at risk of being viewed as either misappropriating County property or using their position to secure a privilege by virtue of the County position.
Accept an incentive from any retailer or vendor. If the officer, employee, or volunteer were to come into possession of such property, the property must be turned over to the County as soon as practicable and a record made of the fact it was turned over to the County. Accept Honoraria in regard to activities related to their County duties or purpose except as allowed in Section 2.14.040. The County may grant the requests of schools, universities, civic organizations, governmental entities, or private businesses to have County officers, employees, or volunteers give speeches or other presentations, teach or provide consultation services. Such services by employees must be authorized by their Department Head and shall be compensated as for other regularly assigned duties. Knowingly accept or solicit any gift for themselves, a relative, a household member or organizations of the officer, employee, volunteer or others, except as allowed in Section 2.14.040. Participate in an official capacity or receive compensation in respect to any transaction between the County and any business entity in which the officer, employee, volunteer or a member of their household is also an officer, director, employee or owns a substantial interest in the company without first filing a conflict of interest disclosure statement as outlined in Section 2.14.080. Have personal investments in any business entity which will create a substantial conflict between their private interests and public duties. Receive or agree to receive compensation for assisting any person or business entity in any transaction involving the County without first filing a conflict of interest disclosure statement as outlined in Section 2.14.080. Employ, appoint, or attempt to influence the appointment or hiring of a relative or household member to any County position or employment paid out of County funds, except as provided in the Utah County Human Resource Rules and Regulations. Directly supervise or evaluate for purposes of pay, benefits, promotion, or discipline a relative or household member in any County position or employment paid out of County funds, except as provided in the Utah County Human Resource Rules and Regulations.


===== 2.14.040 Exceptions To The Gifts And Honoraria Prohibitions =====
The following are exceptions to the gifts and honoraria prohibitions in Section 2.14.030. The gift is a political contribution authorized by law and reported as part of the campaign disclosure requirements of the County or any other governmental entity. Token items of nominal value, with a value less than one hundred dollars ($100.00), including, but not limited to educational materials, shirts, coffee mugs, parking validations or other commemorative or similar souvenir items. An occasional nonpecuniary gift having a value of less than fifty dollars ($50.00). Snacks, beverages, meals, entertainment, educational or informational materials provided at meetings or other functions. Attendance at conventions, seminars or events of a primarily educational nature, including meals and entertainment that are associated with the convention, seminar, or event, and any associated educational or informational materials related to the duties of the officer, employee, or volunteer. Gifts not related to the activities of the officer, employee, or volunteer with the County. Awards and retirement gifts publicly made for public service. Meals and entertainment given at a widely attended reception, meal, event, or meeting by an organization before whom the recipient appears to represent the County and attends, makes a speech, answer questions or participates in part of a program. Attendance at political events that are primarily sponsored by a political party or political candidate. Flowers, plants, balloons or similar tokens which are given to express condolences, congratulations or sympathy for ill health or to commemorate holiday or special occasions. County sponsored programs activities or work. Rewards or prizes given to competitors or entrants in a random drawing in which the County officer, employee, or volunteer is an entrant, including a drawing at an event attended by the County officer, employee, or volunteer where the County paid for their attendance. Discounts and waived membership fees if the discounts are available to a class consisting of all public officers, employees, or volunteers, or to a geographically defined class of public officers, employees, or volunteers.
 Gifts for the County that become the property of the County. Gifts to County officers, employees, or volunteers from other County officers, employees, or volunteers.
 Death transfers including bequests and inheritances. Gifts to blind trusts related to legal defense funds for imminent or pending litigation against officers, employees, or volunteers related to their official duties.


===== 2.14.050 Public Contract And The Procurement Process =====
No Purchasing Official may receive or solicit any gift or a request for employment from a vendor, seller or buyer of real property or service provider which is or anticipates doing business with the County. It is unlawful and punishable as provided by law for any payment, gift or offer of employment to be made by any person to a County officer, employee, volunteer, contractor or any household member of the County officer, employee or contractor of the County to obtain a specific procurement, disposal, contract or subcontract. No County officer, employee, volunteer, nor any person representing the County and receiving compensation therefrom shall travel inside or outside the state of Utah at the expense of any past, present, or future vendor, unless excepted below: Exceptions may be granted by the County Commission in a public meeting for good cause shown, such exceptions shall require: a written explanation justifying the exception; a written disclosure of all the facts associated with the matter; and a written request to the County Commission containing both a written explanation and the disclosure of all the facts. No exception will be allowed in any circumstance which would violate federal, state, or local laws. It shall be the duty of County officers, employees, and volunteers to be aware of federal, state, or local laws and not seek an exception which would violate any such laws. The County Commission may seek an opinion from the County Attorney’s Office whenever the County Commission is presented with a request for an exception. Any exception granted under this Section shall be considered a public document.


===== 2.14.060 Representation Regarding Ethical Standards Clause In Contracts =====
In every contract, bid, proposal, or other offer involving the County made by a nongovernmental entity that nongovernmental entity shall make the following representation:

REPRESENTATION REGARDING ETHICAL STANDARDS FOR COUNTY OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES

The bidder, offeror, vendor, or contractor represents that it has not (1) provided an illegal gift or payoff to a County officer or employee, or his or her relative or business entity; (2) retained any person to solicit or secure this agreement upon an understanding for a commission, percentage, or brokerage or contingent fee, other than bona fide employees or bona fide commercial selling agencies for the purpose of securing business; (3) knowingly breached any of the ethical set forth in the County’s Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, UCC 2.14; or (4) knowingly influenced, and hereby promises that it will not knowingly influence a County officer or employee to breach any of the ethical standards set forth in the County’s Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, UCC 2.14.
 The Purchasing Official, subject to review by the County Commission, may waive the representation requirement in this Section for certain state master contracts, bids, proposals, or other offers upon a written determination that it is in the best interest of the County to waive the representation requirement.


===== 2.14.070 Restricted And Non-Restricted Conflicts Of Interest - Disclosure Required =====
A County officer, employee, or volunteer must complete a disclosure statement as provided in UCC 2.14.080 under the following circumstances: Interest in a business entity regulated by the County or doing business with the County : A County officer, employee, and volunteer who is an officer, director, agent, employee or the owner of a substantial interest in any business entity which is subject to the regulation of the County, including licensure or which does or anticipates doing business with the County. Restricted Conflict of Interest : In addition to filing a disclosure form, the County officer, employee, or volunteer who has a restricted conflict of interest who is also a member of a County body shall publicly disclose such interest to the members of the body and are required to be recused from all discussions, deliberations, decision making or vote regarding the business entity. The disclosure statement shall be entered in the minutes of the meeting. Non-Restricted Conflict of Interest : In addition to filing a disclosure form, the County officer, employee, or volunteer who has a non-restricted conflict of interest who is also a member of a County body shall publicly disclose such interest to the members of the body immediately prior to any discussion and / or vote regarding the business entity. The disclosure statement shall be entered in the minutes of the meeting. Compensation for assistance in transaction involving the County : A County officer, employee, or volunteer who agrees to receive compensation for assisting any person or business entity in any transaction involving the County must disclose the information identified in UCC 2.14.080 at least ten (10) days prior to the date of any agreement between the officer, employee, or volunteer and the person or business entity being assisted or prior to the receipt of compensation by the County officer, employee, or volunteer, whichever is earlier. Interest creating conflict of interest with duties : A County officer, employee, or volunteer or a member of their household who has a personal interest or investment which creates a potential or actual conflict between their personal interests and public duties must disclose the information identified in UCC 2.14.080 and shall publicly disclose such interest to the members of the body immediately prior to discussion and/or vote regarding the conflict of interest and the nature of the conflict. The disclosure shall be entered in the minutes of the meeting.


===== 2.14.080 Disclosure Statement =====
A disclosure statement as required by the “County Officers and Employees Disclosure Act”, Utah Code Chapter 17-16a, and the “Utah Public Officers’ and Employees’ Ethics Act”, Utah Code Chapter 67-16, is a written and sworn document filed with the County Commission and is designated a public document. The disclosure statement shall provide the name and business address of the officer, the name and business address of the person or business entity being assisted and a brief description of the transaction or service provided, or the name and business address of the business in which the County officer, employee, or volunteer has a substantial interest, the position held and the precise nature and value of any interest. The disclosure statement shall be made upon first assisting the person or business, or upon becoming an officer or employee and in January of each year thereafter during which he or she continues to be an officer, director, agent, owner, volunteer, or employee. The disclosure statement must be filed at least ten (10) calendar days prior to the date of any agreement to provide assistance or receive compensation. County officers, employees, and volunteers are responsible for ensuring their disclosure statement is current and is filed with their department head and the County Commission.


===== 2.14.090 Professional Code Of Ethics =====
County officers, employees, and volunteers who work in occupations having professional codes of ethics or standards of professional responsibility shall adhere to those requirements in the performance of their County duties. Failure to abide by professional codes of ethics may adversely affect the employees' ability to perform their duties and may, in appropriate cases, result in disciplinary action or termination of County employment. Failure by a County officer, employee, or volunteer to meet the requirements of their professional or occupational licensing authority, resulting in loss of the license required to practice in such profession or occupation and the inability to continue to work in the County position, may be a basis for immediate termination of employment. County employees and volunteers shall have an ongoing obligation to report to their supervisor any actions taken by the licensing authority, including the facts giving rise to such action, which affects their right to continue to practice in that profession. Failure to report as required may be a basis for immediate termination of employment. County officers, employees, and volunteers are expected to comply with applicable County policies, ordinances and laws in their position with the County.


===== 2.14.100 Loss Of County Property Or Funds =====
Any County officer, employee, or volunteer, who is responsible for, has access to, or has been given possession of County property or funds shall immediately notify his or her department head of the loss of, or inability to account for, County property or funds. Any County officer, employee, or volunteer, who is aware of a loss of County property or funds shall also notify his or her department head. Upon discovery of a loss of County property or funds, the department head shall immediately notify the County Auditor and County Attorney.


===== 2.14.110 Violations =====
If a County employee is found to be in violation of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance and has had employment disciplinary action imposed as a result therefrom, then that employee may appeal that disciplinary action if allowed under the Utah County Human Resource Rules and Regulations. Any procurement, disposal, contract, subcontract, or other government action by the County which involved a violation of a provision of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance is voidable, at the option of the County and without returning any part of the consideration received by the County. Debarment or suspension of doing business in the future with the County may be imposed by the County on any bidder, offeror, vendor, or contractor for violations of the provisions of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, provided that they have had the right to notice and an opportunity for a hearing before the County Commission prior to the imposition of any debarment or suspension. In addition to any penalty allowed by law, any person who knowingly and intentionally violates the provisions of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance is guilty of a class B misdemeanor and may be terminated from employment or removed from office, as provided by law. Any person who induces or seeks to induce any County officer, employee, or volunteer to violate this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance is guilty of a class B misdemeanor. Any person who files a complaint against a County officer, employee, or volunteer pursuant to this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance, knowing that such complaint is frivolous, malicious, false, or is otherwise without merit, shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 2.14.120 Prohibition Of Filing A Complaint Immediately Before An Election =====
A complaint for violation of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance may not be filed during the sixty (60) calendar days immediately before a primary election, if the accused County officer or employee is a candidate in the primary election. A complaint for violation of this Professional Ethics and Conflict of Interest ordinance may not be filed during the sixty (60) calendar days immediately before a general election in which the accused County officer or employee is a candidate unless the accused County officer or employee is unopposed in the election.


===== 2.15 Setting Of Salaries Of Elected Officials =====
2.15.010 Method Of Calculation 2.15.020 Timing Of Reevaluation Of Salaries Of Elected Officials


===== 2.15.010 Method Of Calculation =====
The annual compensation of the elected officials of the County shall be set in accordance with state law. After any required public hearings, after due consideration of all input received at the public hearings and budgetary restrictions, and subject to the discretion and determination of the County Commission, the annual compensation of the elected officials of the County shall be determined by utilizing one or more of the following methods: Current market data comparing Utah County to the top four most populous counties in the State of Utah may be used as a basis for a salary recommendation proposed to the County Commission pursuant to UCC 2.1 5.020. The annual compensation of elected officials may be based upon the approximate ratio of the annual compensation of elected offices to the annual compensation of the county attorney as evidenced in current market data. An average of the annual market-based pay increases of all career service employees within Utah County Government may be used as a basis to support a proposal for an increase in the compensation for all elected officials.


===== 2.15.020 Timing Of Reevaluation Of Salaries Of Elected Officials =====
The annual compensation of the elected officials of the County should be evaluated annually and a recommendation made by the Director of Human Resources.


===== 2.16 County Legislative Body's Consent To The Appointment Of Deputies And Employees =====
2.16.010 Authority To Appoint Deputies And Employees 2.16.020 Method Of Providing Consent By The Legislative Body 2.16.030 Deputy Appointment Process, Liability Of Officer, Effect Of Vacancy, And Oath Of Office


===== 2.16.010 Authority To Appoint Deputies And Employees =====
Each elected County officer, except County Commissioners, may, with the consent of the County Commission, appoint deputies and employees as necessary for the discharge of the duties of the County officer's office.


===== 2.16.020 Method Of Providing Consent By The Legislative Body =====
The County Commission's consent to the appointment of deputies and employees by County officers shall be given by any of the following methods: the County budget approval process, which shall be the standard method of consent by the County Commission; 
 approval or disapproval of the hiring of individual applicants; approval of the County staffing plan; or approval of an allocation of a certain number of positions.


===== 2.16.030 Deputy Appointment Process, Liability Of Officer, Effect Of Vacancy, And Oath Of Office =====
Each elected County officer appointing a deputy shall, for each deputy appointed, file a signed writing with the County Clerk that memorializes the appointment.
 The powers of the deputy include the powers of the elected County officer unless limited by the deputy's written appointment, by office policy, or by job description. The elected County officer appointing the deputy is liable for all official acts of the deputy. If the office of the County officer who appointed the deputy becomes vacant, the deputy may continue to serve despite the vacancy. Each elected County officer appointing a deputy shall, for each deputy appointed, require that the deputy appear before the County Clerk, or a deputy Clerk, to take the oath of office, or shall file a signed writing with the County Clerk that memorializes the proper administration of the oath of office.


===== 2.17 Appeal Of Certain County Recorder Rule Implementations =====
2.17.010 Appeal Right 2.17.020 Notice Of Appeals 2.17.030 Appeal Details


===== 2.17.010 Appeal Right =====
County Recorder decisions may be appealed: by a person with standing that is adversely affected as a result of the Recorder's decision by alleging that the Recorder’s decision did not comply with rules made by the County Recorder Standards Board under Utah Code § 63C-30-202; and only if it is the final decision issued by the Recorder, as appeals of decisions made by supporting staff must be reviewed by the Recorder.


===== 2.17.020 Notice Of Appeals =====
Notices of Appeals must: commence within ten (10) business days of the adverse order, requirement, decision, or determination by filing a written notice of appeal with the County Recorder’s Office; identify the decision being appealed and parties making the appeal; and identify and attach a copy of the rule issued by the County Recorder Standards Board under Utah Code § 63C-30-202 alleged to be implemented incorrectly.


===== 2.17.030 Appeal Details =====
The appellant has the burden of showing the evidence and proving that the Recorder’s decision did not comply with rules made by the County Recorder Standards Board under Utah Code § 63C-30-202. The Appeal Authority shall be the County Commission. When a notice of appeal is filed, the Appeal Authority shall meet at the call of the Commission Chair and any other time the County Commission deems necessary and appropriate. The Appeal Authority shall maintain minutes and records of its proceedings in accordance with the requirements of state and local law. Nothing shall prohibit the Appeal Authority from meeting in conjunction with a regularly scheduled meeting of the County Commission or in conjunction with other meetings that involve the same individuals, such as a meeting of the Board of Equalization. The Appeal Authority may require written briefs or memorandum of the parties as the Appeal Authority deems necessary. At the hearing, the appellant must appear in person or by agent. Using substantial evidence as the standard of review, the Appeal Authority determines the correctness of a decision of the Recorder in its interpretation and application of rules made by the County Recorder Standards Board under Utah Code § 63C-30-202. Only those decisions in which the Recorder has applied rules made by the County Recorder Standards Board under Utah Code § 63C-30-202 may be appealed. The Appeal Authority must issue a decision in writing within fifteen (15) business days of the final hearing, which constitutes a final decision under Utah Code § 17-71-306. Any person adversely affected by a final decision of the Appeal Authority may petition the Fourth District Court for review of the decision as permitted by law. The Appeal Authority may order its decision stayed pending District Court review if the Appeal Authority finds it to be in the best interest of the County.
