===== 3.01 Transient Room Tax =====
3.01.010 Title Of Provisions 3.01.020 Purpose Of Provisions 3.01.030 Statutes Adopted By References 3.01.040 Tax Imposed 3.01.050 Exclusions 3.01.060 Convention Bureau Special Reserve Fund 3.01.070 Contributions And Donations Permitted 3.01.080 Audits 3.01.090 Remittance Tax 3.01.100 Appeals 3.01.110 Penalties And Interest 3.01.120 Repealer


===== 3.01.010 Title Of Provisions =====
The ordinance codified in this chapter shall be known as “The Transient Room Tax Ordinance of the County of Utah.”


===== 3.01.020 Purpose Of Provisions =====
The County Commission, as the Legislative Body of Utah County, Utah, declares that the ordinance codified in this chapter is adopted to achieve the following, among other purposes, and directs that the provisions hereof be interpreted to accomplish these purposes:
 to adopt a transient room tax ordinance that complies with the requirements and limitations contained in Utah Code Chapter 17-78 Part 7 and 59-12 Part 3; to adopt a transient room tax ordinance that incorporates provisions identical to those of Utah Code Chapter 17-78 Part 7 and 59-12 Part 3; to adopt a transient room tax ordinance that imposes a tax not to exceed four and one half percent (4.5%), and provide a measure therefor that can be administered by the County in a manner that adapts itself as fully as practical to the existing statutory and administrative procedures followed by the State Tax Commission in administering and collecting the sales and use taxes of the state; and to adopt a transient room tax ordinance that can be administered in a manner that will provide funds for the purposes of establishing, financing, and promoting recreation, tourism, film production and conventions, and such other purposes as have been authorized by law for the expenditure of transient room taxes and for that purpose create, at the discretion of the County, a reserve fund comprised of any funds collected but not expended during any financial year.


===== 3.01.030 Statutes Adopted By References =====
Except as hereinafter provided, and except insofar as they are inconsistent with the provisions of Utah Code Chapter 17-31 and Utah Code 59-12 Part 3, all of the provisions of Utah Code 59-12 Part 1, known as the Sales and Use Tax Act, and all of the provisions of Utah Code 59-12 Part 2, known as the Local Sales and Use Tax Law of Utah, are hereby adopted and made a part of this chapter as though fully set forth herein to the extent the said provisions are relevant and pertinent to the administration of taxes by the County.


===== 3.01.040 Tax Imposed =====
There is hereby levied on all persons, companies, corporations, or other like and similar persons, groups or organizations doing business in the County as motor courts, tourist homes, motels, hotels, inns, trailer courts or like and similar public accommodations a transient room tax at the rate of four and one half percent (4.5%) of the amounts paid or charged for short-term rentals of tourist home, hotel, motel, or trailer court accommodations and services. Wherever and to the extent that in Utah Code 59-12 Part 1 the State of Utah is named or referred to as the taxing agency, the name of the County shall be substituted therefor. Nothing in this subsection shall be deemed to require substitution of the name of the County for the word “state” when that word is used as part of the title of the State Tax Commission, or of the Constitution of the State of Utah. If an annual license has been issued to a retailer under Utah Code § 59-12-106, an additional license shall not be required by reason of this section but a copy of such license shall be provided to the County within thirty (30) days after the effective date of this ordinance.


===== 3.01.050 Exclusions =====
There shall be excluded from the rent paid or charged by which the tax is measured: the amount of any sales or use tax imposed by the state or by any other governmental agency upon a retailer or consumer; and receipts from the sale or service charge for any food or beverage or room service charges in conjunction with the occupancy of the suite, room, or rooms.


===== 3.01.060 Convention Bureau Special Reserve Fund =====
For the purposes authorized by this chapter, there is hereby created a reserve fund, to be known as the “Convention Bureau Special Reserve Fund,” which shall be maintained separate and apart from general and other special funds of the County, and in which shall be deposited any and all funds received by virtue of the tax imposed hereby but not expended during the fiscal year in which they were received or in any other subsequent fiscal year.


===== 3.01.070 Contributions And Donations Permitted =====
The County Commission is authorized to accept, on behalf of the County, funds contributed, donated, or supplied by any person, corporation, other governmental agency, or from any other source whatever for the purposes outlined in UCC 3.01.020, and, when such funds are received, they shall be deposited and used in the same manner as though they were derived from the tax imposed hereby.


===== 3.01.080 Audits =====
Any records, tax returns, or other information of any person, corporation, company, or other group or organization subject to the taxes imposed by this chapter which relate to the calculations, collection, or remittance to the County of said taxes shall be subject to review, inspection, and audit by the County or its agent to the extent allowed by Utah Code § 59-12-209. The records of the State Tax Commission relating to the collection of sales and use taxes, or Tourism, Recreation, and Convention Center taxes on the same transactions which are the subject of this tax shall be subject to review and audit as provided in the County’s contract with the State Tax Commission for the collection of the local sales and use tax and as provided by law. The taxpayer shall also be subject to such audits and reviews by the Utah State Tax Commission as are provided for by law.


===== 3.01.090 Remittance Tax =====
The tax shall be collected by the State Tax Commission pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-302 and remitted to the County as provided therein.


===== 3.01.100 Appeals =====
Any party aggrieved by any action of the County relating to the assessment, auditing, calculation, or collection of the tax, including any Notice of Deficiency issued by the County, may request a hearing by filing a written Request for Redetermination and Hearing with the County Auditor no later than thirty (30) days after the effective date of the County’s action or the date of issuance of the Notice of Deficiency. The party shall have any additional right of administrative appeal to the authorized under Utah State Code.


===== 3.01.110 Penalties And Interest =====
Any person who fails to file any tax return or information required by this chapter, who fails to pay any tax due hereunder, or who fails to timely pay such tax shall be subject to the imposition of penalties and interest by the County in accordance with Utah Code §§ 59-1-401 and 59-1-402 or any successor provision thereto.


===== 3.01.120 Repealer =====
All existing ordinances of the County imposing a transient room tax are hereby repealed as of the effective date of this chapter.


===== 3.02 Sales And Use Tax =====
3.02.010 Title 3.02.020 Purpose 3.02.030 Effective Date 3.02.040 Sales And Use Tax 3.02.050 Penalties


===== 3.02.010 Title =====
This ordinance enacted in this chapter shall be known as the "Sales and Use Tax Ordinance of the County of Utah."


===== 3.02.020 Purpose =====
The 48th Session of the Utah Legislature authorized the counties and municipalities of the State of Utah to enact sales and use tax ordinances imposing a one percent (1%) tax. It is the purpose of this chapter to conform the Sales and Use Tax of the County to the requirements of the Sales and Use Tax Act, Utah Code Chapter 59-12.


===== 3.02.030 Effective Date =====
This chapter shall be effective as of 12:01 a.m., January 1, 1990.


===== 3.02.040 Sales And Use Tax =====
From and after the effective date of this chapter, there is levied and there shall be collected and paid a tax upon every retail sale of tangible personal property, services, and meals made within the County at the rate of one percent (1%). An excise tax is hereby imposed on the storage, use or other consumption in this County of tangible personal property from any retailer on or after the operative date of this chapter at the rate of one percent of the sales price of the property. For the purpose of this chapter all retail sales shall be presumed to have been consummated at the place of business or delivered by the retailer or the retailer’s agent to an out-of-state destination or to a common carrier for delivery to an out-of-state destination. In the event a retailer has no permanent place of business, the place or places at which the retail sales are consummated shall be as determined under the rules and regulations prescribed and adopted by the State Tax Commission. Public utilities as defined by Utah Code Title 54 shall not be obligated to determine the place or places within any county or municipality where public utilities services are rendered, but the place of sale or the sales tax revenue arising from such service allocable to the County shall be as determined by the State Tax Commission pursuant to an appropriate formula and other rules and regulations to be prescribed and adopted by it. Except as hereinafter provided, and except insofar as they are inconsistent with the provisions of the Sales and Use Tax Act, all of the provisions of Utah Code Chapter 59-12 and in force and effect on the effective date of this chapter, insofar as they relate to sales taxes, excepting Utah Code § 59-12-101 thereof, are hereby adopted and made a part of this chapter as though fully set forth herein. Wherever, and to the extent that in Utah Code Chapter 59-12 the State of Utah is named or referred to as the taxing agency, the name of this County shall be substituted therefor. Nothing in this Subsection B shall be deemed to require substitution of the name of the County for the word "State" when that word is used as part of the title of the State Tax Commission or of the Constitution of the State of Utah, nor shall the name of the County be substituted for that of the State in any section when the result of that substitution would require action to be taken by or against the County or any agency thereof, rather than by or against the State Tax Commission in performing the functions incident to the administration or operation of the chapter. If an annual license has been issued to a retailer under Utah Code § 59-12-106, an additional license shall not be required by reason of this section. There shall be excluded from the purchase price paid or charged by which the tax is measured: the amount of any sales or use tax imposed by the State of Utah upon a retailer or consumer; and the gross receipts from the sale of or the cost of storage, use, or other consumption of tangible personal property upon which a sales or use tax has become due by reason of the sale transaction to any municipality and any other county in the State of Utah, under the sales and use tax ordinance enacted by that county or municipality in accordance with the Sales and Use Tax Act.


===== 3.02.050 Penalties =====
Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a class B misdemeanor and upon conviction thereof shall be punishable by a fine in an amount not more than one thousand dollars ($1,000.00), imprisonment for a period of not more than six (6) months, or by both such fine and imprisonment. Any violation shall also be subject to the imposition of penalties and interest by the County in accordance with Utah Code § 59-1-401 or any successor provision thereto.


===== 3.03 Tourism, Recreation, Cultural And Convention Facilities Tax =====
3.03.010 Provisions 3.03.020 Statutory Authority 3.03.030 Purpose Of Provisions 3.03.040 Definitions 3.03.050 Imposition - Amount 3.03.060 Use Of Revenues 3.03.070 Collection 3.03.080 Licensure


===== 3.03.010 Provisions =====
This chapter shall be known as the Tourism, Recreation, Cultural, and Convention Facilities Tax of the County of Utah.


===== 3.03.020 Statutory Authority =====
The authority for imposing this tax is derived from Utah Code § 59-12-601 et seq.


===== 3.03.030 Purpose Of Provisions =====
This chapter is reenacted to provide the County with a source of revenue specifically for the purposes of financing in whole or in part tourism promotion, and the development, operation, and maintenance of tourist, recreation, cultural, convention and airport facilities, and the County Commission hereby directs that the provisions hereof be interpreted and construed to accomplish the stated purpose.


===== 3.03.040 Definitions =====
As used in this chapter, all terms shall have the same definitions and meanings as terms defined in Utah Code § 59-12-602 et seq.


===== 3.03.050 Imposition - Amount =====
There is hereby levied a tourism, recreation, cultural, convention, and airport facilities tax on all restaurants, as defined herein, in the County at the rate of one percent (1%) of all sales of prepared food and beverages, or food and food ingredients that are sold by restaurants, pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-603 (1) (a)(iii). There is hereby levied a tourism, recreation, cultural, convention, and airport facilities tax on all short-term leases and rentals of motor vehicles in the County not exceeding thirty (30) days, except for leases and rentals of motor vehicles made for the purpose of temporarily replacing a person’s motor vehicle that is repaired pursuant to a repair or an insurance agreement, at the rate of seven percent (7%), pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-603(1)(a)(ii). Except as hereinafter provided, and except insofar as they are inconsistent with the provisions of the Tourism, Recreation, Cultural, Convention, and Airport Facilities Tax Act, all the provisions of Utah Code 59-12 Part 1 which are in force and effect on the effective date of this chapter, are hereby adopted and made a part of this chapter as though fully set forth herein, insofar as they relate to the tax imposed by this chapter, excepting Utah Code § 59-12-101 thereof. Wherever, and to the extent that in Utah Code Chapter 59-12 Part 1 the State of Utah is named or referred to as the taxing agency, the name of the County shall be substituted therefor. Nothing in this subsection shall be deemed to require substitution of the name of the County for the word "State" when that word is used as part of the title of the State Tax Commission or of the Constitution of the State of Utah, nor shall the name of the County be substituted for that of the State in any section when the result of that substitution would require action to be taken by or against the County or any agency thereof, rather than by or against the State Tax Commission in performing the functions incident to the administration or operation of the chapter.


===== 3.03.060 Use Of Revenues =====
The revenues received from the tourism, recreation, cultural, convention, and airport facilities taxes levied pursuant to UCC 3.03.050 shall be used solely for the purposes of financing, in whole or in part, tourism promotion and the development, operation, and maintenance of airport, convention, cultural, recreation, and tourist facilities as defined herein. The Board of County Commissioners may issue bonds under the provisions of the Utah Municipal Bond Act to pay any costs incurred for the purposes set forth above and may pledge the entire proceeds of the tax provided for in this chapter to the payment of principal, interest, premiums, and necessary reserves for any such bonds.


===== 3.03.070 Collection =====
Taxes imposed under this chapter shall be levied at the same time and collected in the same manner as provided for in Utah Code § 59-12-201 et seq., except that the revenue derived from the tax is not subject to the collection and distribution procedures established pursuant to the provisions of Utah Code § 59-12-205(2). All revenues so collected shall be the revenues of the County. As allowed by Utah Code § 59-12-209, any records, tax returns, or other information of any person, corporation, company, or other group or organization subject to the taxes imposed by this chapter which relate to the calculation, collection, or remittance to the State Tax Commission of such taxes shall be subject to review, inspection, and auditing by the County.


===== 3.03.080 Licensure =====
All persons, companies, corporations or other similar persons, groups, or organizations doing business as restaurants shall obtain from the State Tax Commission, a tourism, recreation, cultural, or convention tax license. No such additional license shall be required if the person, company, corporation, group, or organization has obtained a license pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-106.


===== 3.04 Real Property Tax Sale Procedures =====
3.04.010 Purpose 3.04.020 Bidder Registration Procedures 3.04.030 Redemption Rights And Procedures 3.04.040 Prohibition Of Collusive Bidding 3.04.050 Conflict Of Interest Prohibitions And Disclosure Requirements 3.04.060 Criteria For Accepting Or Rejecting Bids 3.04.070 Sale Ratification Procedures 3.04.080 Criteria For Granting Bidder Preference 3.04.090 Procedures For Recording Tax Deeds 3.04.100 Payment Methods And Procedures 3.04.110 Procedures For Contesting Bids And Sales 3.04.120 Criteria For Striking Properties To The County 3.04.130 Procedures For Disclosing Properties Withdrawn From The Sale For Reasons Other Than Redemption 3.04.140 Disclaimers By The County With Respect To Sale Procedures And Actions 3.04.150 Excess Proceeds 3.04.160 Miscellaneous Procedures HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2022-351 Updated Property Tax Sale Procedures on 5/11/2022 Amended by Ord. 2023-378 Updated Property Tax Sale Procedures on 4/19/2023


===== 3.04.010 Purpose =====
To facilitate the sale of properties certified for the annual real property sale and to provide for a consistency of procedure, when the County Auditor conducts the annual real property tax sale pursuant to Utah Code § 59-2-1351.1, the sale shall be conducted in accordance with the provisions of this chapter.


===== 3.04.020 Bidder Registration Procedures =====
All individuals interested in participating in the annual real property tax sale shall be required to register in order to participate in the tax sale. To reduce confusion after bidding begins, an individual who is registering for a sale that is conducted as an in-person auction shall be required to register and receive a bid number before the sale starts and/or bidding begins. Bidders will not be allowed to register after registration has been closed at the start of the sale and/or bidding begins. Bidders will be required to pay any registration fees and produce any funds for deposit, if required, prior to being issued a bidder number. The bidder agreement, signed by the bidder at registration, shall include a schedule of fees, if required, and shall identify if any fee or portion of fee is non- refundable. An individual who is registering for a sale that is conducted as an online auction shall be required to register as per the requirements of the vendor contracted by the County to conduct the online auction. Bidders will be subject to the terms of service, registration requirements, and payment of fees (both refundable and non- refundable) according to the vendors’ terms of service. An individual interested in bidding for more than one entity shall be required to register separately each such entity in order to bid. Tax deeds will only be prepared in the name of the successful bidder, as provided by the winning bidder during registration.


===== 3.04.030 Redemption Rights And Procedures =====
Property may be redeemed on behalf of the record owner by any person at any time prior to the time that bidding starts on the property at the Tax Sale following the lapse of four years from the date the property tax became delinquent. A person may redeem property by paying to the County Treasurer all delinquent taxes, interest, penalties, and administrative costs that have accrued on the property. If two or more persons own a piece of property on which a delinquency exists, any owner may redeem the owner’s interest in the property upon payment of that portion of the taxes, interest, penalties, and administrative costs which the owner’s interest bears to the whole, as determined by the County Commission. If any property is redeemed, the County Treasurer shall make the proper entry in the record of tax sales filed in the Treasurer’s Office and issue a certificate of redemption, which is prima facie evidence of the redemption, and may be recorded in the office of the County Recorder without acknowledgment.


===== 3.04.040 Prohibition Of Collusive Bidding =====
Collusive bidding is prohibited. All bids should be submitted in the name of a single individual, and the tax deed issued thereafter will only be executed in the name of an individual successful purchaser. Collusive bidding is defined as any agreement or understanding reached by two (2) or more parties that changes the bids the parties would otherwise offer absent the agreement or understanding. The County Auditor and/or the County Commission have the right to reject any bid deemed collusive.


===== 3.04.050 Conflict Of Interest Prohibitions And Disclosure Requirements =====
No employee of any County office connected with the Tax Sale may bid on or benefit from property offered for sale, either directly or indirectly, except where the employee is the record owner or an abutting property owner. Where a business associate or relative of an employee of any County office connected with the Tax Sale desires to participate in the Tax Sale, the bidder must complete a written disclosure of any relationships that might create the appearance of a conflict of interest prior to the sale.


===== 3.04.060 Criteria For Accepting Or Rejecting Bids =====
The following bid types will be accepted: a bid in an amount sufficient to pay the taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs for the entire parcel of property; and a bid in an amount sufficient to pay the taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs for less than a one hundred percent (100%) undivided interest in the entire parcel of property, but not less than one percent (1%).
 The following bid methods will be used. The County Auditor shall determine which bid method best protects the interests of the property owner and the interest of the public. A competitive bid process in which bidders offer bids in increasing amounts for the entire parcel of property, also known as Bid-Up. A competitive bid process in which bidders offer bids for decreasing amounts of ownership interest as outlined in Subsection F, also known as Undivided Interest.
 The County Auditor shall establish an opening bid that best protects the interests of the property owner and the interest of the public. The opening bid shall be established as: an amount that is not less than is sufficient to pay the taxes, penalties, interest, and administration costs, also known as the minimum opening bid; an amount that is not more than the most recently assessed market value of the property as established by the County Assessor, unless the taxes, penalties, interest, and administration costs are more than the most recently assessed market value of the property, also known as the maximum opening bid; or any other amount between the minimum and maximum as approved by the County Auditor.
 The County Auditor shall determine the bid method for each parcel of property. The bid increments shall be determined by the individual conducting an in-person auction or by the programmed procedures used by an electronic auction vendor, as authorized by the County Auditor. Unless the County Auditor determines that another method of bid more fully protects the interest of the property owner and the interest of the public, the method of bid for the following types of parcels of property shall be Bid­-up: parcels of property which are twenty (20) feet or less in width, at the narrowest point, and which are not condominiums and do not contain dwellings; parcels of property which have an area of one tenth (0.1) acre or less, and which are not condominiums and which do not contain dwellings; and parcels of property for which the ownership interest has been previously divided and the portion being sold at the tax sale is ninety-nine percent (99%) or less undivided ownership interest. Unless the County Auditor determines that another method of bid more fully protects the interest of the property owner and the interest of the public, the method of bid for parcels of property which are not sold by the highest bid for the entire parcel of property shall be Undivided Interest. The first bid shall be for the amount of taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs for a one hundred percent (100%) ownership interest in the entire parcel of property. Acceptable subsequent bids shall be for the amount of taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs for a lesser undivided percentage ownership interest, but not less than one percent (1%). In the event a bid is received for the amount of taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs for an undivided one percent (1%) interest in the entire parcel of property, the method of bid shall be the highest bid amount for the undivided one percent (1%) interest.
 Once the County Auditor has closed the sale of a particular parcel of property as a result of accepting a bid on the parcel, the successful bidder or purchaser of the property may not unilaterally rescind the bid. The County Commission, after the acceptance of a bid, may enforce the terms of the bid by forfeiting any required deposit of the purchaser and obtaining a legal judgment against the purchaser in the amount of the bid, plus interest and attorney's fees.
 Acceptance of a bid by the County Auditor is tentative and is subject to acceptance or rejection by the County Commission. The County Commission may reject any or all bids.
 HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-216 Updated Criteria for Accepting or Rejecting Bids on 2/26/2025


===== 3.04.070 Sale Ratification Procedures =====
All bids accepted by the County Auditor at the annual real property tax sale shall be submitted to the County Commission for ratification. Any bid not ratified shall be deemed rejected and the bid amount paid shall be refunded to the bidder minus any non-refundable fee or deposits as required when the bidder submitted their bidder registration.


===== 3.04.080 Criteria For Granting Bidder Preference =====
At the discretion of the County Auditor a parcel of real property may be offered as a Preferred Sale to bidders who are granted Preferred Bidder Status as provided for herein. “Preferred Sale” is the sale of a single, designated parcel of real property being offered for sale at the annual property tax sale in which: only bidders that have been granted Preferred Bidder Status will be able to participate; and if no bids are received during the Preferred Sale, the sale of the designated parcel shall proceed under normal operating procedures (not a Preferred Sale) and all qualified bidders will be able to participate.
 A parcel may be offered as a Preferred Sale if it meets the following criteria: the parcel has been determined not to be an economically viable unit of property to other than a preferential interest based upon consideration of such characteristics as size, shape, access, zoning, or other factors that may affect the economic value and use of the parcel; or a non-preference sale of the parcel would create a nuisance and/or cloud upon an existing interest in the property and could unreasonably diminish the value of such an interest.
 “Preferred Bidder Status” may be granted to bidders who meet the following criteria: the bidder can demonstrate a possessory interest in the parcel being offered for Preferred Sale; or the bidder owns a parcel or parcels that abut the parcel being offered for Preferred Sale.


===== 3.04.090 Procedures For Recording Tax Deeds =====
Upon payment by the successful bidder, the County Treasurer or online auction vendor will issue a temporary receipt. After ratification by the County Commission and after recordation, the County Auditor will mail the Tax Deed to the name and address listed on the bid sheet and bidder registration form. Deeds issued by the County Auditor shall recite the following: the total amount of all the delinquent taxes, penalties, interest, and administrative costs which were paid for the execution and delivery of the deed; the year for which the property was assessed, the year the property became delinquent, and the year the property was subject to tax sale; a full description of the property; and the name of grantee.
 When the deed is executed and delivered by the County Auditor, it shall be prima facie evidence of the regularity of all proceedings subsequent to the date the taxes initially became delinquent and of the conveyance of the property to the grantee in fee simple. The deed issued by the County Auditor under this section shall be recorded by the County Recorder. The fee for the recording shall be included in the administrative costs of the sale.


===== 3.04.100 Payment Methods And Procedures =====
The only acceptable methods of payment for properties purchased at the annual real property tax sale shall be in cash, certified check, money order or other method deemed acceptable by either the County Treasurer or vendor conducting an online auction. No personal checks will be accepted for payment.


===== 3.04.110 Procedures For Contesting Bids And Sales =====
Any person wishing to contest any action taken in conjunction with the annual real property tax sale must file a written protest with the County Commission of the County within thirty (30) days after the date of the annual real property tax sale. The County Commission are not obligated to grant hearings for purposes of contesting a bid or sale and may render a decision based upon all information following a review of submissions provided by the contesting parties. The County Commission may award the property to one of the bidders, reject all bids and order it re-offered for sale, or, upon a finding that it is in the best public interest, withdraw the property from the sale.


===== 3.04.120 Criteria For Striking Properties To The County =====
Any property offered for sale for which there is no purchaser and which it is not in the public interest to withdraw and recertify to a subsequent sale shall be struck off to the County by the County Auditor, who shall then: Publicly declare substantially as follows: “All property here offered for sale which has not been struck off to a private purchaser is hereby struck off and sold to the County of Utah, and I hereby declare the fee simple title of the property to be vested in the County.” Make an endorsement opposite each of the entries in the delinquency tax sale record described in Utah Code § 52-2-1338 substantially as follows: “The fee simple title in the property described in this entry in the year 20 sold and conveyed to the County of Utah in payment of general taxes charged to the property”; and Sign the County Auditor’s name to the record.
 The fee simple title shall then vest in the County.


===== 3.04.130 Procedures For Disclosing Properties Withdrawn From The Sale For Reasons Other Than Redemption =====
The County shall have the right and authority to remove any parcel of property from the annual real property tax sale for any reason at any time before or during the sale. Disclosure will be made by the County Auditor before or during the sale of all properties withdrawn from the sale for reasons other than redemption.


===== 3.04.140 Disclaimers By The County With Respect To Sale Procedures And Actions =====
Properties sold during the annual real property tax sale shall be conveyed by Tax Deed. This form of deed is not a warranty deed. The County makes no representations as to the title conveyed, nor as to the purchaser’s right of possession of the property. Similarly, the County makes no warranties or representations as to whether the property is buildable or developable, nor does the County make any representations regarding whether the property complies with applicable zoning regulations. The County does not warrant or represent that any property purchased during the Tax Sale is habitable or in any particular condition. The County also makes no warranties or representations regarding the accuracy of the assessment of the property or the accuracy of the description of the real estate or improvements thereon. The County attempts to protect the rights of all parties by complying with all notice provisions of Utah Code Title 59. While the County attempts to give notice to all property owners, and those persons, corporations, partnerships, and entities with a substantial interest in the parcels of property, it does not perform a title search on each parcel of property due to budgetary and time constraints, and specific tax sales may be challenged on due process grounds. If, at any time, it comes to the attention of the County that the County failed to notify a necessary party that a parcel of property was included in the annual real property tax sale, the County shall have the option, at any time, to invalidate the sale for the affected parcel. If the County does invalidate the sale of a parcel of property, the County will refund any monies paid by the successful bidder for the parcel at the tax sale minus any non-refundable fees collected at the time of registration. The successful bidder shall then be required to execute a quit-claim deed in favor of the County for the parcel of property in question. All bidders, by bidding at the annual real property tax sale, shall be bound by this provision of the ordinance. Due to the backlog of property transactions at the County Recorder’s Office, the County has the right to delay the issuance of tax deeds for the annual real property tax sale for a period of up to four months.


===== 3.04.150 Excess Proceeds =====
Any amount received in excess of the taxes, interest, penalties, and administrative costs of the delinquent property ("excess proceeds") shall be treated as unclaimed property, pursuant to Utah Code Chapter 67-4a, Unclaimed Property Act. Claims to Excess Proceeds Excess proceeds, except those for which the County receives a notice of a claim as required herein, shall be deposited with the State of Utah under the Unclaimed Property Act and claims may be made under the Unclaimed Property Act. A person or entity wanting to claim excess proceeds, hereinafter "the Claimant", must, within ninety (90) days after the ratification of the tax sale by the County Commission, petition the Utah State Fourth District Court to adjudicate the excess proceeds. Before the expiration of the ninety (90) days, the Claimant must provide a written notice to the County Tax Administration office which includes a copy of the petition filed with the court.
 The Claimant must provide notice to all other persons and entities of which the Claimant is aware that have an interest in the excess proceeds, including all parties with a recorded lien or a recorded ownership interest. The excess proceeds shall be distributed as ordered by the court. Persons making claims to excess proceeds on behalf of the Claimant, in addition to the requirements contained within Subsection B of UCC 3.04.150, shall adhere to the following requirements. Unlicensed "Fee Finders": An unlicensed Fee Finder is defined as any individual or entity that is not a currently licensed attorney, a currently licensed CPA, or CPA firm. Fees for discovering excess proceeds that are in the custody of the County shall be limited to not more than twenty percent (20%) of the amount collected. With the notification to the County as required by Subsection B3 of UCC 3.04.150, the Fee Finder must submit: a signed, dated, and notarized copy of the contract between the Fee Finder and the Claimant which satisfies the requirements of the Unclaimed Property Act and specifies the obligations of the parties as well as the fee arrangement between the finder and claimant; and if the Fee Finder charges a contingent fee, a copy of the active private detective license issued to the finder by the Utah Department of Public Safety, Bureau of Criminal Identification (BCI). A licensed attorney, licensed CPA, or a licensed CPA firm may pursue a claim for recovery of excess proceeds held by the County provided the attorney, CPA, or CPA firm have a contractual relationship with the Claimant.


===== 3.04.160 Miscellaneous Procedures =====
In the event a parcel of property which is subject to the Farmland Assessment Act ("Greenbelt") no longer qualifies for greenbelt status, a rollback tax will then be imposed, which tax shall be in addition to the advertised delinquent tax amount. Such rollback tax shall be the responsibility of the successful bidder of the parcel of property in question. The current year’s taxes for properties included in the annual real property tax sale are not being paid by the successful bidders at the tax sale. Additional taxes for the current year will be due in November of the current year. In the event the entire parcel is purchased at the tax sale, new address forms must be filled out by the successful bidders in order to receive tax notices for the current year. If an undivided interest is sold at the tax sale, tax notices will continue to go to the record owners. After the conclusion of the bidding for the delinquent parcels of property, all bidders will be required to pay their winning bids by the method and within the time frame required by the County Auditor. Any bids for parcels of property which have not been paid within the time frame prescribed may be oﬀered up for auction again or oﬀered to the bidder who placed the second or third highest bid as authorized by the County Auditor. Bidders who fail to pay their bid will be banned from oﬀering any additional bids for any property oﬀered for re‐auction and will forfeit any required deposit. If no bid is received at the re‐auction for the parcel of property or if the winning bidder of the re‐auction also fails to pay the bid, the County Auditor may recommend one of the following actions to the County Commission at the time the sale is put forth to be ratified: recommend the County enforce the original accepted bid as per UCC 3.04.060 subsection G; recommend the County enforce the re‐auction accepted bid as per UCC 3.04.060 subsection G; recommend the auction be declared as a “no‐sale” and the parcel be struck to the County; or recommend the parcel be pulled and oﬀered for sale the following year. Any individual who is a successful bidder for a parcel of property included in the annual real property tax sale and who fails to pay for said parcel will be subject to the following actions by the County Auditor as applicable. The bidder shall be banned from all future Utah County Tax Sales. The County will also provide the banned bidder’s information to the other counties in the state, noting the bidder has been banned from future Utah County tax sales and the reason why. Any bidders wishing to be reinstated may appeal their ban to the County Auditor no less than fourteen (14) months after the ban was instituted. Bidders who attempt to circumnavigate the ban and participate in future auctions without first successfully appealing their ban with the County Auditor, will have their ban extended and not able to appeal the ban for a period of no less than thirty-six (36) months after the date of which the extension was implemented. Bidders with extensions will be required to pay a one‐time appeal filing fee of five hundred dollars ($500) and submit an appeal to the ban in writing to the County Auditor in order to request the ban be lifted. All individuals and entities participating in the annual real property tax sale shall be subject to the County tax sale procedures enumerated in this chapter, together with all additional instructions set forth by the County Auditor and any auction service provider prior to the start of bidding at the annual real property tax sale. HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2024-875 Updated Miscellaneous Procedures on 10/24/2024


===== 3.05 911 Access Line Surcharge =====
3.05.010 Definitions 3.05.020 911 Access Line Surcharge


===== 3.05.010 Definitions =====
As used in this Chapter: “Local exchange service” means the provision of public telecommunications services by a wireline common carrier to customers within a geographic area encompassing one or more local communities as described in the carrier’s service territory maps, tariffs, price lists, or rate schedules filed with and approved by the Public Service Commission.

“Local exchange service switched access line” means the transmission facility and local switching equipment used by a wireline common carrier to connect a customer location to a carrier’s local exchange switching network for providing two-way interactive voice, or voice capable, services.

“911 emergency telephone service” means the communication system which provides citizens with rapid direct access to the public emergency operation center operated by the County by dialing or texting the telephone number “911” with the objective of reducing the response time to situations requiring law enforcement, fire, medical, rescue, and other emergency services.

“Radio communications access line” means the radio equipment and assigned customer identification number used to connect a mobile or fixed radio customer in the County to a radio communication service provider’s network for two-way interactive voice, or voice capable, services.

“Radio communications service” means a public telecommunications service providing the capability of two-way interactive telecommunications between mobile and fixed radio customers, and between mobile or fixed radio customers and the local exchange service network customers of a wireline common carrier. Radio communications service providers include corporations, persons or entities offering cellular telephone service, enhanced specialized mobile radio service, rural radio service, radio common carrier services, personal communications services, and any equivalent wireless public telecommunications service, as defined in 47 CFR, parts 20, 21, 22, 24, and 90.

“Wireline common carrier” means a public telecommunications service provider that primarily uses cables and wires for connecting customers to its local exchange service networks.


===== 3.05.020 911 Access Line Surcharge =====
The County Commission hereby levies a monthly emergency telephone charge on each local exchange service switched access line and each revenue producing radio communications access line within the area served by the 911 emergency telephone service communication system operated by the County, except as provided in Subsection B.
 Access lines provided for public coin telephone service are exempt from emergency telephone charges. A radio communications access line customer with a billing address within the boundaries of the area served by the 911 emergency telephone service communication system operated by the County shall be subject to the emergency telephone charge on a maximum of five (5) radio communications access lines. Access lines located within an area outside the area served by the 911 emergency telephone service communication system operated by the County shall not be subject to the emergency telephone charge.
 The amount of the charge hereby levied is fifty-three cents (53¢) per month for each local exchange service switched access line and fifty-three cents (53¢) per month for each radio communications access line. Notification of the intent to levy this charge shall be given to the Public Service Commission at least thirty (30) days prior to the effective date. The emergency telephone charge levied under this section shall be billed, collected, and remitted by the corporation, person, or entity that provides the local exchange service switched access line services or radio communications access line services to the County. The money received by the County for the provision of 911 emergency telephone service shall be deposited in a special emergency telephone service fund. The money in the emergency telephone service fund shall be expended by the County to pay the costs of establishing, installing, maintaining, and operating a 911 emergency telephone system and/or integrating a 911 system into an established public safety dispatch center, including contracting with the providers of local exchange service, radio communications service, and vendors of appropriate terminal equipment as necessary to implement the 911 emergency telephone service. Revenues derived for the funding of 911 emergency telephone service may only be used for that portion of costs related to the operation of the 911 emergency telephone system when such a system is integrated with any public safety dispatch system.


===== 3.06 Optional County Sales And Use Tax =====
3.06.010 Title Of Provisions 3.06.020 Statutory Authority 3.06.030 Purpose Of Provisions 3.06.040 Imposition - Amount 3.06.050 Incorporation Of State Law 3.06.060 Administration, Collection And Distribution By State 3.06.070 Exemptions 3.06.080 Effective Date


===== 3.06.010 Title Of Provisions =====
The ordinance codified in this chapter shall be known as the “Optional County Sales and Use Tax.”


===== 3.06.020 Statutory Authority =====
The authority for imposing this tax is derived from Utah Code Chapter 59-12 Part 11.


===== 3.06.030 Purpose Of Provisions =====
The ordinance codified in this chapter is enacted to provide the County with a source of revenue to allow the County to carry out its role more effectively as a political and legal subdivision of the State of Utah. The County Commission hereby directs that the provisions hereof be interpreted and construed to accomplish this stated purpose.


===== 3.06.040 Imposition - Amount =====
In addition to all other taxes imposed, the County does hereby impose and levy for collection a sales and use tax of one quarter percent (0.25%) upon the sales and uses described in Utah Code § 59-12-103(1), subject to the exemptions provided for in Utah Code § 59- 12-104. This tax is imposed upon all sales and uses made in the County, including sales and uses made within the corporate limits of the cities and towns of the County. Provisions of this ordinance shall be subject to the provisions of the Sales and Use Tax laws of Utah to which reference is hereafter made in this chapter and which are hereby enacted and made a part of this ordinance as though fully set forth herein.


===== 3.06.050 Incorporation Of State Law =====
Except as hereinafter provided and except insofar as they are inconsistent with provisions of the County Option Sales and Use Tax Act, all of the provisions of Utah Code Chapter 59-12 Part 1 in force and effect on the effective date of this ordinance insofar as they relate to the tax imposed by this ordinance except Utah Code § 59-12-101, thereof, are adopted and made a part of this ordinance as though fully set forth herein. Wherever and to the extent that in Utah Code Chapter 59-12 Part 1 the State of Utah is named or referred to as the taxing agency, the name of this County shall be substituted therefor. Nothing in this subsection shall be deemed to require substitution of the name of the County for the word “state” when that word is used as part of the title of the State Tax Commission, or the Constitution of the State of Utah, nor shall the name of the County be substituted for that of the state in any section when the result of that substitution would require action to be taken by or against the County or any agency thereof, rather than by or against the State Tax Commission in performing the functions incident to the administration or operation of this ordinance.


===== 3.06.060 Administration, Collection And Distribution By State =====
Taxes imposed pursuant to this chapter shall be levied at the same time and collected in the same manner as provided for in Utah Code § 59-12-201, et. seq., except that the revenue derived from the tax is not subject to the collection and distribution procedures established pursuant to the provisions of Utah Code § 59-12-205(2). Revenues collected pursuant to this ordinance shall be distributed in accordance with Utah Code 59-12-1102(3) and the rules adopted by the Utah State Tax Commission pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-1102(3)(d). All revenues so collected shall be revenues of either the County or any other county entitled to distribution of the same pursuant to the statute. Any records, tax returns or other information of any person, corporation, company, or other group or organization subject to the taxes imposed by this ordinance which relate to the calculation, collection, or remittance to the State Tax Commission of such taxes shall be subject to review, inspection, and auditing by the County, as allowed under Utah Code § 59-12-209. The fee charged the County by the State Tax Commission under Utah Code § 59-12-206 shall be based on the distribution amount resulting after all the applicable distribution calculations under Utah Code § 59-12-1102(3) have been made.


===== 3.06.070 Exemptions =====
The sale, storage, use, or other consumption of tangible personal property which is exempt from sales or use taxation pursuant to Utah Code § 59-12-104 is exempt from the application of the Optional County Sales and Use Tax.


===== 3.06.080 Effective Date =====
The tax imposed by this chapter shall take effect January 1, 1998.


===== 3.07 Administration Of Property Tax Refund, Abatement, Payment Agreement, Deferral, Credit And Exemption Requests =====
3.07.010 Uniform Policies Established 3.07.020 Processing Requests For Property Tax Refund, Abatement, Payment Agreement, Deferral, Credit And Exemption


===== 3.07.010 Uniform Policies Established =====
To provide consistency of procedure and uniformity of application in the processing of requests for property tax refunds, abatements, payment agreements, deferrals, credits, and exemptions, including related penalties and interest, under Utah Code §§ 59-2-1106, 59-2-19, 59-2-1115, 59-2-1321, 59-2-1347, and 59-2-1201
through 59-2-1220, the County Commission finds it to be in the best interest of the public welfare to establish uniform standards and policies governing such requests.


===== 3.07.020 Processing Requests For Property Tax Refund, Abatement, Payment Agreement, Deferral, Credit And Exemption =====
Unless otherwise provided for herein, all requests for refund, abatement, payment agreement, deferral, credit or exemption of property taxes, and related penalties or interest, under Utah Code §§ 59-2-1106, 59-2-19, 59-2-1115, 59-2-1321, 59-2-1347, and 59-2-1201 through 59-2-1220 shall be by written application, shall set forth the specific basis for the request and shall include all evidence supporting the request. A decision shall be made on each request based upon the submitted materials, unless the County Commission desires further input from the applicant and takes action in a public meeting to place the matter on the commission agenda for further review. Except as specifically otherwise authorized herein or by official action of the County Commission, each request for refund, abatement, payment agreement, deferral, credit, or exemption of property taxes, and related penalties or interest, shall be referred to the property tax officer for review and recommendation. The property tax officer shall be an attorney from the civil division of the County Attorney’s Office, designated by the County Commission. The property tax officer may request recommendations from involved county offices, may request additional information from the applicant, and may request that the applicant appear to address issues raised by the application. The property tax officer shall review all of the submitted materials and information and shall then provide a recommendation on the application to the County Commission. The County Commission shall review the submitted materials and the recommendation of the property tax officer and shall then take action on the request, based upon the submitted materials. Routine corrections and abatements initiated by individual county offices shall be submitted directly to the County Commission for action. The property owner is responsible for the timely payment of the applicable property taxes, and no mistake in the name or address of a property owner renders a tax assessment invalid. Any refund amount that is approved shall first be applied to any known existing delinquency for that property, then to any known existing delinquency on any other property owned by the applicant, and then refunded to the party making the excess payment. Applications for exemptions of taxable tangible personal property with a total aggregate fair market value that is at or below the statutorily prescribed amount shall be processed as follows: Applications for property tax exemptions of taxable tangible personal property with a total aggregate fair market value that is at or below the statutorily prescribed amount pursuant to Utah Code § 59-2-1115 shall be administratively processed by the Office of the County Assessor. The County Assessor is hereby authorized to administratively exempt the personal property tax upon the receipt of a complete application and the determination that the applicant qualifies for the exemption under Utah Code § 59-2-1115. Applications must satisfy the requirements herein and comply with state code and otherwise contain the information and attached documentation deemed necessary by the County Assessor. The County Assessor shall provide notice to an applicant under this chapter, including the determination made regarding the application for exemption. The determination made by the County Assessor as provided for herein shall constitute the decision and determination of the County Commission, or as appropriate the Board of Equalization.
 Tax relief under the statutory armed forces exemption, blind exemption, indigent abatement, and circuit breaker tax credits shall be processed as follows: Applications for tax relief under the statutory armed forces exemption, blind exemption, indigent abatement, pursuant to Utah Code §§ 59-2-1106 and 59-2-19, and circuit breaker tax credit, pursuant to Utah Code §§ 59-2-1201 through 59-2-1220, shall be administratively processed by the County Auditor’s Office. The County Auditor is hereby authorized to administratively exempt, abate or apply the tax credit as applied for upon the receipt of a complete application and the determination that the applicant qualifies for the exemption under the appropriate Utah Code §§ 59-2-1106 and 59-2-19 or 59-2-1201 through 59-2-1220. Applications must satisfy the requirements herein and comply with state code and otherwise contain the information and attached documentation deemed necessary by the County Auditor. The County Auditor shall provide notice to an applicant under this chapter including the determination made regarding the application for exemption, abatement, or tax credit. The determination made by the County Auditor as provided for herein shall constitute the decision and determination of the County Commission, or as appropriate the Board of Equalization.


===== 3.08 Property Penalty Reductions =====
3.08.010 Purpose 3.08.020 Personal Property Penalty Reduction Not Based On County Error 3.08.030 Personal Property Penalty Waiver And Reduction Based On County Error 3.08.040 Real Property Penalty Reduction Not Based On County Error 3.08.050 Real Property Penalty Waiver And Reduction Based On County Error HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2025-161 Updated Property Penalty Reductions on 2/12/2025


===== 3.08.010 Purpose =====
The purpose of this chapter is to establish the procedure pursuant to Utah Code § 59-2-307(2)(a) for the review and approval of reductions and waivers for penalties imposed for: failure to timely file a signed statement required by Utah Code § 59-2-306; failure to timely file a signed statement with respect to name and place of residence; failure to appear and testify; or penalties and interest imposed on real property.


===== 3.08.020 Personal Property Penalty Reduction Not Based On County Error =====
The County Commission hereby authorizes the Office of the County Assessor to reduce the penalty imposed by Utah Code § 59-2-307(1) to zero dollars ($0) if the tax amount for the subject year is two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500.00) or less, based upon an appropriately filed request for reduction or waiver, establishing the best human interests and the interests of the State and the County are served, and by a finding that each of the following requirements are met: the assessment of the penalty was not caused by a County error; all taxes for the current year and for all prior years have been paid in full; the tax amount for the subject year does not exceed two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500.00); the penalty has been assessed because of a failure to file the signed statement required by Utah Code § 59-2-306 or because of a failure to file the signed statement with respect to name and place of residence; the current owner has not been assessed a penalty under Utah Code § 59-2-307(1), related to any of the prior three (3) tax years; and the owner has filed the delinquent signed statement prior to or at the time of filing the request for reduction or waiver of the penalty. The County Assessor shall provide notice to an applicant under this chapter including the determination made regarding the application for reduction of penalty. The determination made by the County Assessor as provided for herein shall constitute the decision and determination of the County Commission.


===== 3.08.030 Personal Property Penalty Waiver And Reduction Based On County Error =====
Where the County Assessor finds that an error made by the County precipitated assessment of the penalty under Utah Code § 59-2-307, the penalty and interest may be reduced or waived.


===== 3.08.040 Real Property Penalty Reduction Not Based On County Error =====
The County Commission, acting under authority granted in Utah Code § 59-2-1347, hereby authorizes the Office of the County Treasurer to reduce the penalty and interest imposed by Utah Code § 59-2-1331 to zero dollars ($0) if the tax amount for the subject year is five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) or less, based upon an appropriately filed request for reduction or waiver, establishing the best human interests and the interests of the State and the County are served, and by a finding that each of the following requirements are met:
 the assessment of the penalty was not caused by a County error; all taxes for all prior years have been paid in full; the penalty has been assessed because of a failure to meet Property Tax due date requirements outlined in Utah Code § 59-2-1331; the tax amount for the subject year does not exceed five thousand dollars ($5,000.00); the current owner has not been assessed a penalty under Utah Code§ 59-2-1331, related to any of the prior three (3) tax years; the owner has submitted an application to the County Treasurer's Office. The County Treasurer shall provide notice to an applicant under this chapter including the determination made regarding the application for reduction of penalty. The determination made by the County Treasurer as provided for herein shall constitute the decision and determination of the County Commission.


===== 3.08.050 Real Property Penalty Waiver And Reduction Based On County Error =====
Where the County Treasurer finds that an error made by the County precipitated assessment of the penalty under Utah Code § 59-2-1331, the penalty and interest may be reduced or waived.


===== 3.09 Property Tax Abatement Or Deferral Applications =====
3.09.010 Applications Based On Financial Hardship 3.09.020 Applications For Property Tax Abatement Or Deferral Not Based On Extreme Financial Hardship


===== 3.09.010 Applications Based On Financial Hardship =====
The County Commission may grant an application for abatement or deferral of property taxes under Utah Code §§ 59-2-18 for a person under the age of 65 years based on disability, or based on extreme financial hardship, upon a finding by the County Commission that each of the following requirements are met:
 the applicant is in extreme financial hardship which is not an intended result of voluntary actions by the applicant, or by members of the applicant’s household; or, the applicant is disabled and is either receiving Social Security disability payments or meets the requirements to receive such payments; the applicant, and other members of the applicant’s household, do not have resources or sources of funds to pay the taxes; provided, however, that equity in the applicant’s residence shall not be considered as a source of funds if the amount of the equity is less than the amount of the primary personal residence exemption provided for under Utah Code § 78B-5-503(2); the applicant is not the owner of income producing assets that could be liquidated to pay the tax; the best human interests are served by the abatement or deferral; the interests of the State of Utah and of the County are served by the abatement or deferral; the applicant meets all of the requirements under Utah Code § 59-2-18, has filed a complete application and documentation establishing the nature and extent of the disability, or the nature and extent of the extreme financial hardship, and has submitted financial information and documentation establishing the applicant’s total household income as defined by Utah Code § 59-2-1202, provided that if the household income for the preceding year does not reflect the current household income, the applicant shall establish the applicant’s current household income with a detailed explanation of the factual basis for the difference between the preceding year’s and current year’s household income; the property is the sole residence of the applicant; the applicant is unable to meet the tax assessed on the applicant’s residence as the tax becomes due; and in the case of a deferral, the applicant has filed the written consent of the holder of each mortgage and trust deed outstanding on the property. The County Commission may grant an application for abatement or deferral of property taxes under Utah Code §§ 59-2-18 for a person the age of 65 years or older, upon a finding by the County Commission that each of the following requirements are met: the applicant is unable to meet the tax assessed on the applicant’s residence as the tax becomes due and the inability to meet the tax assessed is not an intended result of voluntary actions by the applicant, or by members of the applicant’s household; the applicant is not the owner of income producing assets that could be liquidated to pay the tax; the best human interests are served by the abatement or deferral; the interests of the State of Utah and of the County are served by the abatement or deferral; the applicant meets all of the requirements under Utah Code §§ 59-2- 18, has filed a complete application and documentation establishing the factual basis for the application, and has submitted financial information and documentation establishing the applicant’s total household income as defined by Utah Code § 59-2-1202, provided that if the household income for the preceding year does not reflect the current household income, the applicant shall establish the applicant’s current household income with a detailed explanation of the factual basis for the difference between the preceding year’s and current year’s household income; the property is the sole residence of the applicant; and in the case of a deferral, the applicant has filed the written consent of the holder of each mortgage and trust deed outstanding on the property.
 The County Commission may grant an application for abatement or deferral of property taxes under Utah Code § 59-2-1347 based on extreme financial hardship, upon a finding by the County Commission that each of the following requirements are met: the applicant is in extreme financial hardship which is not an intended result of voluntary actions by the applicant, or by members of the applicant’s household; the applicant, and other members of the applicant’s household, do not have resources or sources of funds to pay the taxes; provided, however, that equity in the applicant’s residence shall not be considered as a source of funds if the amount of the equity is less than the amount of the primary personal residence exemption provided for under Utah Code § 78B-5-503; the best human interests are served by the abatement or deferral; the interests of the State of Utah and of the County are served by the abatement or deferral; the applicant has filed a complete application and documentation establishing the factual basis for the application and has submitted financial information and documentation establishing the applicant’s total household income as defined by Utah Code § 59-2-1202, provided that if the household income for the preceding year does not reflect the current household income, the applicant shall establish the applicant’s current household income with a detailed explanation of the factual basis for the difference between the preceding year’s and current year’s household income; the property is the sole residence of the applicant; the applicant is unable to meet the tax assessed on the applicant’s residence as the tax becomes due; and in the case of a deferral, the applicant has filed the written consent of the holder of each mortgage and trust deed outstanding on the property.


===== 3.09.020 Applications For Property Tax Abatement Or Deferral Not Based On Extreme Financial Hardship =====
The County Commission may grant applications for abatement or deferral of property taxes under Utah Code § 59-2-1347, not based on extreme financial hardship, upon a finding by the County Commission that each of the following requirements are met: the best human interests are served by the abatement or deferral; the interests of the State of Utah and of the County are served by the abatement or deferral; the applicant has filed a complete application and documentation establishing the factual basis for the application;
 in the case of a deferral or payment agreement, the applicant has filed the written consent of the holder of each mortgage and trust deed outstanding on the property and has signed a written deferral agreement, or payment agreement, in a form acceptable to the County; in the case of a request to abate a real property tax penalty or interest, based upon an alleged failure of the County to provide the tax notice to the correct address, the applicant has also submitted a copy of the last recorded deed vesting title in the applicant, and a copy of any address change request allegedly filed by the applicant; in the case of a request to abate a real property tax penalty or interest filed with the County not later than March 31st of the year following the imposition of the penalty, based upon an alleged failure of the County to provide the tax notice to the correct address, the applicant did not have actual notice of the tax notice, the County did not mail the tax notice to the record owner as of January 1st of the year in question, the County did not mail the tax notice to a subsequent record owner, the County did not mail the tax notice to the address located on the last recorded deed vesting title in the applicant, and the penalty and interest were imposed solely as a result of an error by the County; and in the case of a request to abate a real property tax penalty or interest filed with the County after March 31st of the year following the imposition of the penalty, based upon an alleged failure of the County to provide the tax notice to the correct address, the applicant did not have actual notice of the tax notice, the County did not mail the tax notice to the record owner as of January 1st of the year in question, the County did not mail the tax notice to a subsequent record owner, the County did not mail the tax notice to the address located on the last recorded deed vesting title in the applicant, the penalty and interest were imposed solely as a result of an error by the County, and the complete application was filed with the County not later than September 30th of the year following the imposition of the penalty, unless the Board of County Commissioners extends the deadline based upon a finding of exceptional circumstances (only the penalty, not the interest, can be abated if the application is filed after March 31st of the year following the imposition of the penalty).


===== 3.10 Residential Property Tax Exemptions =====
3.10.010 Authority And Purpose 3.10.020 Procedure 3.10.030 Criteria 3.10.040 Grandfather Provision


===== 3.10.010 Authority And Purpose =====
The Utah Constitution, Article XIII, Section 3(22) and Utah Code §§ 59-2-102(32) and 103(3) provide that a residential exemption from property tax of forty-five percent (45%) is available for “primary residences,” and the Utah Legislature enacted Utah Code § 59-2-103.5 establishing procedures to obtain an exemption for residential property and authorizing the county legislative body to adopt an ordinance for the allowance of a residential exemption for residential property.


===== 3.10.020 Procedure =====
All owners of residential property as defined in Utah Code § 59-2-102(34) shall submit an application to the Board of Equalization no later than March 1 of the current tax year for exemption from property taxes for residential property used as a primary residence. A separate application shall be filed for each parcel of property. The application shall include the following information for each specific parcel of property for which the exemption is requested: the owner(s) of record of the property; the property parcel number; the location of the property; the basis of the owner’s knowledge of the use of the property; a description of the use of the property; evidence of domicile of the inhabitant(s) of the property; and the signature of all owners of the property certifying that the property is residential property.
 In the event that an application is not timely filed, an appeal to file a late application may be granted by the Board of Equalization, upon a finding of good cause, on an individual appeal basis, for the current tax year only. Applications for exemption shall be accepted for the current year only.
 Except for nursing homes, mixed commercial and residential use properties, and all properties receiving a partial residential exemption, all of which are required to file an application each year, if the Board of Equalization allows an owner the residential exemption, the Board may not require the owner to file another signed statement in order to receive the residential exemption for that property unless; the property did not receive the residential exemption during the calendar year immediately preceding the calendar year for which the owner is seeking to claim the residential exemption for that residential property; any ownership interest in the property changes; or
 the Board of Equalization determines that there is reason to believe that the property no longer qualifies for the residential exemption in accordance with Utah Code § 59-2-103.
 Submission of the application authorizes the Assessor to request or collect information sufficient to allow the Board of Equalization to verify the primary residence status and make the determination if the property is entitled to receive the residential exemption. If an applicant requests a property be designated as a primary residence, the residential exemption shall not be granted without evidence that the property serves as the primary residence. The burden of proof shall remain at all times with the applicant.


===== 3.10.030 Criteria =====
A primary residence means the location where domicile has been established. “Domicile” means the place where an individual has a true, fixed, permanent home and principal establishment, and to which place he has (whenever he is absent) the intention of returning. It is the place in which a person has voluntarily fixed the habitation of himself and family, not for a mere special or temporary purpose, but with the present intention of making a permanent home. After domicile has been established, two (2) things are necessary to create a new domicile: first, an abandonment of the old domicile; and second, the intention and establishment of a new domicile. The mere intention to abandon a domicile once established is not of itself sufficient to create a new domicile; a new domicile must also be shown. A person can have only one domicile. A household can qualify for only one residential exemption, except for rental property where the property is the primary residence of the tenant, and except for property under construction where the assessor determines that the property will qualify as a primary residence after construction is complete. Factors or objective evidence determinative of domicile are set forth in Utah Administrative Code Rule 884-24P-52. To qualify for the residential exemption, a property need not be owner occupied. Apartments and other rental housing used as a primary residence of the occupant(s) qualify for the residential exemption upon accepted application in accordance with this chapter. A primary residence does not include property used for transient residential use, or condominiums used in rental pools. In addition to other evidence of domicile, only the primary residence which is occupied more than six (6) months out of the year qualifies for the residential exemption. The residential exemption is limited to up to one (1) acre of land per residential dwelling unit on a single property description. A partial exemption may be applied against the property taxes of mixed commercial and residential property, but it is presumed that the entire property is commercial. This presumption may be rebutted by the filing of the application for residential exemption, as described in this chapter, every year prior to March 1st, which includes evidence of domicile of each qualifying resident. The Board of Equalization may require additional information as necessary to determine the percentage of the property qualifying for the residential exemption. A partial exemption may be applied against the property taxes of qualifying nursing homes, but it is presumed that the entire property is commercial. This presumption may be rebutted by the applicant. In addition to the other information required by this chapter, the application, which must be filed every year prior to March 1st, shall include the number of residents living at the facility, the name and street address of each resident living at the facility, the current name and street address of the spouse of each resident who is married, the property tax serial number of each residential property in which the resident (or the resident’s spouse) holds an ownership interest, a sworn statement signed by each qualifying resident certifying that the resident lived at the facility for more than six (6) months out of the prior year and that neither the resident nor any spouse of the resident receives the residential exemption based on a domicile at any other property, the total square footage of the facility, and the total non-residential square footage. The Board of Equalization may require additional information as necessary to determine the percentage of eligibility of the property for the exemption.


===== 3.10.040 Grandfather Provision =====
As of the effective date of this chapter, a property that is currently listed by the County as having a residential exemption and is an owner occupied residential property, apartment, and other rental property that is being used as the primary residence of the occupants (not including nursing homes or mixed commercial and residential use properties) shall not be required to file an application to continue its status, unless the Board of Equalization or the Assessor determines that there is reason to believe that the property no longer qualifies for the residential exemption in accordance with Utah Code § 59-2-103, or any ownership interest in the property changes. A property that is initially listed by the County as having a residential exemption; is an owner occupied residential property, apartment, and other rental property that is being used as the primary residence of the occupants (not including nursing homes or mixed commercial and residential use properties); and is constructed after the effective date of this ordinance, shall not be required to file the residential exemption application, unless the County Board of Equalization or the Assessor determines that there is reason to believe that the property no longer qualifies for the residential exemption in accordance with Utah Code § 59-2-103, or any ownership interest in the property changes.

===== 3.12 County Option Sales And Use Tax For Transportation =====
3.12.010 Title Of Provisions 3.12.020 Statutory Authority 3.12.030 Purpose Of Provisions 3.12.040 Imposition - Amount 3.12.050 Incorporation Of State Law 3.12.060 Administration, Collection, Enforcement And Distribution By State 3.12.070 Exemptions 3.12.080 Effective Date


===== 3.12.010 Title Of Provisions =====
The ordinance codified in this chapter shall be known as the “County Option Sales and Use Tax for Transportation Ordinance of Utah County.”


===== 3.12.020 Statutory Authority =====
The authority for imposing this tax is derived from Utah Code Chapter 59-12 Part 22.


===== 3.12.030 Purpose Of Provisions =====
The ordinance codified in this chapter is enacted to provide the County with a source of revenue to allow the County to carry out its role more effectively as a political and legal subdivision of the State of Utah. The County Commission hereby directs that the provisions hereof be interpreted and construed to accomplish this stated purpose.


===== 3.12.040 Imposition - Amount =====
In addition to all other taxes imposed, the County does hereby impose and levy for collection a sales and use tax of three-tenths percent (0.30%) upon the sales and use transactions described in Utah Code § 59-12-103(1), subject to the exemptions provided for in Utah Code § 59-12-104. This tax is imposed upon all sales and use transactions made in the County, including sales and use transactions made within the corporate limits of the cities and towns of the County. Provisions of this ordinance shall be subject to the provisions of the Sales and Use Tax laws of Utah to which reference is hereafter made in this chapter and which are hereby enacted and made a part of this ordinance as though fully set forth herein.


===== 3.12.050 Incorporation Of State Law =====
