 a septic system density study, performed by a geotechnical or hydrogeologic professional licensed in the State of Utah, clearly indicates that the installation of the proposed number of approved onsite wastewater systems will not increase the maximum contamination level (MCL) of nitrate concentration within the affected groundwater source above the United States Environmental Protection Agency's currently stated MCL goal, in which case an approved onsite wastewater system may be used; or a sceptic system density study has not been performed, the proposed and installed wastewater system must be a contaminant and nitrate reducing alternative onsite wastewater system approved by the Utah County Health Department, and meet the requirements set forth below: the applicant has received written permission for the proposed onsite wastewater system from the public water system(s) who has delineated the drinking water source protection zones(s); or the applicant may inquire with the Health Department if the public water system(s) who delineated the drinking water source protection zone(s) utilized the Preferred Delineation Procedure, as set forth by the State of Utah to draw the relevant zone(s); and if the Preferred Delineation Procedure was utilized: The public drinking water system(s) shall be notified by the Health Department and have five (5) business days after receiving the notice in which to submit an objection to the Health Department regarding the applicant's proposed installation of an onsite wastewater system, noting specific concerns. The applicant may then hire an engineer licensed to evaluate groundwater contamination issues to
respond to the objection. If the engineer responds to the objections and concludes that the proposed contaminant and nitrate reducing alternative onsite wastewater system is unlikely to contaminate the drinking water source(s) in question, the Health Department shall approve the application if no other concern is raised by the State. if the preferred Delineation Procedure was not utilized then no objection may be raised. the applicant has received written permission for the proposed onsite wastewater system from the public water system(s) who has delineated the drinking water source protection zones(s); and
 for Zone 2, except in instances where a septic system density study, performed by a geotechnical or hydrogeologic professional licensed in the State of Utah, clearly indicates that the installation of the proposed number of a conventional onsite wastewater systems will not increase the maximum contamination level (MCL) of nitrate concentration within the affected groundwater source above the United States Environmental Protection Agency’s currently stated MCL goal, in which case a conventional onsite wastewater system may be used, the proposed and installed wastewater system is a contaminant reducing alternative onsite wastewater system approved by the Utah County Health Department. HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2023-24 Updated Alleged Overly Protective Zones on 1/4/2023


===== 6.05.090 Administration =====
This chapter shall be administered by the Utah County Health Department, provided that, in addition to any other remedies, a public water system, retail water supplier, or wholesale water supplier may seek enforcement of this chapter in a district court located in Utah County if the County (i) notifies the public water system, retail water supplier or wholesale water supplier within ten (10) days of receiving notice of a violation of this chapter that the County will not seek enforcement of this chapter; or (ii) does not seek enforcement within two days of a notice of violation of this chapter when the violation may cause irreparable harm to the groundwater source.


===== 6.06 Utah State Fire Code =====
6.06.010 Adoption Of Utah State Fire Code 6.06.020 Establishment And Duties Of Bureau Of Fire Prevention 6.06.030 Definitions - Utah State Fire Code 6.06.040 Fire Flow And Fire Sprinkler Requirements 6.06.050 Establishment Of Limits Of Districts In Which Storage Of Explosives And Blasting Agents Is To Be Prohibited 6.06.060 Appeals 6.06.070 Penalties 6.06.080 Superseding Conflicting Ordinances 6.06.090 Recovery Of Fire Suppression Costs 6.06.100 Exemptions Of Temporary Tents And Remote Yurts


===== 6.06.010 Adoption Of Utah State Fire Code =====
There is hereby adopted by the County, as provided in Utah Code § 15A-1-403, for the purpose of prescribing regulations governing conditions hazardous to life and property from fire or explosion occurring in the unincorporated area of the county, the Utah State Fire Code which adopts, with amendments, additions, and modifications, the International Fire Code, the most current edition adopted by the state, issued by the International Fire Code Council, Inc., (“IFC”), save and except such portions as are hereinafter deleted, modified, or amended by the Utah County Code, as authorized in Utah Code § 15A-1-403, hereinafter referred to as the Fire Code. In the event of any conflict between the terms of the Fire Code, the terms of this chapter shall control to the extent permitted under state law. The following appendices to the International Fire Code are hereby adopted: Appendix B-Fire Flow Requirements for Buildings, Appendix C-Fire Hydrant Locations and Distribution, Appendix D-Fire Apparatus Access Roads, Appendix E-Hazard Categories, Appendix F-Hazard Ranking, and Appendix G-Cryongenic Fluids-Weight and Volume Equivalents. The Fire Code is hereby adopted in book form as provided in Utah Code § 17-64-502, and incorporated by such adoption as fully as if set out at length herein, and shall be controlling within the limits of the unincorporated area of the County.


===== 6.06.020 Establishment And Duties Of Bureau Of Fire Prevention =====
There is hereby established a Bureau of Fire Prevention within the Utah County Community Development Department, which Bureau shall be managed by the Community Development Director, which shall operate as the department of fire prevention when the function of such department is required by the Fire Code. The Fire Code shall be enforced by the said Bureau of Fire prevention and its officers as hereinafter set forth. There shall be the position of Fire Marshal within the Bureau of Fire Prevention, who shall serve as the “code official” or “Fire Marshal”, when the function of such officer is required by the Fire Code, and shall have the duties required in state law, the Fire Code, and other ordinances of the County appertaining to his office or required by the Fire Marshal's supervision. In addition to the position of Fire Marshal, the Bureau of Fire Prevention may include technical inspectors and other staff members, when authorized by the County Commission, who work under the direction of the Fire Marshal to assist the Fire Marshal in completing the Fire Marshal's duties. In addition to, or as a part of, the duties specified in this chapter, the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall carry out the duties required by it in the contracts which the County has made with incorporated cities for fire protection in the unincorporated area. The Bureau of Fire Prevention in carrying out the enforcement of the Fire Code, may request and shall receive in so far as may be necessary and practical, the assistance and cooperation of other officials of the County and the contracting cities. Where there are practical difficulties involved in carrying out the provisions of the Fire Code, the Fire Marshall shall have the authority to grant modifications for individual cases, provided the Fire Marshall shall first find that special individual reason makes the strict letter of the Fire Code impractical and the modification is in compliance with the intent and purpose of the Code and that such modification does not lessen health, life and fire safety requirements. The details of action granting modifications shall be recorded and entered in the files of the Bureau of Fire Protection.


===== 6.06.030 Definitions - Utah State Fire Code =====
When used in this title the following words and phrases shall have the following meanings:

“Building area” shall mean the area included within the surrounding exterior walls (or exterior walls and firewalls) exclusive of vent shafts and courts. Areas of the building not provided with surrounding walls shall be included in the building area if such areas are included within the horizontal projections of the roof or floor above.

“Chief appointing authority”, “appointing authority” or “governing body” when used in the International Fire Code, shall mean the County Commission.

“Classification of roof covering” or “roof assembly” shall refer to the classification of a covering established by the International Building Code. The three International Building Code classifications of roof allowed in Urban Interface areas are Class A, Class B and Class C.

“Cluster” shall refer to developments of not less than five (5) separate building sites or lots (except for platted mountain home developments or recreational resorts having fewer than five (5) building sites or lots for the entire development). A cluster shall be so designed as to provide that each building site within the cluster shall contain a location for a one-family dwelling which meets the standards of the International Building Code.

“Code official” when used in the Fire Code, shall mean the Fire Marshal.

“Construction” shall mean the erection, building enlargement, alteration, repairing, moving, or structure or the wiring, piping, heating, cooling, ventilation, refrigeration, sanitation, or transportation fixtures and equipment therein, or the excavation, filling or paving of land.

“Development” shall be construed to include any man-made change to improved or unimproved real estate, including but not limited to paving, excavation, drilling operations, storage of equipment or materials or landscaping.

“Fuel break” or “fire break” shall refer to a maintained area of land denoted by the thinning of
 
native vegetation, the removal of dead plant material, and the replacing of highly flammable vegetation with irrigated areas and fire resistant plants in a manner and breadth approved by the Fire Marshal.

“Fire Department” when used in the International Fire Code, shall mean the Utah County Community Development Department.

“Fire flow” shall mean the flow rate of a water supply measured at twenty (20) pounds per square inch residual pressure that is available for fire fighting.

“Heavy fuel” shall mean vegetation consisting of round wood three (3) to eight (8) inches in diameter.

“Heavy timber” shall have the meaning as defined in the International Building Code as adopted by Utah County.

“Large scale development” shall mean a large scale development as defined in the Utah County Land Use Ordinance .

“Light fuel” shall mean vegetation consisting of herbaceous plants and round wood less than 1/4 inch in diameter.

“Log wall construction” shall mean a type of construction in which the exterior walls are constructed of solid wood members and where the smallest horizontal dimension of each solid wood member is at least six (6) inches.

“Medium fuel” shall mean vegetation consisting of round wood 1⁄4 to three (3) inches in diameter.

“Temporary Tent” shall mean tents, or tensioned membrane structures, that are not erected for a period of more than one hundred eighty (180) days within a twelve (12) month period on a single premise.

“Tent” shall mean a structure, enclosure or shelter, with or without sidewalls or drops, constructed of fabric or pliable material supported by any manner except by air or the contents it protects.


===== 6.06.040 Fire Flow And Fire Sprinkler Requirements =====
Pursuant to authority granted in the Fire Code and in Utah Code § 15A-5-203, all structures shall be required to provide fire flow consistent with the International Fire Code, but the following shall be exempt from the fire flow requirements of the Fire Code (the occupancy types referred to herein under shall be defined in section 202, occupancy classification of the International Fire Code): Structures with type “U” occupancies. Public utility poles and lines. Structures built in accordance with the International Residential Code (IRC) or “R3" occupancies under six thousand (6,000) square feet of building area (any expansion or addition which causes the structure to exceed said limitation shall require that the entire structure meet the fire flow requirements) if such occupancy complies with the following: the occupancy has a fire break approved by the Fire Marshal and maintained by the owner or occupant; and the occupancy is on a lot equal to or greater than five (5) irrigated acres. The following occupancies, if under nine hundred (900) square feet of building area and separated in distance from other structures by at least twenty feet: “B”, “E”, “F-2”, “M”, and “S2". Structures built in accordance with the International Residential Code (IRC) or “R3” occupancies over six thousand (6,000) square feet and under ten thousand (10,000) square feet of building area (any expansion or addition which causes the structure to exceed said limitation shall require that the entire structure meet the fire flow requirements) if such occupancy complies with Paragraphs A3a and A3b, and has approved fire sprinklers. Structures built under the IRC or R-3 occupancies over ten thousand (10,000) square feet of building area shall be fire sprinklered in accordance with National Fire Protection Association (NFPA) standards and meet fire flow requirements. The Fire Marshal is authorized to use the current edition of NFPA 1142 as a guide when determining fire-flow in areas without a central water supply with the following stipulations: Structures shall be fire sprinklered. Water tanks for fire suppression shall be pre-designed with site-specific engineering meeting the requirements of NFPA 22. Water storage tanks for fire suppression shall have a capacity of not less than fifteen thousand (15,000) gallons. Additionally, the minimum requirements for water supplies shall be subject to increase by the Fire Prevention Bureau to compensate for circumstantial conditions such as: limited fire department resources, extended fire department response time or distance, potential delayed discovery of fire, limited access, hazardous vegetation, structural attachments such as decks and porches, unusual terrain, special uses or unusual occupancies and exposure structures. Subsections A, B, and C shall be in accordance with the IFC Section 501 as amended by the Utah State Fire Marshal’s Office. Notwithstanding the above, the Fire Marshal may require an automatic fire sprinkler system for the structures that meet any of the conditions in Utah Code § 15A-5-203, which is hereby adopted into this chapter.


===== 6.06.050 Establishment Of Limits Of Districts In Which Storage Of Explosives And Blasting Agents Is To Be Prohibited =====
The storage of explosives and blasting agents as these terms are defined in the Fire Code is prohibited in the following areas: All areas of the County lying East of the following line: Beginning at the intersection of the center of the right-of-way of Utah State Highway U-68 and the Utah County-Salt Lake County boundary line; thence Southerly along the center of the right-of-way to its intersection with the center of the right-of-way of Utah State Highway US-6; thence Easterly along the center of said right-of-way to its intersection with the center of the right-of-way of Utah State Highway I-15 at Santaquin City; thence Southerly along the center of said right-of-way to its intersections with the Utah County-Juab County boundary line; plus, the storage of explosives and blasting agents is prohibited within one (1) mile of the municipal limits of any municipality, city or town.


===== 6.06.060 Appeals =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 53-7-204, there is hereby created the Fire Code Board of Appeals of Utah County (the “Board”) to hear and decide appeals of orders, decisions or determinations from the administration and enforcement of the Fire Code as adopted and amended herein. The members of the board shall be appointed by the County Commission and shall serve until replaced or a resignation is accepted by the County Commission. The Board shall consist of three members who are qualified by training and experience to pass on such code related matters pertaining to the Fire Code and are not employees of Utah County. There must be a minimum of two (2) members to form a quorum. The chair, or the acting chair, shall conduct all hearings and may administer oaths and compel the attendance of witnesses. The Secretary of the Community Development Department shall be the Secretary of the Board and shall keep minutes of the proceedings showing the vote of each member upon each question or, if absent or failing to vote, indicating such fact; and shall keep records of its examinations and other official actions. These minutes and records, along with the appeal application, written statements, and other facts bearing on the appeal and decision of the Board, shall be filed in the office of the Board and shall be public record. The Secretary shall make an audio recording of the proceedings of the Board, and a transcription may be requested from the Secretary. The Board shall establish rules relating to such requests, including a reasonable compensation to be paid to the Secretary for making the transcription. The Secretary shall oversee the making of the transcript and shall be responsible for maintaining the integrity of the recording. Where more than one record exists or is purported to exist, a transcription caused to be prepared by the Secretary and certified to its accuracy before a notary public, shall be the official record of the proceedings of the Board. Any person or entity wishing to appeal a decision made in applying the Fire Code may commence such action by completing the standard forms and filing the forms in the office of the Secretary of the Board. The Secretary of the Board shall accept and process such forms only if they are properly completed and accompanied by the filing fee in the current amount set by the County Commission. Any appeal of a decision made in applying the Fire Code must be properly filed within twenty (20) calendar days of the date of the contested decision, or it shall be time-barred and not heard. The Board shall fix a reasonable time for hearing the appeal and decide the same. The Board shall set a standard procedure for conducting public hearings and reviewing requests before the Board, which may include: reasonable limits of time in which appellants, respondents, and other parties may speak; sign-up sheets for those who wish to speak at the public hearing (and cut off times for adding names); deadlines for submitting written comment; and other rules needed to conduct a fair and orderly hearing. The decision of the Board shall be based upon the facts and not upon expressions of support or protest, or lack of support or protest, which may be made at the hearing. Any party may appear at the hearing in person or by agent or by attorney. An appeal shall be approved only when the Board finds that all of the forms, procedures, and rules have been completed and fully complied with. To reverse any order, requirement, decision, or determination made in administering or interpreting the Fire Code it shall require the concurring vote of a majority of the members of the Board hearing the matter. When an appeal is approved, the Board shall enter into the official minutes the specific reasons for approval, any conditions or limitations of the approval, and the names of those voting for and against. If the decision of the Board is to deny an appeal, the Board shall enter into the official minutes the specific reasons for denial and the names of those voting for and against. The Board shall not grant the relief appealed for unless it finds that all of the following standards have been met: Prior to filing the appeal with the Board, the Board shall lack jurisdiction and shall not hear the appeal unless, within ten (10) calendar days of the date of the decision being appealed, the appellant provided to the Code Official, or other person or entity which made the decision in question, a claim of error which fully sets forth a brief statement of the facts, and the nature of the claim of error. Within fifteen (15) calendar days of the date of the decision being appealed, the Code Official, or other person or entity which made the decision in question may, but is not required to, respond in writing to the claim of error. The claim of error shall be deemed denied if the Code Official, or other person or entity which made the decision in question, fails to approve or deny the claim of error or otherwise respond. A copy of the claim of error, and any response received, shall be attached to the appeal application. If the Board finds that the claim of error was timely filed, the Board shall not grant the reversal or relief appealed for unless it finds that all of the following standards have been met: The appellant has filed a properly completed written application for appeal, which states with specificity the nature of the alleged error and how the appellant has been adversely affected by said alleged error. The application for appeal was properly filed with the Secretary of the Board twenty (20) calendar days or less after the date of the decision being appealed. This twenty (20) calendar day filing deadline is jurisdictional and the Board shall dismiss the appeal if the Board finds that the application for appeal filing deadline has not been met. The appellant is a party which was adversely affected by the subject decision applying the Fire Code. If the Board grants the appellant’s request, the result will be consistent with the provisions of the Fire Code, and not waive or modify any of the terms or requirements thereof. The applicant has the burden of proving that an error was made; however, expressions of support or protest alone shall not constitute the basis of approval or denial. The Board shall presume that the order, requirement, decision, or determination made in the administration or determination of the Fire Code is valid; and determine only whether or not the order, requirement, decision, or determination is arbitrary, capricious, or illegal. An order, requirement, decision, or determination is valid if the decision is supported by substantial evidence in the record and is not arbitrary, capricious, or illegal. A determination of illegality requires a determination that the order, requirement, decision, or determination violates a law, statute, or ordinance in effect at the time the order, requirement, decision, or determination was made. If there is a record, the Board’s review is limited to the record provided; and the Board may not accept or consider any evidence outside the record, unless that evidence was previously offered and it was improperly excluded. If there is no record, the Board may call witnesses and take evidence. After a decision has been made, the secretary shall file the written decision of the Board in the Community Development Office and mail a copy of the decision to the applicant at the address supplied in the application form. The decision of the Board shall be deemed final at the time it is filed in the Community Development Office. Any person adversely affected by any decision of the Board may file a petition with the Fourth District Court for Utah County for a review of that decision. Any such appeal or petition shall be barred unless it is filed within twenty (20) calendar days of the date when the decision was filed in the Community Development Office. The petition shall be limited to the allegation that the decision of the Board was arbitrary, capricious, or illegal. The Board shall transmit to the reviewing court the complete record of its proceedings, including applications, exhibits, minutes, findings, orders, and any transcript of audio recordings which may be on file with the Board. The person or entity filing the petition for review shall request from the Secretary to the Board a verbatim transcript of the record and such person or entity shall pay the reasonable transcription fees as set by the Board. If there is a record, the review of the district court is limited to the record, and the court may not accept or consider evidence outside of the record unless it determines that such evidence was offered to the Board and improperly excluded. If there is no record, the court may call witnesses and take evidence. The court shall affirm the decision of the Board if the decision is supported by substantial evidence in the record. Filing a petition for review with the court does not automatically stay the decision of the Board. Before filing the petition for review with the court, the aggrieved party may petition the Board to stay its decision. The Board shall take action on any petition to stay only in a meeting where proper notice was given and where a quorum is present. Upon considering such petition to stay, the Board may grant the stay if it finds such to be in the best interests of the County. After filing a petition for review with the court, the petitioner may seek from the court an injunction staying the decision of the Board. No decision of the Board shall be subject to rehearing by the Board, except when remanded from a court of competent jurisdiction.


===== 6.06.070 Penalties =====
Any property owner or other person, firm, corporation, partnership, or entity violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a class B misdemeanor; it shall be a separate offense for each and every day within which a violation of any provision of this chapter is committed. Upon conviction, the violator shall be punished as provided by UCC 1.04.010.


===== 6.06.080 Superseding Conflicting Ordinances =====
Consistent with Utah Code § 15A-1-403, should the provisions of the Fire Code be deemed to be in conflict with the requirements of another ordinance, the more restrictive regulations shall apply.


===== 6.06.090 Recovery Of Fire Suppression Costs =====
Any person responsible for the existence or spread of a wildland fire, or any other fire, on public or private property, necessitating suppression action shall be liable for the payment of the costs of the suppression action. The County, by and through its County Commission, may recover from any person responsible for the existence or spread of a wildland fire, or any other fire, on public or private property, the costs of the suppression action. The payment of suppression costs under this section does not constitute an admission of liability or negligence in any legal action for damages. The County may obtain assistance from the Utah State Attorney General or a municipal attorney of the affected jurisdiction to assist the County in recovering fire suppression costs.


===== 6.06.100 Exemptions Of Temporary Tents And Remote Yurts =====
This chapter shall not prohibit the use of temporary tents or membrane structures that are used solely for recreational camping. This chapter shall not prohibit the use of remote yurts as defined in Utah Code § 15A-1-204(12)(b) that meet all of the requirements of Utah Code § 15A-1-204. HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2020-572 on 7/29/2020


===== 6.07 Fire Safety Permit =====
6.07.010 Permit Required


===== 6.07.010 Permit Required =====
Consistent with Utah Code § 53-7-204, which provides for the issuance of permits, no construction shall commence on any new building or structure, no building or structure shall be moved onto property, and no building or structure shall be occupied until a Fire Safety Permit therefore has been issued by the Utah County Fire Marshal stating the conditions under which the building has been approved according to the provisions adopted by this chapter.


===== 6.08 Open Fires =====
6.08.010 Regulations For Open Fires 6.08.020 Open Fire Permits 6.08.030 Open Fire Or Smoking Areas Designated


===== 6.08.010 Regulations For Open Fires =====
Regulation for open fires shall be in accordance with the International Fire Code Section 307.


===== 6.08.020 Open Fire Permits =====
As provided in Utah Code § 53-7-204, the Fire Marshal may issue permits for open fires in a manner not inconsistent with Utah Code §§ 11-7-1 or 19-2-114. Open fire permits shall be issued in accordance with International Fire Code Section 105.6.32.


===== 6.08.030 Open Fire Or Smoking Areas Designated =====
The Fire Marshal may, and the Fire Marshal is hereby authorized in his or her discretion to, designate places where it shall be lawful or unlawful to build open fires or to light, ignite or set fire to any tobacco, cigarette, pipe, cigar or other smoking device on private property, with the permission of the owner thereof, or upon any public road, or within any public park, or upon any public land. The Fire Marshal may place or cause to be placed uniform signs or posters on or at such premises indicating the place or limits where such fires may be built and maintained, or where such smoking may take place, without further permit or permission; provided, however, that it shall be unlawful to leave or cause or permit to be left, any lighted tobacco or any fire burning in such place. Before leaving such place tobacco or fire shall be thoroughly extinguished and so completely covered with mineral earth in such manner as to adequately prevent such tobacco or fire from burning thereafter.


===== 6.09 Fireworks Act =====
6.09.010 Conformance With The Utah Fireworks Act


===== 6.09.010 Conformance With The Utah Fireworks Act =====
The sections of this chapter are intended to be adopted so as to conform to the Utah Fireworks Act, which comprises Utah Code §§ 53-7-220 through 53-7-225; all interpretations of this chapter shall be made in accord with such Act. Rules, specifications, standards or requirements promulgated by the Utah State Fire Prevention Board are included and adopted and incorporated by reference.


===== 6.10 General Fire Protection Standards =====
6.10.010 Maintenance


===== 6.10.010 Maintenance =====
The fire protection provisions required by the Fire Code and/or by this title shall be maintained by property owner and/or the applicable homeowner’s association as a condition of maintaining “adequate fire protection” in accordance with Utah Code § 11-7-1 of the Fire Code, and the protective covenants made with the County at the time of development approval. Any fire alarm system shall be the responsibility of the owner to be maintained in proper operating order. After a maximum of two (2) recorded false alarms from an alarm company or local alarm during any twelve (12) month period, the owner shall be responsible for costs of fire department and/or law enforcement response related to any additional false alarms occurring within said twelve (12) month period.


===== 6.11 Wildland/Urban Interface Areas =====
6.11.010 Scope 6.11.020 Access 6.11.030 Emergency Water Supplies 6.11.040 Structural Design And Construction 6.11.050 Manufactured Homes 6.11.060 Defensible Space Requirements 6.11.070 Storage Of Combustible Materials 6.11.080 Fireworks 6.11.090 Outdoor Fires


===== 6.11.010 Scope =====
This chapter applies to “Large Scale Developments” which include: Recreational Resorts, Mountain Home Developments and Subdivisions in the Wildland/Urban Interface lands, and structures not included within a platted Large Scale Development. The specific needs for fire prevention in these high fire prone areas are subject to the regulations of this chapter are delineated on the most recent version of the Wildland Urban Interface fire overlay map as promulgated by the State of Utah, incorporated herein by reference. The provision of both UCC 6.08 and UCC 6.09 shall apply to all areas of the Wildland/Urban Interface shown in the aforementioned map.


===== 6.11.020 Access =====
Driveways : Driveways shall be provided when any portion of an exterior wall of the first story of a building is located more than one hundred fifty (150) feet from a fire apparatus access road. Driveways shall provide a minimum unobstructed width of twelve (12) feet and a minimum unobstructed height of thirteen (13) feet six (6) inches. Driveways in excess of one hundred fifty (150) feet in length shall be provided with turnarounds. Driveways in excess of two hundred (200) feet in length and less than twenty (20) feet in width shall be provided with turnouts in addition to turnarounds. Driveway turnouts shall be all-weather surfaced road at least ten (10) feet wide and thirty (30) feet long and located not more than two hundred (200) feet apart or as approved by the County Engineer. A driveway shall not serve in excess of five (5) dwelling units. Driveway turnarounds shall have inside turning radii of not less than thirty (30) feet and outside turning radii of not less than forty-five (45) feet. Driveways that connect with a road or roads at more than one (1) point may be considered as having a turnaround if all changes of direction meet the radii requirements for driveway turnarounds. Grade : The gradient for fire apparatus access roads and driveways shall not exceed the maximum approved by the code official. It will be up to the code official to ascertain the maximum grade based on specifications of the fire equipment providing fire protection, provided that the maximum grade shall not exceed ten percent (10%) unless a steeper grade is approved by the Fire Marshal. Address Markers : All buildings shall have a permanently posted address, which shall be placed on the structure or at each driveway entrance, and be visible from both directions of travel along the road. In all cases, the address shall be posted at the beginning of construction and shall be maintained thereafter, and the address shall be visible and legible from the road on which the address is located.


===== 6.11.030 Emergency Water Supplies =====
Notwithstanding Section 507 and Appendix B of the International Fire Code and because of a greater exposure of fire in Urban Interface areas, an approved water supply capable of supplying the required fire flow for fire protection shall be provided to all premises upon which facilities, buildings, or portions of buildings are hereafter constructed or moved into clustered large scale developments in the Urban Interface. The minimum fire flow and flow duration requirements for a one family dwelling having a building area which does not exceed three thousand six hundred (3,600) square feet shall be one thousand (1,000) gallons per minute for two (2) hours. The fire flow for all other occupancies and for dwellings greater than three thousand six hundred (3,600) square feet shall be determined by the Fire Marshal using the International Fire Code as a guide. A reduction in required fire flow of up to seventy-five percent (75%), as approved by the Fire Marshal, is allowed when the building is provided with an approved automatic sprinkler system, and when an adequate vegetation clearance is maintained. Water supplies for fire sprinklers shall comply with currently adopted NFPA standards. The above fire flow requirements shall not apply to any dwelling outside of a Large Scale Development which is in the Urban Interface when provided with a fire break as defined herein and as such firebreak is maintained by the owner-occupants. Also excluded are any “U” and “S2” occupancies under nine hundred (900) square feet and farm buildings not within fifty (50) feet of a structure with habitable space. Exception : Structures intended for human occupancy over ten thousand (10,000) square feet of building area shall have approved fire flow as outlined in Paragraph B. The above fire flow requirements shall not apply to dwellings within clustered large scale developments previously approved without fire flow when provided with approved fire sprinkler systems. Water storage supplies shall likewise be calculated to provide the required daily culinary supply for each building lot plus the required fire flow for the building having the greatest demand. The fire flow requirements of this section shall not be applied in Large Scale Developments approved prior to September 15, 1978. Facilities, buildings, and structures constructed or expanded after the effective date of this chapter, in said developments, which fail to comply with the emergency water requirements of this section shall not exceed one thousand (1500) square feet of building area as defined by the International Fire Code unless a listed automatic sprinkler system in accordance with currently adopted NFPA standards is installed or adequate fire flow is provided.


===== 6.11.040 Structural Design And Construction =====
The International Fire Code is hereby amended to include the following provisions:
 New structures and additions, expansions, or alterations or remodels of more than fifty percent (50%) of building component or area being affected of existing structures in the Wildland/Urban Interface areas of Utah County shall meet the following requirements: Automatic Fire Sprinkler Systems : Automatic fire sprinkler systems shall be installed in all dwellings. All other occupancies shall have fire sprinkler systems as required by the International Fire Code. Automatic fire sprinkler systems shall comply with currently adopted NFPA standards. Roof Covering : Roofs shall have at least a Class B roof assembly or an approved noncombustible roof covering. For roof coverings where the profile allows a space between the roof covering and roof decking, the space at the eave ends shall be fire-stopped to preclude entry of flames or embers, or have one layer of seventy-two (72) pound (32.4 kg) mineral-surfaced, non- perforated cap sheet complying with ASTM D 3909 installed over the combustible decking. Roof Valleys : When provided, valley flashings shall be not less than 0.019 inch (0.48 mm) (No. 26 galvanized sheet gage) corrosion-resistant metal installed over a minimum thirty-six (36) inch-wide (914 mm) underlayment consisting of one layer of seventy-two (72) pound (32.4 kg) mineral-surfaced, non-perforated cap sheet complying with ASTM D 3909 running the full length of the valley. Existing Roof Coverings : The roof covering on buildings or structures in existence prior to the adoption of this code that are replaced or have fifty percent (50%) or more replaced in a twelve (12) month period shall be replaced with a roof covering required for new construction.
 Protection of Eaves : Combustible eaves, fascias and soffits shall be enclosed with solid materials with a minimum thickness of three quarters (3/4) inch (19 mm) or approved ignition resistant materials. No exposed rafter tails shall be permitted unless constructed of heavy timber materials. Gutters and Downspouts : Gutters and downspouts shall be constructed of noncombustible material. Gutters shall be provided with an approved means to prevent the accumulation of leaves and debris in the gutter. Exterior Walls : Exterior walls of buildings or structures shall be constructed with one of the following methods: Approved noncombustible materials. Heavy timber or log wall constructions.
 Fire-retardant-treated wood on the exterior side. The fire-retardant-treated wood shall be labeled for exterior use and meet the requirements of Section 2303.2 of the International Building Code.
 Approved building materials installed over fire-rated sheetrock or other similar noncombustible material with ignition-resistant materials on the exterior side. Such material shall extend from the top of the foundation to the underside of the roof sheathing or approved soffit.
 Unenclosed Under Floor Protection : Buildings or structures shall have all under floor areas enclosed to the ground, with exterior walls in accordance with exterior walls requirements above.
 Exception : Complete enclosure shall not be required where the underside of all exposed floors and all exposed structural columns, beams and supporting walls are protected as required for exterior one (1) hour fire- resistance-rated construction or heavy timber construction or fire- retardant-treated wood. The fire-retardant-treated wood shall be labeled for exterior use and meet the requirements of Section 2303.2 of the International Building Code.
 Appendages and Projections : Unenclosed accessory structures attached to buildings with habitable spaces and projections, such as decks, shall be constructed of, or a combination of: approved noncombustible materials; fire-retardant-treated wood identified for exterior use and meeting the requirements of Section 2303.2 of the International Building Code; heavy timber construction. The minimum required dimensions for heavy timber used in residential decks in WUI areas of unincorporated Utah County shall be as follows: posts, nominal eight by eight (8”x8”); beams, nominal six by ten (6”x10”); joists, nominal six by ten (6”x10”); one-hour fire-resistance-rated construction; or when the attached structure is located and constructed so that the structure or any portion the roof projects over a descending slope surface greater than ten percent (10%), the area below the structure shall have all under floor areas enclosed to within six (6) inches of the ground, in accordance with exterior wall construction above.
 Exterior Glazing : Exterior windows, window walls and glazed doors, windows within exterior doors, and skylights shall be tempered glass, multilayered glazed panels, glass block or have a fire-protection rating of not less than 20 minutes.
 Exterior Doors : Exterior doors shall be approved noncombustible construction, solid core wood not less than one and three quarters (1 3/4)-inches thick (45 mm), or have a fire protection rating of not less than twenty (20) minutes. Windows within doors and glazed doors shall be in accordance with Exterior glazing requirements above. Vents : Attic ventilation openings, foundation or under floor vents or other ventilation openings in vertical exterior walls and vents through roofs shall not exceed one hundred forty-four (144) square inches each. Such vents shall be covered with noncombustible corrosion-resistant mesh with openings not to exceed one quarter (1/4) inch or shall be designed and approved to prevent flame or ember penetration into the structure.
 Vent Locations : Attic ventilation openings in soffits, in eave overhangs, between rafters at eaves, or in other overhang areas, shall be fire resistant and shall be approved by the Fire Marshal. Gable end and dormer vents shall be located at least ten (10) feet from lot lines. Under floor ventilation openings shall be located as close to grade as practical. Detached Accessory Structures :
 Detached accessory structures located less than fifty (50) feet (15,240 mm) from a building containing habitable space shall have exterior walls constructed in accordance with Paragraph A5. When the detached accessory structure is located and constructed so that the structure or any portion thereof projects over a descending slope surface greater than ten percent (10%), the area below the structure shall have all under floor areas enclosed to within six (6) inches (152 mm) of the ground, with exterior wall construction in accordance with exterior wall requirements above or under floor protection in accordance with unenclosed under floor protection requirements above.
 Exception : The under floor area enclosure shall not be required where the underside of all exposed floors and all exposed structural columns, beams and supporting walls are protected as required for exterior one (1) hour fire-resistance-rated construction or heavy-timber construction or fire-retardant-treated wood on the exterior side. The fire-retardant-treated wood shall be labeled for exterior use and meet the requirements of Section 2303.2 of the International Building Code.
 Spark Arrestors : Chimneys serving fireplaces, barbecues, incinerators or decorative heating appliances in which solid or liquid fuel is used, shall be provided with a spark arrester. Spark arresters shall be constructed of woven or welded wire screening of Number 12 USA standard gage wire (0.1046 inch) (2.66 mm) having openings not exceeding one eighth (1/8) inch (12.7 mm). The net free area of the spark arrester shall not be less than four times the net free area of the outlet of the chimney.
 Exceptions to Construction Requirements in the Wildland-Urban Interface Code : Additions, alterations, or remodels to existing non-conforming structures, involving less than a fifty percent (50%) expansion, alteration, or remodel of the structure shall not be required to meet the requirements of new construction provided the additions or alterations or remodel meet or exceed the fire safety requirements of the existing structure.
 Dwellings located in an area of light fuel extending at least one half (1⁄2) mile beyond a conforming defensible space and conforming with the water supply requirements of UCC 6.11.030 shall meet the following less restrictive requirements:
 Automatic Fire Sprinkler Systems : Automatic fire sprinkler systems shall be installed in all dwellings and in all other occupancies as required by the International Fire Code. Automatic fire sprinkler systems shall comply with NFPA standards. Roof Covering : Roofs shall have at least a Class C roof assembly or an approved noncombustible roof covering. For roof coverings where the profile allows a space between the roof covering and roof decking, the space at the eave ends shall be fire-stopped to preclude entry of flames or embers, or have one layer of seventy-two (72) pound mineral-surfaced, non-perforated cap sheet complying with ASTM D 3909 installed over the combustible decking Roof Valleys : When provided, valley flashings shall be not less than 0.019 inch (0.48 mm) (No. 26 galvanized sheet gage) corrosion-resistant metal installed over a minimum thirty-six (36) inch-wide (914 mm) underlayment consisting of one layer of seventy-two (72) pound (32.4 kg) mineral-surfaced, non-perforated cap sheet complying with ASTM D 3909 running the full length of the valley. Existing Roof Coverings : The roof covering on buildings or structures in existence prior to the adoption of this code that are replaced or have fifty percent (50%) or more replaced in a twelve (12) month period shall be replaced with a roof covering required for new construction. Unenclosed Under Floor Protection : Buildings or structures shall have all under floor areas enclosed to the ground with exterior walls. Exception : Complete enclosure may be omitted where the underside of all exposed floors and all exposed structural columns, beams and supporting walls are protected as required for exterior 1-hour fire- resistance-rated construction or heavy timber construction. Gutters and Downspouts : Gutters and downspouts shall be constructed of noncombustible material. Gutters shall be provided with an approved means to prevent the accumulation of leaves and debris in the gutter. Spark Arrestors : Chimneys serving fireplaces, barbecues, incinerators or decorative heating appliances in which solid or liquid fuel is used, shall be provided with a spark arrester. Spark arresters shall be constructed of woven or welded wire screening of 12 USA standard gage wire (0.1046 inch) (2.66 mm) having openings not exceeding one half (1/2) inch (12.7 mm). The net free area of the spark arrester shall not be less than four (4) times the net free area of the outlet of the chimney. Exceptions to Construction Requirements in Light Fuel Areas of the Wildland-Urban Interface Code : Additions, alterations, or remodels to existing non-conforming structures, involving less than a fifty percent (50%) expansion, alteration, or remodel of the structure shall not be required to meet the requirements of new construction provided the additions or alterations or remodel meet or exceed the fire safety requirements of the existing structure.
 The most current edition of the International Wildland-Urban Interface Code shall, at the discretion of the Fire Marshal, be consulted for guidance in determining fire safety requirements.


===== 6.11.050 Manufactured Homes =====
Where manufactured homes are permitted, they shall meet all applicable construction and safety standards and shall be provided with full skirting constructed of noncombustible materials. Any porches and sun-decks shall be constructed of non-combustible or listed fire-retardant materials.


===== 6.11.060 Defensible Space Requirements =====
Notification : The Utah County Fire Marshal shall be notified, in writing, and a site plan submitted for approval before development or construction of any structure commences so that vegetation can be evaluated and ample clearance around structure(s) can be determined. Minimum Requirements : Unless otherwise specified by the Fire Marshal, minimum vegetation clearance will be as follows: Grass and Groundcovers : Dry or non-fire resistive grass and groundcovers shall be cleared and maintained to a maximum of five (5) inches in height within thirty (30) feet of the structure and a maximum of eighteen (18) inches in height from thirty (30) to fifty (50) feet from the structure. Areas beyond fifty (50) feet from the structure shall be determined by the fire marshal.
 Trees : Trees within fifty (50) feet of a structure shall comply with the following: Canopies of individual trees or small groups of trees shall be a minimum of ten (10) feet apart. Canopies shall not be permitted to extend within ten (10) feet from under eaves, decks, or balconies or chimney openings. Trees within fifty (50) feet of a structure shall be pruned to a height of six (6) feet above the ground, trees less than twenty (20) feet in height shall have the lower one-fourth (1/4) pruned. All trees within fifty (50) feet of a structure shall be kept free of dead or dying materials.
 Dead Vegetative Material : All dead vegetative material shall be removed and maintained clear within fifty (50) feet of any structure.
 Propane or Fuel Tanks : Ground vegetation around tanks shall be maintained no higher than inches for a minimum of ten (10) foot radius. Trees and brush shall be trimmed maintaining a minimum of three (3) feet above the tank. Fire Hydrants : Vegetation or other obstructions shall be maintained below four (4) inches high. Clearance shall be provided three (3) feet on all sides of the hydrant and extend to the roadway.
 Disposal of Flammable Vegetation and Fuels : Disposal, including chipping, burying, or removal to an approved landfill shall be completed prior to final inspection of a building permit. Open Burning of these materials is prohibited.


===== 6.11.070 Storage Of Combustible Materials =====
General : Firewood and combustible material shall not be stored in unenclosed spaces beneath buildings or structures, or on decks or under eaves, canopies or other projections or overhangs. When required by the code official, storage of firewood and combustible material stored in the defensible space shall be located a minimum of thirty (30) feet from structures and separated from the crown of trees by a minimum horizontal distance of fifteen (15) feet. Storage for Off-site Use : Firewood and combustible materials not for consumption on the premises shall be stored so as to not pose a hazard.


===== 6.11.080 Fireworks =====
Fireworks shall not be used or possessed in Wildland/Urban Interface areas.


===== 6.11.090 Outdoor Fires =====
No person shall build, ignite or maintain any outdoor fire of any kind for any purpose in or on any Wildland/Urban Interface area, except by the authority of a written permit from the code official. Exception : Outdoor fires specifically allowed by any governmental agency having jurisdiction over the land. Exception : Outdoor fires within inhabited premises or designated campsites where such fires are in a permanent barbecue, listed commercially manufactured portable barbeque, outdoor fireplace, incinerator or grill and are a minimum of thirty (30) feet from any combustible material or non-fire-resistive vegetation.
 No person shall use a permanent barbeque, portable barbeque, outdoor fireplace or grill for the disposal of rubbish, trash or combustible waste material. Incinerators, outdoor fireplaces, permanent barbeques and grills shall not be built, installed or maintained in Wildland/Urban Interface areas without approval of the code official. Openings in such appliances shall be provided with an approved spark arrestor, screen or door.
 The pile of fuel to be burned in the fire area for recreational fires shall not be larger than three (3) feet in diameter and one (1) foot in height. A garden hose connected to a water supply or other approved fire-extinguishing equipment shall be readily available for use at recreational fires. Recreational fires are required to be constantly attended by a person knowledgeable in the use of the fire-extinguishing equipment. The attendant shall supervise the burning material until the fire has been extinguished. Openings in fireplaces – including fire pits – shall be provided with an approved spark arrestor, screen or door. Fire pits shall have a flat or doomed spark screen constructed of woven or welded wire or approved metal and have openings not exceeding one half (1/2) inch.


===== 6.12 Hazardous Materials =====
6.12.010 Definitions 6.12.020 Recovery Of Expenses


===== 6.12.010 Definitions =====
The definition of terms used herein are intended to be consistent with Title 29, Chapter XVII, Part 1910, Subpart Z, Section 1910.1200(c) of the Code of Federal Regulations. Hazardous Material : "Hazardous chemical" means any chemical which is a physical hazard or a health hazard. "Health hazard" means a chemical, mixture of chemicals or a pathogen for which there is statistically significant evidence based on at least one study conducted in accordance with established scientific principles that acute or chronic health effects may occur in an exposed individual. The term "health hazard" includes chemicals which are carcinogens, toxic or highly toxic agents, reproductive toxins, irritants, corrosives, sensitizers, heptaotoxins, nephrotoxins, neurotoxins, agents which act on the hematopoietic system, and agents which damage the lungs, skin, eyes, or mucous membranes. It also includes stress due to temperature extremes. "Physical hazard" means a chemical for which there is scientifically valid evidence that it is a combustible liquid, a compressed gas, explosive, flammable, an organic peroxide, an oxidizer, pyrophoric, unstable (reactive) or water-reactive. "Hazardous material incident" means a sudden and unexpected release or threat of release of any substance that because of its quantity, concentration, or physical, chemical, or infectious characteristics presents a direct threat to health, public safety, the environment, or property. "Expenses" means direct and indirect costs of labor, equipment, administrative overhead, governmental agencies, private contractors, contract labor and materials, disposable and damaged items associated with the response, containment, control, and mitigation of a hazardous material incident.


===== 6.12.020 Recovery Of Expenses =====
The County may recover from those persons whose actions caused the costs of a hazardous materials emergency response incurred by the County or any of its agencies directly associated with the response to a hazardous materials incident taken under authority of this chapter or any other applicable law. The payment of expenses under this section does not constitute an admission of liability or negligence in any legal action for damages. The County may obtain assistance from the Utah State Attorney General or a municipal attorney of the affected jurisdiction to assist the County in recovering expenses and legal fees. Any recovered costs shall be deposited in the general fund of the County. 
 7 PUBLIC PEACE / MORALS / WELFARE 7.01 Miscellaneous Offenses 7.02 Weapons 7.03 Large Public Gatherings 7.04 Curfew Ordinance 7.05 Use Of County Roads And County Property 7.06 Lewdness 7.07 Camping 7.08 County Trails 7.09 Nuisances 7.10 Cleaning Of Real Property 7.11 Utah County Weed Control 7.12 Unreasonable Noise 7.13 Utah County Criminal Justice Coordinating Council


===== 7.01 Miscellaneous Offenses =====
7.01.010 Representation Of Articles Sold At Auction 7.01.020 Boosters At Auctions 7.01.030 Resisting Or Obstructing A Peace Officer 7.01.040 Obstructing Service Of Process 7.01.050 Refusing To Aid An Officer 7.01.060 Personating A Public Officer 7.01.070 Sale Of Tobacco To Minors 7.01.080 Purchase Or Possession Of Tobacco By Minors 7.01.090 Disposition Of Stolen Property 7.01.100 Prohibited Conduct In Hospitals, Treatment Facilities 7.01.110 County Sheriff To Designate Unsafe Areas 7.01.120 Entrance Upon Unsafe Areas Prohibited 7.01.130 Items Prohibited At Utah County Jail 7.01.140 Penalties 7.01.150 Prohibited Targets


===== 7.01.010 Representation Of Articles Sold At Auction =====
All auction sales and all persons participating in sales must truly and correctly represent at all times to the public attending such auction the facts in respect to quality of merchandise being sold.


===== 7.01.020 Boosters At Auctions =====
No person shall act in any sale by auction as a by-bidder or "booster" to bid on behalf of the auctioneer or owner, to run up the price of the article to be sold, or to make any false bid.


===== 7.01.030 Resisting Or Obstructing A Peace Officer =====
A person who knowingly resists or obstructs the performance, by one known to the person to be a peace officer, of any authorized act within the peace officer’s official capacity, shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.040 Obstructing Service Of Process =====
A person who knowingly resists or obstructs the authorized service or execution of any civil or criminal process or order of any court shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.050 Refusing To Aid An Officer =====
A person who, upon command, refuses or knowingly fails reasonably to aid a person known by him to be a peace officer in apprehending a person whom the officer is authorized to apprehend, or to prevent the commission by another of any offense, shall be fined not to exceed one hundred dollars ($100.00).


===== 7.01.060 Personating A Public Officer =====
Any person who falsely personates a public officer, sheriff, deputy sheriff, justice of the peace, coroner or notary public or other peace officer of any character whatsoever, and in such assumed character arrests or detains or attempts or threatens to arrest or detain, or otherwise intimidates or searches the person, building or other property of any person, or obtains money, or property or other thing of value, shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.070 Sale Of Tobacco To Minors =====
Any person who sells, gives or furnishes any cigar, cigarette, or tobacco in any form, to any person under the age of twenty-one (21) years is guilty of a class C misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.080 Purchase Or Possession Of Tobacco By Minors =====
Any person under the age of twenty-one (21) years who buys, accepts or has in that person’s possession any cigar, cigarette, or tobacco, in any form, shall be guilty of an infraction.


===== 7.01.090 Disposition Of Stolen Property =====
Definition : For purposes of this chapter:

"Stolen property" is defined as all property held by the County Sheriff as part of a criminal investigation into the theft or robbery of goods or related offenses. Duty to investigate : The County Sheriff shall investigate all goods reported stolen by theft or deception and may seize the same pending the completion of the criminal investigation. Return of property : All property seized by the County Sheriff as part of a criminal investigation shall be returned in accordance with any court orders governing the disposition of the goods in the criminal proceeding or as stated in Utah Code Title 24, Utah Code Chapter 77-24a, or applicable successor statutes.


===== 7.01.100 Prohibited Conduct In Hospitals, Treatment Facilities =====
Possession of unauthorized alcohol, intoxicants, drugs, etc. : It shall be unlawful for any person to take any unauthorized alcohol, intoxicant, and/or unauthorized drugs of any kind or description into any treatment facility or hospital, any portion of which is used for treatment of persons for alcoholism or addiction to drugs. Making unauthorized substances available to persons under treatment : It shall be unlawful for any person to make available to any other person who is under treatment for alcoholism, or drug addiction, any alcoholic substance, any intoxicant, or unauthorized drug when the person providing the substances knows or should know that the recipient is under treatment for alcoholism or drug addiction. Trespass : It shall be unlawful for any person to trespass within any hospital or treatment facility, any portion of which is used for the care and treatment of persons for alcoholism or drug addiction. Any such person shall be deemed to be trespassing when the person remains on the premises after having been requested to leave. Firearms, dangerous weapons : It shall be unlawful for any person, other than a peace officer or other authorized person, to take a firearm of any description or any other dangerous weapon into, or onto the grounds of any hospital or treatment facility, any portion of which is used for treatment of persons for alcoholism or drug addiction. Unauthorized drug, intoxicant, defined : For purposes of this section, an unauthorized drug or intoxicant will be any drug, intoxicant, or similar substance not prescribed by a physician for the use of the person being treated at the subject facility or hospital. Violation; penalty : Any violation of this section shall be deemed a class B misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.110 County Sheriff To Designate Unsafe Areas =====
The County Sheriff may designate any area of ground in the County as unsafe. The County Sheriff in determining that any area is unsafe shall take into consideration:
 the condition of the weather; the location of said area; the condition of the snow in respect to the likelihood of snowslides being caused by reason of persons going upon or within close proximity of the same; and any other hazard or dangerous condition that exists or is likely thereafter to exist.


===== 7.01.120 Entrance Upon Unsafe Areas Prohibited =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to knowingly and intentionally go upon any area of ground designated by the County Sheriff to be unsafe.


===== 7.01.130 Items Prohibited At Utah County Jail =====
Except as provided by jail policy or rule, the following items may not be possessed at the Utah County Jail or transported to or upon the Utah County Jail: any type of tobacco product; any match, lighter, or other device capable of igniting a fire; any drug paraphernalia; any item which could be used as a weapon and which is not already prohibited by Utah Code § 76-8-311.3; or any pornographic material as defined in Utah Code 76-10 Part 12.


===== 7.01.140 Penalties =====
Any person who knowingly and intentionally possesses at the Utah County Jail or transports to or upon the Utah County Jail any item set forth in UCC 7.01.130 shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 7.01.150 Prohibited Targets =====
No person within the unincorporated area of the County, including state and federal public lands, shall use or possess with intent to use as a target any object, either solid, liquid, vapor, or particulate, that will shatter, break apart, fragment, ignite, or explode, that may create a hazard or nuisance to any persons, property, public lands, wildlife, or livestock. This section does not apply to:
 any objects used as targets commonly referred to as clay pigeons, sporting clays, or objects of a similar nature; and any private property owner on the owner’s property, or any person on the private property owner’s property in possession of written permission from the property owner to engage in recreational shooting activities on the property.


===== 7.02 Weapons =====
7.02.010 Adoption Of Utah Code Chapter 76-10 Part 5: Weapons 7.02.020 Children To Be Accompanied By Adults While Hunting With Or Discharging Any Weapon 7.02.030 Discharge Of Weapons 7.02.040 Night Hunting Restricted; Exceptions, Penalties


===== 7.02.010 Adoption Of Utah Code Chapter 76-10 Part 5: Weapons =====
Utah Code §§ 76-10-501 to 524: Weapons, is hereby expressly adopted as a Utah County Ordinance. Any part of said code which cannot be legally imposed for a violation of a county ordinance is not adopted.


===== 7.02.020 Children To Be Accompanied By Adults While Hunting With Or Discharging Any Weapon =====
As used in this section, "accompanied" means at a distance within which visual and verbal communication is maintained for the purposes of advising and assisting. A person under the age of fourteen (14) years must be accompanied by the person’s parent or legal guardian, or other responsible person of the age of twenty-one (21) years or older and approved by the person’s parent or guardian, while hunting with, or discharging, any weapon. A person of at least fourteen (14) years of age and under sixteen (16) years of age must be accompanied by the person’s parent or legal guardian, or other responsible person of the age of twenty-one (21) years or older and approved by the person’s parent or guardian while hunting big game with any weapon. A person of at least fourteen (14) years of age and under sixteen (16) years of age must be accompanied by a person of the age of twenty-one (21) years or older while hunting wildlife other than big game with any weapon or while discharging any weapon. A person under the age of twelve (12) years is not permitted to hunt for protected wildlife except as provided by rules of the Utah Wildlife Board.


===== 7.02.030 Discharge Of Weapons =====
A person may not, without written permission from the owner or other person in charge, discharge a firearm across, into, or within six hundred (600) feet of: a house or dwelling; any structure where an animal is kept or fed including a barn, poultry yard, corral, feeding pen, or stockyard; or any public park, orchard, standing cornfield, standing silo, vehicle, or farm equipment.
 Hunting of any wildlife or the discharging of any firearm is prohibited within the boundaries of all state parks except those designated by the Division of Parks and Recreation (R651-603-5 Utah Administrative Code). Hunting with or discharging a rifle, handgun, or a muzzle-loader in, or within one mile of all state park facilities designated "open"—including buildings, camp or picnic sites, overlooks, golf courses, boat ramps, and developed beaches—is prohibited. Hunting with or discharging shotguns and archery tackle is prohibited within one quarter of a mile of the above stated park areas.
 Nothing herein shall prohibit the possession and use of firearms at an approved shooting range during the appropriate hours of operation.


===== 7.02.040 Night Hunting Restricted; Exceptions, Penalties =====
Pursuant to Utah Code § 23-13-17, hunting at night is permitted under the following conditions: The hunting shall be only for coyote, red fox, striped skunk, or raccoon and no other form of terrestrial or avian wildlife. Any artificial light used to spotlight the animal must be carried by the hunter. A motor vehicle headlight or light attached to or powered by a motor vehicle may not be used to spotlight the animal. While hunting with the use of an artificial light, the hunter may not occupy or operate a motor vehicle. The only firearm permitted for night hunting will be a shotgun using shot that is #2 size or smaller. The term "spotlight" or "spotlighting" means casting the rays of any artificial light on any highway, in any field, woodland, forest, or other land while having in possession a weapon by which protected wildlife may be killed. The term "motor vehicle" shall have the meaning as defined in Utah Code § 41-6a-102. Nothing in this section shall prevent any landowner or the landowner’s agent from using an artificial light, or reasonably and carefully discharging a firearm at any time on lands under the owner’s control for the purpose of protecting crops or domestic animals, so long as the owner does not endanger human life. Nothing in this section shall prevent any peace officer, conservation officer, or animal damage control agent in the official performance of the officer’s duties from the use of an artificial light or discharging a firearm at any time. “Nighttime” or “night”, within the meaning of this section, shall be that time from one- half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise. Any person who violates any provision of this section shall be guilty of an infraction. Nothing in this section shall prevent a person from night hunting for the express and limited purpose of conducting bona fide scientific research and study sanctioned by the Utah State Division of Wildlife Resources or the U.S. Division of Wildlife Resources, when said person shall have first obtained written approval from the office of the County Sheriff. Nothing herein shall grant any person the right to trespass upon private, county, state, or federal property.


===== 7.03 Large Public Gatherings =====
7.03.010 In General 7.03.020 Permit HISTORY Amended by Ord. 2023-666 Updated Large Public Gatherings on 6/28/2023


===== 7.03.010 In General =====
Definition : For the purposes of this chapter, these words and phrases shall have the following definitions: "Gathering" shall mean a group or company of persons gathered together at any location at any single time for any purpose. "Large public gathering" shall mean an actual or reasonably anticipated gathering of two hundred and fifty (250) up to nine-hundred-ninety-nine (999) people which continues or can reasonably be expected to continue for two (2) or more consecutive hours. Enforcement and Penalty : The provisions of this chapter may be enforced by injunction in any court of competent jurisdiction. The holding of a gathering in violation of any provisions or condition contained in this chapter shall be deemed a threat to health, safety, and welfare of the public and public nuisance under both Utah Code § 76B-6-1101 and Utah County Code 7.09 and may be immediately abated as such. Any person, including, but not limited to, the applicant(s), event manager(s)/operator(s), and property owner(s), who violates the provisions of this chapter shall be jointly and severely liable and guilty of a class B misdemeanor, with a mandatory minimum criminal penalty of $950, and a civil fine not to exceed $1,000. Every 30 minutes after two hours for which the gathering continues to be in violation of this chapter shall constitute an additional count for each criminal and civil offense. Every individual over 250 shall constitute an additional count for each criminal and civil offense. For all criminal violations, the County is required under Utah Code 788-22-301 to provide for indigent defense services. Exceptions : The provisions of this chapter shall not apply to: a religious gathering at any regularly established, pennanent place of worship, stadium, athletic field, arena, auditorium, coliseum, or other similar permanently established gathering place which do not exceed the maximum capacity of the facility where the gathering is held;
 government-sponsored fairs held on regularly established fairgrounds; or spontaneous or unexpected gatherings. Applicability : This chapter shall only apply in unincorporated areas of Utah County, on both private and public property.
 Maximum Number of People Assembled : A permit issued under the provisions of this chapter shall permit the gathering of only the maximum number of people stated in the permit. The permittee shall not sell tickets to nor allow more than the maximum permissible number of people to gather at the permitted location. All gatherings of one thousand (1,000) people or more shall comply with Utah Administrative Code R392-400.


===== 7.03.020 Permit =====
Required : No person shall permit, maintain, conduct, undertake, or manage a large public gathering, whether on public or private property, unless a permit to hold the gathering has first been issued by Utah County. Gatherings with fewer people or of a shorter duration do not require a permit but are subject to other applicable laws and ordinances, including nuisance laws.
 Application : Application for a permit to hold a large public gathering shall be made in writing to Utah County Public Works at least thirty (30) days prior to such gathering.
 The application shall contain a statement made upon oath or affirmation that the statements contained therein are true and correct to the best knowledge of the applicant and shall be signed and sworn to or affirmed by the individual submitting the application. In all cases, the individual submitting the application shall have authority to sign the application and bind the applicant and its organization or entity, if applicable, and the property owner(s).  The minimum facility requirements shall be listed in the permit application, which shall contain and disclose:
 the name, mailing address, telephone number, and email of each applicant(s) and event manager(s)/operator(s);
 the number of people expected to attend the gathering; a description of the type of gathering to be held with the date range and times the gathering will be held;
 estimated length of stay of attendees; name, address, telephone number, and email of the property owner(s); the location of the gathering and a site plan delineating the area where the gathering is to be held including the following: the parking area available for patrons; location of entrance, exit, and interior roadways and walkways; location, type, and provider of restroom facilities; location and description of water stations; location and number of food stands, and the types of food to be served if known; location, number, type, and provider of solid waste containers; location of manager's/operator's headquarters at the gathering;
 a plan to provide lighting adequate to ensure the safety of attendees and staff; location and size of each parking area designated for the gathering and under the manager's/operator's control and appropriate parking and traffic control measures to be taken; and the location of each first aid station and emergency medical provider; the name of the solid and liquid waste haulers with whom the manager(s)/operator(s) has contracted, unless exempted; a site clean-up plan after the gathering;
 the total number and qualifications for first aid station personnel; an emergency medical services operational plan and contact information of the emergency medical provider;
 a plan for directional and exit signs; a plan developed by the manager(s)/operator(s) to address nuisance or health and safety hazards associated with animals present at the gathering;
 a plan to address hazardous conditions, including a contingency plan for evacuation, cancellation, or delay of the gathering;
 the plans of the applicant to limit the maximum number of people permitted to gather;
 the plans for fencing the location of the gathering and the gates contained in such fence or other plans to confine the area of the gathering;
 the plans for camping facilities, if any, including facilities available and their location;
 the plans for security including the number of guards, their deployment, and their names, addresses, credentials and hours of availability, as approved by the Utah County Sheriffs Office;
 the plans for serving alcohol, if any, and a copy of the appropriate alcohol license;
 the plans for fire protection including the number, type, and location of all protective devices including alarms and extinguishers, and the number of emergency fire personnel available to operate the equipment; and the plans applicable Utah County Health Department permits for food concessions and concessions who will be allowed to operate on the grounds including the names and addresses of all concessionaires and their license or permit numbers. The application shall include the deposit required and permit fee. The requirements set forth in this section are the minimum facility and plan requirements that shall be met and maintained for the duration of the gathering. Fee : The fee for each permit to hold a large public gathering shall be five hundred dollars ($500.00) for each day.
 Refundable Deposit : Before any permit shall be issued under the provisions of this section, a refundable deposit must be made to the Utah County Public Works Department in the amount of two thousand dollars ($2000.00). The County reserves the right to retain the deposit for actual costs incurred for clean-up, abatement, or law enforcement response related to the large public gathering. If no costs are incurred by the County, the deposit shall be promptly returned to the applicant after the conclusion of the gathering and upon written request of the applicant.
 Upon request of the applicant, the County Commission may waive this requirement if the applicant is a non-profit organization, and the gathering would contribute to the safety, health, prosperity, moral well-being, peace, order, comfort, or convenience of the County's inhabitants.
 Issuance : The application of a permit required by the provisions of this chapter shall be processed within twenty (20) days of receipt and shall be issued if all conditions are complied with. 
 Effect of Issuance : A permit to hold a large public gathering issued to one person shall permit any person to engage in any lawful activity in connection with the holding of the permitted gathering. 
 Revocation : The permit issued under the provisions of this chapter may be revoked by the County at any time if any of the conditions of the permit are not complied with. 
 Review : In the event a permit is not granted, the applicant(s), event manager(s)/operator(s), or property owner(s) has the right to have the denial reviewed by the Utah County Commission upon written request to the Utah County Commission within five (5) days after the permit application has been denied.


===== 7.04 Curfew Ordinance =====
7.04.010 Purpose 7.04.020 Definitions 7.04.030 Sixteen-Year-Old Curfew 7.04.040 Eighteen-Year-Old Curfew 7.04.050 Parental Liability 7.04.060 Exceptions 7.04.070 Enforcement


===== 7.04.010 Purpose =====
The County Commission finds, due to a seemingly ever-increasing incidence of violence and other crime among juveniles in the County, being both drug related and gang related, that such crimes may be significantly inhibited and reduced by the enactment and enforcement of local law establishing a curfew prohibiting juveniles from remaining idly and purposelessly on the public streets late at night.


===== 7.04.020 Definitions =====
"Care and custody" means the legal authority of a parent or guardian to supervise or otherwise be responsible for a minor, or the express authority given from such parent or legal guardian of a minor to a responsible adult to supervise or otherwise be responsible for the activities and care of the minor.
