Any person or entity violating the provisions of this chapter, either by failing to do those acts required herein, or by doing any act prohibited herein, is guilty of a class C misdemeanor punishable in accordance with the provisions of state statute that define the fines, imprisonment or other penalties that apply to a conviction of a class C misdemeanor. 9 PUBLIC SERVICES 9.01 Purpose And Cross Connections Of Water Systems Operated By Utah County 9.02 Sewers 9.03 Open Wells, Cesspools, Privy Vaults 9.04 Solid Waste 9.05 Septic Tank, Cesspool Cleaners 9.06 Agriculture Protection Area


===== 9.01 Purpose And Cross Connections Of Water Systems Operated By Utah County =====
9.01.010 Purpose 9.01.020 Cross Connections 9.01.030 Cross Connection Control Or Elimination 9.01.040 Cross Connection Enforcement 9.01.050 Cross Connection Hazard Assessment Surveys 9.01.060 Termination Of Service


===== 9.01.010 Purpose =====
The purpose of this chapter is to protect the water supply systems operated by the County (Hereinafter “public drinking water system(s)”) from contamination or pollution from any cross connections existing or potential; and to assure that approved backflow prevention assemblies are tested when put into service and at least on an annual basis thereafter. This ordinance is in compliance with Section R309.102.5 of the Utah Public Drinking Water Rules (“UPDWR”)as enacted or hereinafter amended and the Uniform Plumbing Code (“UPC”) as adopted or hereinafter amended by the State of Utah.


===== 9.01.020 Cross Connections =====
The installation or maintenance of any unprotected cross connection which would endanger or permit the contamination or pollution of the water supply of public drinking water systems operated by the County is prohibited. Any such cross connection now existing or hereafter installed is hereby declared unlawful and shall be immediately protected or eliminated.


===== 9.01.030 Cross Connection Control Or Elimination =====
The control or elimination of cross connections and the criteria for determining degree of hazard and prescribing appropriate levels of protection shall comply with the UPC and the UPDWR as presently enacted or hereafter amended.


===== 9.01.040 Cross Connection Enforcement =====
Water service from public drinking water systems to any premises shall be contingent upon the customer providing appropriate cross connection control if required by UCC 9.01.030. Cross connection control determinations and enforcement shall be the responsibility of the County Public Works Department. In addition to criminal penalties, water service may be refused or terminated to any premises where an unprotected cross connection may allow contamination or pollutants to backflow into the public drinking water system.


===== 9.01.050 Cross Connection Hazard Assessment Surveys =====
Authorized employees of the County, with proper identification, shall have free access at reasonable hours of the day to all areas of a premise or building to which drinking water is supplied from a public drinking water system for the purpose of conducting cross connection hazard assessment surveys. In addition to criminal penalties, water service from a public drinking water system may be refused or terminated, or maximum backflow protection may be required, to a premise where access to perform cross connection hazard assessment surveys is denied, where unprotected cross connections are located, or in the event that installed assemblies are not tested and maintained as required by State and local regulations, including but not limited to the UPC and the UPDWR.


===== 9.01.060 Termination Of Service =====
Before any water service from a public drinking water system is terminated, the customer will be notified by personal contact or in writing addressed to the customer’s last known address providing a reasonable time for compliance prior to termination. However, in the event of an actual backflow incident which endangers the public health or contaminates or pollutes a public drinking water system, water service may be terminated immediately and shall not be restored until the cross connection is either eliminated or adequately protected in accordance with this chapter.


===== 9.02 Sewers =====
9.02.010 Definitions 9.02.020 Permit Required 9.02.030 Application For Permit 9.02.040 Plans, Specifications 9.02.050 Compliance With Permit


===== 9.02.010 Definitions =====
For the purposes of this chapter, the following words and phrases shall have the meanings respectively ascribed to them:

"Disposal system" means a system for disposing of wastes, and includes sewerage system and treatment works.

"Sewerage system" means pipelines or conduits, pumping stations and all other constructions, devices, appurtenances, and facilities used for collecting or conducting wastes to a point of ultimate disposal.

"Treatment works" means any plant, disposal field, lagoon, dam, pumping station, incinerator, or other works used for the purpose of treating, stabilizing, or holding wastes.


===== 9.02.020 Permit Required =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to construct or add to a sewerage system or treatment works in this County without securing a permit from the County Building Inspector and the County Health Department.


===== 9.02.030 Application For Permit =====
The application for a permit required by UCC 9.02.020 shall be accompanied by a set of detail plans showing the area to be served, the location of all proposed sewers, the location of proposed treatment works, and the location and source of public and semipublic water supplies.


===== 9.02.040 Plans, Specifications =====
Plans and specifications for work coming under the provisions of this chapter shall be in accordance with the "Standards for Sewage Works" of the State Water Pollution Control Board.


===== 9.02.050 Compliance With Permit =====
The County Health Department shall have the right to refuse to issue said permit unless all rules, regulations and provisions of the law have been complied with; and any person proceeding without such permit shall be guilty of a class C misdemeanor.


===== 9.03 Open Wells, Cesspools, Privy Vaults =====
9.03.010 Open Holes Prohibited 9.03.020 Duty To Fill 9.03.030 County May Fill 9.03.040 County To Recover Cost


===== 9.03.010 Open Holes Prohibited =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to maintain, permit or suffer to exist or be maintained any uncovered or open well or open hole or cesspool upon any property situated in the County.


===== 9.03.020 Duty To Fill =====
It shall be the duty of the owner, occupant, or tenant of any property situated in the County upon which any uncovered or open well or open hole or cesspool has been maintained or has existed to remove all contaminants and fill in the same.


===== 9.03.030 County May Fill =====
In the event of the failure or refusal of any owner, occupant, or tenant of any property situated in the County to comply with the provisions of this chapter, the County shall have the right by its officials, agents, servants, or employees to enter upon said land, and remove all contaminants and fill in any such uncovered or open well or open hole or cesspool as herein required.


===== 9.03.040 County To Recover Cost =====
If County must remove contaminants or fill in any uncovered or open well, open hole, or cesspool as provided in this chapter, the owner of the property shall be liable for all costs incurred by County.


===== 9.04 Solid Waste =====
9.04.010 Definitions 9.04.020 Enforcement And Supervision 9.04.030 Complaints 9.04.040 Powers Of Condemnation 9.04.050 Fees For The Use Of County Landfill Areas 9.04.060 Dumping Illegal 9.04.070 Waste Disposal Methods Condemned 9.04.080 Burning Of Waste 9.04.090 Receptacles For Transportation 9.04.100 Spilling On Streets And Property 9.04.110 Delay On Streets 9.04.120 Receptacles Required 9.04.130 Acceptable Refuse Receptacles 9.04.140 Misuse Of Receptacles 9.04.150 Littering 9.04.160 Private Storage Of Garbage And Refuse 9.04.170 Uncovered Garbage 9.04.180 Wind-Blown Refuse 9.04.190 Accumulation Of Refuse On Premises 9.04.200 Removal Of Refuse 9.04.210 Dead Animals - Prompt Removal Required 9.04.220 Dead Animals - Deposit In Water Illegal


===== 9.04.010 Definitions =====
For the purposes of this chapter, the following words and phrases shall have the meanings respectively ascribed to them:

"Cinders" means the residue of materials burned in boilers, furnaces and heating plants of public buildings, places of business, apartment houses, hotels, and manufacturing plants.

"Community waste" means lawn cuttings, clippings from bushes and shrubs, leaves, sweepings from yards, tin cans, boiler ashes, newspapers, magazines, cardboard cartons and stove ashes, but not building materials.

"Garbage" means swill and all animal, vegetable, and food refuse from kitchens of residences, hotels, cafes restaurants and places where food is prepared for human consumption.

"Market waste" means condemned or decayed or unsound vegetables, meat, fish and fruit, and all waste and offal thereof from markets, stores and factories, and all vegetable waste from such markets, stores, and factories.

"Night soil" means the contents from privy vaults, cesspools, septic tanks, grease tanks, and water closets.

"Place of business" means any place in the county in which there is conducted or carried on principally or exclusively any pursuit or occupation by any person or persons for the purpose of gaining a livelihood.

"Public buildings and places" means indoor or outdoor areas, whether privately or publicly owned, to which persons not employed at the place have general and regular access by right or by invitation, express or implied, whether by payment of money or not, including office buildings, theaters, garages, auto camps, hotels, clubs, churches, schools, hospitals, or other places of similar character.

"Refuse" means combustible trash, including, but not limited to, paper, cartons, boxes,
 
barrels, wood, excelsior, tree branches, yard trimmings, wood furniture, bedding; noncombustible trash, including but not limited to, metal, tin cans, metal furniture, dirt, small quantities of rock and pieces of concrete, glass, crockery, other mineral wastes; street rubbish, including, but not limited to, street sweepings, dirt, leaves, catch basin dirt, contents of litter receptacles. Refuse shall not, however, include earth and wastes from building operations, nor shall it include solid wastes resulting from building operations, nor shall it include solid wastes resulting from industrial processes and manufacturing operations, such as food-processing wastes, boiler house cinders, lumber, scraps, and shavings.

"Residences" means buildings or dwellings comprising not more than three (3) dwelling units designed for separate housekeeping tenements and where no business of any kind is conducted except such home occupations as are defined in the Zoning Ordinances of the County. Condominiums shall be deemed residences for the purposes of this chapter.

"Scavenger" means any person who engages in the business of collecting, carrying, transporting, or hauling garbage, market waste, trade waste, dead animals, night soil, or other refuse.

"Solid waste" means any garbage, refuse, sludge (including sludge from the waste treatment plant, water supply treatment plant, or air pollution control facility) or other discarded material, including solid, liquid, semisolid or contained gaseous material resulting from industrial, commercial, mining, or agricultural operations and from community activities, but does not include solid or dissolved materials in domestic sewage or in irrigation return flows or discharges.

"Stove ashes" means the residue of material burned in stoves and furnaces in private residences, but not the residue from furnaces in apartment houses, hotels, business houses, heating, or manufacturing plants.

"Trade waste" means all discarded wooden boxes, barrels, broken lumber, cardboard boxes, cartons, waste papers, leather, rubber, packing materials, cuttings, sweepings, rags and other flammable waste materials and all discarded trade or manufacturing refuse form stores, factories or other places of business which are not included within the definition of garbage, stove ashes, and market waste.


===== 9.04.020 Enforcement And Supervision =====
All scavenger works shall be subject to the direction and control of the County Health Department, and the County Health Department shall enforce the provisions of this chapter in reference to garbage and the collection thereof. The County Health Department shall have authority to issue and require permits and establish appropriate fees to direct and control the provisions of this chapter.


===== 9.04.030 Complaints =====
It shall be the duty of the County Health Department, or other appointed official, to answer and investigate all complaints and, when necessary in its judgment, to declare the unsanitary condition a public nuisance.


===== 9.04.040 Powers Of Condemnation =====
It shall be the duty of the County Health Department, or other appointed official, to declare worn-out, rusted, or filthy containers, as described in this chapter, public nuisances; and it shall be unlawful for any person, group of persons or commercial establishment to continue to use said containers. It shall be the duty of such person, persons or commercial establishment to clean or replace said containers with adequate containers as described in this chapter.


===== 9.04.050 Fees For The Use Of County Landfill Areas =====
Fees and charges associated with the use of County owned or County operated landfills shall be adopted by the County Commission, and the amount thereof shall be set at the discretion of the County Commissioners.


===== 9.04.060 Dumping Illegal =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to sweep or deposit garbage, paper, or other rubbish into any gutter or ditch within the county limits, or to empty any swill, house slops, or other filthy refuse in any gutter or ditch within the county limits. It shall be unlawful to deposit or dispose of used petroleum products or other chemical waste in any gutter, ditch, or body of water in the county. It shall also be unlawful for any person to dump any refuse, garbage, paper, trash, junk, market waste, trade waste, night soil, used petroleum products or other hazardous chemical waste on any premises in the county, except in an area designated and approved by the County Health Department.


===== 9.04.070 Waste Disposal Methods Condemned =====
It shall be unlawful to drain, empty, or discharge any of the contents of any cesspool, septic tank, house sink, or any other receptacle receiving human wastes into any open ditch, open or closed drain, open or closed irrigation stream, or into any other body of water, or onto the surface of the ground. Whenever in the opinion of the health officer any privy, privy vault, cesspool, or septic tank has become a menace to health, the Health Department shall have the power to order such privy, septic tank, or cesspool disinfected or filled in and abandoned.


===== 9.04.080 Burning Of Waste =====
It shall be unlawful to burn any garbage, market waste or other organic or offensive refuse in the open air or in any furnace or stove within the county. It shall be unlawful to burn papers, excelsior, trade waste, or any other material which may be blown about by the wind anywhere in the county unless the same is burned in a stove, fireplace, furnace, or in an incinerator approved by the Utah Air Quality Board.


===== 9.04.090 Receptacles For Transportation =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to haul, convey, or transport through or upon any of the public streets any garbage, ashes, market wastes, trade wastes, night soil, loose papers, scrap lumber, excelsior, tree limbs, bush clippings, lawn clippings, house refuse, yard refuse, liquid wastes, or any other refuse materials in open trucks, open trailers, or other open conveyances, unless covered completely with a heavy-duty canvas or other heavy acceptable material at all times when the vehicle is being used for the collection of, or carrying, transporting, or hauling of garbage, market waste, night soil, dead animals, or other refuse, and is to be driven for a distance of five (5) blocks or more without making a stop for collection.


===== 9.04.100 Spilling On Streets And Property =====
It shall be unlawful for any person engaged in the collection and transportation of garbage, trade waste, rubbish, or other matter of any kind, to permit, allow, or cause any of said matter to fall or remain on any property, place, building, premises, street, road or highway. It shall be the duty of any person engaged in the transportation of garbage, market waste, trade waste, rubbish or matter of any kind to see and ensure that all such matter is procured and disposed of by that person, or that person’s agents or employees, in a manner which shall not be offensive or filthy in relation to any person, place, building, premises, street, road, or highway.


===== 9.04.110 Delay On Streets =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to suffer, permit or allow any vehicle loaded with garbage, slop, swill, market waste, or other refuse to be or remain standing upon any public street within the county, any longer than may be necessary for the purpose of loading and transporting the same.


===== 9.04.120 Receptacles Required =====
It shall be the duty of every owner, lessee, or occupant of any building, premises, or place of business to provide:
 suitable and sufficient metallic, plastic, or other approved receptacles with tight- fitting lids for receiving and holding garbage, market waste, and other wet refuse that may accumulate from said building, place of business, or upon said premises or the portion thereof under the control of such person; and suitable and sufficient receptacles for receiving and holding trade waste and other dry refuse that may accumulate from said building, place of business, or upon said premises or the portion thereof under the control of such person.


===== 9.04.130 Acceptable Refuse Receptacles =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to deposit garbage or other wet refuse in wooden boxes, barrels, or pasteboard cartons. Trade waste and dry refuse may be deposited in wooden boxes, pasteboard cartons, barrels, or similar receptacles; provided, such receptacles shall be of adequate construction to prevent garbage from being scattered from faulty bottoms, etc. Such receptacles must have wide necks and mouths to prevent clogging and littering. All pasteboard boxes shall be disposed of with their contents. Garbage and refuse of every kind may, however, be disposed of in properly and sufficiently treated weather-resistant bags manufactured specifically for use in garbage and refuse collection and having a twenty (20) to thirty (30) gallon capacity for receiving and holding garbage, market waste, and other refuse.


===== 9.04.140 Misuse Of Receptacles =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to willfully or negligently break, deface, or injure any receptacle used to contain garbage or other refuse, or to do or permit to be done in connection with such receptacles anything which shall be damaging to the property of another.


===== 9.04.150 Littering =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to litter or cause to be littered any property, place, building, premises, street, road or highway with the contents of garbage or refuse receptacles.


===== 9.04.160 Private Storage Of Garbage And Refuse =====
It shall be unlawful for any person or commercial establishment to place receptacles for the storage of garbage, market waste, trade waste or other refuse in such a locality or position that they shall be filthy, unsightly, or offensive to any person inhabiting premises contiguous to such storage facility. Such conditions shall be deemed to constitute a public nuisance.


===== 9.04.170 Uncovered Garbage =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to place or permit to remain anywhere in the county any garbage or other material subject to decay other than leaves or grass, excepting in a tightly covered metal, plastic, or other approved container.


===== 9.04.180 Wind-Blown Refuse =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to cause or permit to accumulate in the county except in a covered container any dust, ashes, or trash of such material that it can be blown by the wind.


===== 9.04.190 Accumulation Of Refuse On Premises =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to permit night soil, other refuse, or dead animals to remain in or on any premises within the county.


===== 9.04.200 Removal Of Refuse =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to refuse to remove all garbage, manure, night soil, ashes, dead animals, and other refuse and offal to a place designated by the health council within a reasonable time after notice is given by the Health Department to do so.


===== 9.04.210 Dead Animals - Prompt Removal Required =====
It shall be the duty of every owner of any animal found dead within the county to immediately remove the same. It shall be unlawful for any owner of any animal found dead to permit the dead animal to be or remain upon any public street or any premises within the county.


===== 9.04.220 Dead Animals - Deposit In Water Illegal =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to throw or deposit any dead animal or fowl, or any live animal or fowl for the purpose of drowning, in any reservoir, pool, canal, creek, or other stream or body of water within the county.


===== 9.05 Septic Tank, Cesspool Cleaners =====
9.05.010 Business License 9.05.020 Permit Required 9.05.030 Rules, Regulations


===== 9.05.010 Business License =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to engage in the business of emptying or cleaning any vault, privy, septic tank, cesspool, or grease trap in this county unless that person has a license to do so.


===== 9.05.020 Permit Required =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to engage in the business of emptying or cleaning any vault, privy, septic tank, cesspool, or grease trap in the county unless that person possesses a valid permit from the County Health Department.


===== 9.05.030 Rules, Regulations =====
The County Health Department shall make such rules and regulations as are necessary to insure the proper transportation and disposal of the contents of the above receptacles.


===== 9.06 Agriculture Protection Area =====
9.06.010 Definitions 9.06.020 Review Of Agriculture Protection Areas 9.06.030 Notice, Review, Public Hearing, Evaluation, Area Adding, And Area Removing Requirements 9.06.040 Designation 9.06.050 Duties 9.06.060 Proposal For Creation Of Agriculture Protection Area 9.06.070 Area Requirements


===== 9.06.010 Definitions =====
For the purposes of this chapter, the words and phrases shall have the meanings ascribed to them in Utah Code Chapter 17-81, with the addition of:
 “contiguous” means touching along a common side or so close in proximity as to be separated only by the width of a road, canal, railroad track, easement, or similar right-of-way; and "calendar year" means the period from January 1 to December 31 inclusive.


===== 9.06.020 Review Of Agriculture Protection Areas =====
The County adopts the review of agriculture protection requirements of Utah Code Chapter 17-81.


===== 9.06.030 Notice, Review, Public Hearing, Evaluation, Area Adding, And Area Removing Requirements =====
The County adopts the noticing, review of proposal, public hearing, evaluation criteria, and procedures for adding or removing area in agriculture protection requirements of Utah Code Title 17 Chapter 41.


===== 9.06.040 Designation =====
There is hereby created an Agriculture Protection Area Advisory Board consisting of five (5) members appointed by the County Commission from the County's soil conservation district boards of supervisors. Board members shall serve without salary but may be reimbursed for mileage or other actual expenses incurred. Board member terms. Board members shall be appointed to terms of five (5) years until their successors are appointed. For the first Advisory Board, the terms of the regular members shall be staggered so that the term of one member shall expire each year on December 31st. Appointments to fill vacancies shall be for the unexpired term of the vacant office.


===== 9.06.050 Duties =====
The Agriculture Protection Area Advisory Board shall have the duties outlined in Utah Code Chapter 17-81.


===== 9.06.060 Proposal For Creation Of Agriculture Protection Area =====
A proposal to create an agriculture protection area or an amendment to an existing agriculture protection area within the unincorporated part of the County may be filed with the County Commission by completing the standard forms adopted by the County Commission and by filing said forms in the office of the County Commission. To be accepted for processing by the County Commission, a proposal under Subsection A shall be signed by a majority of the land area in agricultural production within the proposed agriculture protection area. For the purpose of Subsection A1, the owners of real property shall be determined by the records of the County Recorder. The County Clerk, or other person designated by the County Commission to receive and process proposals, shall accept and process such forms only if they are properly completed. A complete application consists of: a completed application for Agricultural Protection meeting all applicable requirements of Utah Code Chapter 17-81; the filing fee as set forth in the most recently adopted Utah County Government Fee Schedule. In addition, the applicant shall reimburse the County for all required costs of property notice mailings incurred by the County; and an electronic submittal of a complete address mailing list of property owners and applicants within one thousand (1000) feet of the proposed area.


===== 9.06.070 Area Requirements =====
In accordance with Utah Code § 17-81-201, the minimum number of continuous acres that must be included in an agriculture protection area within the unincorporated part of the County is hereby established as twenty (20) acres. A variance to the minimum acreage requirement may be given: for land which is used in intensive livestock operations or in fruit production; or if the acreage is contiguous to an existing agriculture protection area or contiguous to an existing or proposed agriculture protection area that is not within the unincorporated part of the County. Creation of an agriculture protection area shall not impair the ability of land within the area to obtain the benefits of Utah Code Chapter 59-2 Part 5, Farmland Assessment Act. The eligibility of land for the benefits of Utah Code Chapter 59-2 Part 5, Farmland Assessment Act, shall be determined exclusively by the provisions of that act, notwithstanding the land's location within an agriculture protection area. 10 RECREATION 10.01 Skiing Areas 10.02 Boating 10.03 Boat Rental License 10.04 Boating Equipment 10.05 Boating Operation Regulations


===== 10.01 Skiing Areas =====
10.01.010 Sheriff To Enforce 10.01.020 Unsafe Areas - Authorization To Restrict Entrance 10.01.030 Entrance Prohibited 10.01.040 Unsafe Acts - Reckless Skiing Prohibited 10.01.050 Skier Duty In Event Of Collision 10.01.060 Violation - Penalty


===== 10.01.010 Sheriff To Enforce =====
For the purpose of promoting greater safety to persons using ski areas in the County, the Sheriff is hereby authorized and directed to carry out or cause to be carried out the provisions of this chapter.


===== 10.01.020 Unsafe Areas - Authorization To Restrict Entrance =====
The Sheriff, Forest Service, National Park Service, Utah Department of Transportation or ski patrol for the ski resort upon which the designated area is located shall be authorized to designate closed or unsafe areas through the use of regulatory signs or other devices.


===== 10.01.030 Entrance Prohibited =====
It shall be unlawful for any person, for the purpose of skiing or other reason, to knowingly and willfully enter upon any area designated by the Sheriff, Forest Service, National Park Service, Department of Transportation, or ski patrol as closed or unsafe.


===== 10.01.040 Unsafe Acts - Reckless Skiing Prohibited =====
No person shall ski in a reckless or negligent manner so as to endanger the life, limb, or property of any person. The primary duty shall be on the person skiing downhill to avoid collision with any lower person or object.


===== 10.01.050 Skier Duty In Event Of Collision =====
Every skier involved in a collision with another skier must stop and render any reasonably necessary assistance. Such assistance may include warding off other skiers while waiting for aid or contacting resort personnel or ski patrol to inform them that aid is necessary. Any skier involved in a collision must, upon request, present identification or supply the skier's name and address to resort personnel, ski patrollers, or the injured skier.


===== 10.01.060 Violation - Penalty =====
Any person who violates any provision of this chapter shall be guilty of a class B misdemeanor.


===== 10.02 Boating =====
10.02.010 Definitions 10.02.020 Application And Jurisdiction 10.02.030 Enforcement 10.02.040 Violation And Penalty 10.02.050 Flotation Device Required On Provo River 10.02.060 Trespass Upon Boats


===== 10.02.010 Definitions =====
For the purposes of this chapter, the word "boat" shall mean any vessel (such as rafts, canoes, skiffs, dinghies, rowboats, sailing vessels, or motorboats) and shall include any vessel propelled in whole or part by machinery, including vessels temporarily equipped with detachable motors.


===== 10.02.020 Application And Jurisdiction =====
The provisions of this chapter shall be applicable to all boats navigating the waters within the jurisdiction of this County, except where inconsistent with any laws or regulations of any higher governing authority, in which case such laws or regulations shall prevail.


===== 10.02.030 Enforcement =====
The Sheriff is hereby charged with the enforcement of all of the provisions of this chapter.


===== 10.02.040 Violation And Penalty =====
Any person who shall violate any of the terms, clauses or conditions of this chapter shall be guilty of an infraction, unless a different penalty shall be provided for such violation by this chapter.


===== 10.02.050 Flotation Device Required On Provo River =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to engage in swimming activity or use inner tubes, air mattresses or other floating devices, or to allow a minor in the person's charge to engage in such activity on any portion of the Provo River that lies within the unincorporated areas of this County, unless the person is wearing a life vest or other jacket flotation device. The flotation device must be Coast Guard approved and in good and serviceable condition and of proper and adequate size to provide positive buoyancy and maintain the person using the device in an upright position.


===== 10.02.060 Trespass Upon Boats =====
It shall be unlawful for any person or persons not the owner of and without the permission of the owner of any boat to enter upon or into, to take hold of, or in any way tamper with or trespass upon, any boat or mooring line of any boat which is anchored or moored at any boat slip or pier, or anchored upon any lake or stream in the County.


===== 10.03 Boat Rental License =====
10.03.010 Required 10.03.020 Inspection 10.03.030 Inspection Markings


===== 10.03.010 Required =====
Application for a license to rent or offer to rent or lease a boat or other aquatic transportation device shall be made according to the provisions of UCC 4. No boat shall thereafter be rented or offered for rent until such license has been granted and the boat marked as hereinafter provided.


===== 10.03.020 Inspection =====
The inspectors appointed by the County Commission are hereby empowered and shall have the duty of prescribing methods of inspection to determine the weight capacity for each boat and to make sure that such boat is of a suitable structure for the service in which it is to be employed and is in a condition to warrant the belief that it may be used in navigation with safety to life. It shall be the duty of the inspectors to cause such boats to be inspected upon each application for license, and to see that all boats comply with the standards prescribed. Whenever, upon such inspection, it shall be found that any boat so inspected does not comply with such standards, it shall thereupon be the duty of the inspectors to notify the owner, proprietor or agent in charge of any such boat to comply with such standards and with the provisions of this section prior to further use of such boat.


===== 10.03.030 Inspection Markings =====
In addition to the license provided for by the provisions of this chapter, there shall be painted conspicuously on the after quarter of each boat, the number of pounds of weight authorized to be carried therein. No owner, agent or lessor, shall knowingly permit more than that number of pounds of weight to occupy the boat at any time.


===== 10.04 Boating Equipment =====
10.04.010 Life Preservers 10.04.020 Fire Extinguishers 10.04.030 Anchors, Oars And Paddles, Water Pumps, Bailing Cans 10.04.040 Fuels 10.04.050 Lights 10.04.060 Muffling Devices 10.04.070 Ventilators


===== 10.04.010 Life Preservers =====
All boats shall be provided with one U.S. Coast Guard approved life jacket, ring buoy or buoyant cushion for each person on board.


===== 10.04.020 Fire Extinguishers =====
All motorboats shall be equipped with at least one hand portable, U.S. Coast Guard approved fire extinguisher.


===== 10.04.030 Anchors, Oars And Paddles, Water Pumps, Bailing Cans =====
Anchors with an adequate scope of anchor line several times the depth of the water, oars and paddles, and water pumps or bailing cans shall be standard equipment for all boats.


===== 10.04.040 Fuels =====
All liquid fuel shall be carried only in approved safety cans; and equipment to transfer said liquid fuel to the fuel tank, cook stove, heating or lighting device, shall be provided to prevent spillage or leakage. In no instance shall fuel be carried in glass containers.


===== 10.04.050 Lights =====
Each boat, when under way between sunset and sunrise, shall display such lights as are required by state law.


===== 10.04.060 Muffling Devices =====
It shall be unlawful to use a boat propelled in whole or in part by gas, gasoline or naphtha, unless the same is provided with a stock factory muffler, underwater exhaust or other modern devices capable of adequately muffling the sound of the exhaust of the engine. The phrase "adequately muffling" shall mean that the motor's exhaust at all times be so muffled or suppressed as not to create excessive or unusual noise. The discharge of cooling water through exhaust of an inboard engine shall be considered an adequate muffling device. Nothing in the provisions of this section shall be construed to mean that the operator of a motorboat actually competing in a race or regatta which is sanctioned by an appropriate government shall not be allowed to do so.


===== 10.04.070 Ventilators =====
All cabin or semi-cabin boats shall be equipped with ventilators which can be used without exposing extra openings in the hull, which would admit spray or splash during rough weather.


===== 10.05 Boating Operation Regulations =====
10.05.010 Exception 10.05.020 Operation Generally 10.05.030 Reckless Operation 10.05.040 Speed In Harbors 10.05.050 Meeting 10.05.060 Crossing 10.05.070 Overtaking 10.05.080 Obstruction Of Passageways 10.05.090 Operation In Swimming Areas 10.05.100 Overloading 10.05.110 Mooring To Beacons 10.05.120 Intoxication 10.05.130 Accidents 10.05.140 Interference By Swimmers


===== 10.05.010 Exception =====
Nothing in the provisions of this chapter should be construed to mean that the operator of a motorboat actually competing in a race or regatta which is sanctioned by an appropriate government shall not attempt to attain high speeds on a marked racing course.


===== 10.05.020 Operation Generally =====
Every operator of a motorboat shall at all times navigate the same in a careful and prudent manner and at such rate of speed as not to endanger the life, limb or property of another person.


===== 10.05.030 Reckless Operation =====
Reckless navigation of a motorboat shall include operating the same in a manner which unnecessarily interferes with the free and proper use of the navigable waters of the state or unnecessarily endangers other boats therein, or the life and limb of any person.


===== 10.05.040 Speed In Harbors =====
It shall be unlawful for any person to operate a boat within any harbor at a rate of speed which would cause a wake from the boat he is operating. In addition to any penalty which may be imposed for the violation of this section, any person causing a wake shall be personally liable for any damage resulting from such wake.


===== 10.05.050 Meeting =====
When two (2) boats are approaching each other "head on" or nearly so (so as to involve risk of collision), it shall be the duty of each boat to bear to the right and pass the other boat on its left side.


===== 10.05.060 Crossing =====
When boats approach each other obliquely or at right angles, the boat approaching on the right side has the right-of-way.


===== 10.05.070 Overtaking =====
One boat may overtake another on either side but must grant right-of-way to the overtaken boat.


===== 10.05.080 Obstruction Of Passageways =====
No person shall so anchor a boat for fishing or other purposes on any body of water over which the County has jurisdiction in such a position as to obstruct a passageway ordinarily used by other boats.


===== 10.05.090 Operation In Swimming Areas =====
No person shall operate a boat within a water area which is clearly marked by buoys or some other distinguishing device as a swimming area.


===== 10.05.100 Overloading =====
No boat shall be loaded with passengers or cargo beyond its safe carrying capacity taking into consideration weather and other normal operating conditions.


===== 10.05.110 Mooring To Beacons =====
Any person mooring any boat to any of the buoys or beacons placed in any waterway by the authority of the state or by the authority of the County Commission, or in any manner hanging on with a boat to such buoy or beacon shall be guilty of an infraction.


===== 10.05.120 Intoxication =====
It shall be unlawful for any person who is under the influence of intoxicating liquor or narcotic drugs or barbiturate or any person who is an habitual user of same to operate, propel or be in actual physical control of any boat. It shall be unlawful for the owner of any boat or any person having such in charge or in control to authorize or knowingly permit the same to be propelled or operated by any person who is under the influence of intoxicating liquor, narcotic drugs or barbital.


===== 10.05.130 Accidents =====
The operator of any boat involved in an accident resulting in injury or death to any person or in damage to property shall immediately stop such boat at the scene of such accident and shall give his name, address, full identification of his boat and the name and address of the owner, to the person struck or the operator or occupants of the boat collided with, and shall render to any person injured in such an accident reasonable assistance and shall report such accident to the nearest or most convenient law enforcement agency or office.


===== 10.05.140 Interference By Swimmers =====
No swimmer or other person shall take hold of or in any manner interfere with any boat or watercraft or the operation of the same while any such boat is under way. ===== ATTORNEY =====
The Utah County Attorney's Office shall collect fees in the following amounts and the individual or entity requesting discovery must remit the appropriate fee prior to the Attorney's Office sending or providing discovery. Discovery Fees Data or media storage device (e.g. video tape, audio tape, DVD disc, DC disc, etc.) $15.00 Black and white photocopies (per page) $0.25 Color photocopies (per page) $0.50 Facsimile transmission (first page) $2.00 Each additional page $1.00 Mailing $2.00 per mailing
or actual cost plus $1.50, whichever is greater
 The Utah County Attorney's Office shall not collect fees for providing discovery in criminal and juvenile cases when the charged defendant or juvenile does not have counsel, the discovery consists only of documents, the number of photocopied pages is less than ten, and the defendant or juvenile picks up the discovery at the Attorney's office.

The Utah County Attorney Office shall not collect fees for providing discovery when discovery is made available via email or internet accessible database.

===== CONSTABLE =====
17-25.2 FEES FOR CONSTABLES – CIVIL .
 (1) Constables may for their own use collect as compensation in civil matters the same fees as those specified for sheriffs in Section 21-2-4.
(2) Constable fees that exceed the amounts in Section 21-2-4 are recoverable:
(a) by the constable only if he has received prior approval for the increased fee from the party requesting the service; and
(b) by prevailing party as a cost of the action only if the court finds the service and increased fee are justifiable.
 17-25-3 FEES FOR CONSTABLES – CRIMINAL .
 (1) (a) In criminal matters constables shall be paid for each copy of a summons, subpoena, notice, court order, or other criminal paper, except a warrant of arrest;
(i) $5 for each defendant served; and
(ii) mileage of $1 per mile for each mile necessarily traveled in going only, to be computed from either the courthouse, or when transmitted by mail, from the post office where received.
(b) If more than one trip is necessary to serve, or diligently attempt to serve, service of process, mileage charges for more than two trips may be collected only if the party requesting the service of process has approved the additional mileage charges.
(c) Each charge shall be individually documented on the affidavit of return of service.
(2) Lower charges may be established by contract for services under this subsection.
(3) If a constable serves process in a county other than the county where the process originated, travel expenses may not exceed the fee that would be charged if served by the sheriff of that county.
(4) (a) For each mile traveled for the purpose of serving, or to diligently attempt service of, a warrant of arrest, both in going to and returning from defendant's address, a fee of $1 may be charged.
(b) If more than one trip is necessary to serve, or diligently attempt to serve, a warrant of arrest, no more than two additional mileage charges may be collected.
(c) Each charge shall be individually documented on the affidavit of return of service.
(5) For arresting each prisoner and bringing him into court, or otherwise satisfying a warrant, a fee of $15 may be charged.
 17-25-4 CONSTABLES' FEES IN CRIMINAL CASES – PROCEDURE .
 Accounts against the county filed by constables for services in criminal cases shall be certified as correct by the county attorney or district attorney and shall be presented to the auditor. The county legislative body may reject such bills in all causes or proceedings in which the county attorney or district attorney has not in writing authorized the issuance of the warrant of arrest.
 17-25-5 CONTRACTS FOR CONSTABLE SERVICES .
 The governing body of any municipality or county where a justice court exists may contract with any one or more of the constables appointed in the county where the court sits to provide services in criminal cases for the contracting governmental entity by any method and for any amount mutually agreed upon.

===== RECOVERY ZONE FACILITY BONDS =====
Part I : The Section of the Utah County Government Fee Schedule adopted in book form by Section 2-1-6 of the Utah County Code, is hereby amended by adding a Fee Schedule relating to the issuance of Recovery Zone Facility Bonds by Utah County as follows: A. Retainer Fee Actual fees, pursuant to the schedule of fees set forth below, shall be deducted from the retainer.
 The balance of the retainer, if any, shall be returned to the Applicant upon completion of the issuance of Recovery Zone Facility Bonds, or upon termination of Applicant's application.
 $5,000.00 B. Administrative costs fee The non-refundable fee shall be deducted from the retainer fee enumerated in Section A hereof. $500.00 C. County Attorney/Deputy Attorney hourly fee as member of County Recovery Zone Facility Bond Review Committee, and for services as Issuer's counsel $200.00 D. Actual time hourly fee of other County officials (including the members of County Recovery Zone Facility Bond Review Committee) $125.00 E. County's Financial Advisor fee Rate of $2.00/$1000.00 of principal amount of bonds with $10,000.00 limit
 F. Any other actual fees or costs incurred by Utah County shall be charged to and shall be paid by the Applicant. Part II : If any of the sections, sentences, clauses or provisions of this ordinance shall for any reason be adjudged inapplicable or invalid by a court of competent jurisdictions, such shall not affect or invalidate the remaining portion contained herein.

===== SHERIFF'S OFFICE =====
Civil Process Service Fees (Revised May 2020) Utah State Statute allows for the Utah County Sheriff’s Office to receive fees for civil process services. The amounts are governed by Utah Code § 17-22-2.5. The amount charged is determined by the type of process being served plus mileage. There are deposits required for certain types of services and upfront payment is required prior to any service being attempted. However, refunds may be issued if service is not completed or the deposit is more than required once service is rendered. For more information, please contact the Judicial Support Bureau at (801) 851-4036, 4042 or email: civil@utahcounty.gov. 

 General Service Fees : $20.00 for each defendant, plus mileage 
Includes the service of: 
 Garnishment
 Garnishee Execution
 Notice (Note: Evictions require additional process service fees)
 Order (e.g., Order to Show Cause)
 Other process by which an action or proceeding is commenced
 Rule
 Subpoena
 Summons
 Summons and Complaint
 Summons and Petition
 **The Utah County Sheriff’s Office does not charge a fee for the service of protective orders, temporary protective orders, or stalking injunctions. 

 Service of Writs, Attachments and Executions : $50 for each defendant, plus mileage 
Includes the service of:
 An Attachment on Personal Property
 Executing a Civil Order of Arrest or a Civil Bench Warrant. (A non-refundable detention and processing fee of $241.75 to allow for incarceration (up to 3 days) is also required to be paid at time of service request for civil arrest orders and civil bench warrants.)
 Executing an Order for the Delivery of Personal Property
 Writ of Assistance 
 Writ of Execution 
 Writ of Possession
 Writ of Replevin 
 Writ of Restitution 
 Eviction Process :
 Includes:

Posted Notice to Vacate $20, plus 1 Mileage (See mileage costs by City)
Summons and Complaint $20, plus 3 Mileage (See mileage costs by City)
Order/Writ of Restitution $50, plus 1 Mileage (See mileage costs by City)
Total Cost for Eviction Process: $90 for Paper Service Fees plus Mileage Fees

Mileage Fees - $2.50 per mile as follows:
 The fees for 3 one-way trips to the city being served will be charged in addition to the identified civil process fee. All fees must be paid in advance. 
 Miles traveled one way for service (starting address is Provo 4th District Court)
 Miles traveled one way for service plus two additional attempts. Mileage will be charged up front for 3 total one-way attempts unless specified otherwise.
 Miles traveled to transport a Civil Prisoner from place of arrest to Jail or before a Court or Magistrate
 Miles traveled to transport a Civil Prisoner from Jail before a Court or Magistrate
 Miles traveled to transport a mental health patient to the Utah State Hospital or to or from a hospital or a mental health facility, when payable by a private individual. If assistance is required to transport the person, the Sheriff may also charge the actual and necessary cost of that assistance.
